## CONCEPT + FRAMING
Hexacopter surveys; UNO Q onboard ML detects standing water in downward stills; descend over (or beside, see TF-Luna risk) puddle; drop granular Bti (demo=inert salt); on landing UNO Q=base station (heatmap/report). Judged loop detect->descend->treat runs onboard.
Framing: model detects "standing-water candidates", stagnation confirmed by persistence across passes/operator; document honestly. AI-authorship: disclose "AI-assisted; architecture/testing/debug mine"; keep dataset+bench evidence; be able to explain every design decision in Q&A.
REGULATORY: Digital Sky parked (portal blocks self-reg); >2kg AUW=Small category; compliance gap acknowledged deliberately; salt avoids pesticide law; narrative to write (user).

## AIRCRAFT (fixed facts)
F550 hexa X (FRAME_CLASS 2 TYPE 1; SENSOR_ANGLE_OFFSET_DEG 0, corrected 2026-08-06: ring sits BETWEEN the arms, sensor 0 out the nose, NOT arm-mounted). S550 predecessor was 1.85kg, hovered 30% throttle (user: settled, do not re-litigate); F550 lighter. Payload budget ~650g; AI payload ~230-340g.
Pixhawk 2.4.8 = Pixhawk1-bdshot 4.7.0 stable (flashed 2026-07-25). Motors 6x DJI A2212 920KV, DJI-style 1045 props, handed M6 nutcaps (black CW silver CCW). 6x 45A BLHeli_32 (RPM notch impossible on this board: AUX5-6 NODMA; motors stay MAIN PWM; ESCs = quality+passthrough only). 3S 8000mAh XT60. Charger HTRC B6 V2. NEO-M8N+compass mast. FS-i6X/FS-iA10B iBUS. 433MHz SiK. OLED SH1106 (NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE 2, compass I2C, 5V UBEC; splices need soldering; glanceable top-plate mount).
Switches: ch5 arm (RC5_OPTION 153), ch8 kill (RC8_OPTION 31), ch7 3pos Stab/AltHold/Loiter (FLTMODE_CH 7), spare 2pos=RTL (CHANNEL UNCONFIRMED: ask user, then uncomment RC6_OPTION,4 in setup file + push). Simple Mode per-mode checkbox (not restored post-reset; reconfigure if wanted).
Parts: ALL arrived. Frame assembly in progress.

## POWER MAP
RESOLVED 2026-08-02 (user checked the bench): TWO buck modules exist, identical fixed-5V units differing only in output port (one USB-C, one USB-A); NEITHER is adjustable, so every "XY-3606 set to 5.00V" instruction in this file is VOID (that adjustable module does not exist; the name lives on below only as why-history). Rail assignment now: USB-C module -> ETZIN splitter charging port -> UNO Q (this is the unit that metered 5.20V loaded, acceptance PASSED); USB-A module -> MG90 servo + ESP32 (tap via cut USB-A cable or solder pads). Two-rail rationale unchanged (servo stall must not brown out the companion). VERIFY before first connect: USB-A module's LOADED output voltage by meter, and its CURRENT RATING from listing/markings (servo stall + ESP32 + mux + 7x VL53L0X share it; rating currently UNKNOWN). PIXHAWK WIRE CHECKLIST given to user 2026-08-02 (per SERIAL MAP + standard Pixhawk-1-class ports, silkscreen-VERIFY flagged): POWER=power module; TELEM1=SiK; TELEM2=ESP32 (TX2->GPIO16, RX2<-GPIO17, GND; no 5V); GPS=puck; I2C=compass + OLED -> I2C SPLITTER NEEDED if both (new part, was not in any plan; VERIFY OLED bus compat); SERIAL4/5 split cable per SERIAL MAP (pins 4/5 provisional on TODO 7); RCIN=receiver; MAIN1-6=ESCs; AUX1=MG90 signal+GND w/ red from USB-A buck; BUZZER + SWITCH (VERIFY switch not integrated in puck); CAN/SPI/ADC/SB unused.
Power module (new) -> Pixhawk (calibrate V/I vs multimeter on install; UBEC bench V readings w/o sense pins = floating artifacts). UNO Q FLIGHT FEED SUPERSEDED 2026-07-29 (splitter gate pass, see AWAITING USER (b)): UBEC 5V -> ETZIN EPL-1207CC charging port -> UNO Q USB-C as sink (supported path, camera on splitter A-port); 5V-pin and VIN feeds BOTH DROPPED from flight design. Old plan kept below for the why-history only: UBEC 5V/5A -> UNO Q 5V pin (NOT VIN, verified 2026-07-27: VIN 7-24V exists BUT board disables USB VBUS out when VIN-powered (protective Schottky design, Arduino staff: unsupported scenario) = B525 on USB-C gets no power; 5V-pin feed keeps USB host power alive; also isolates from motor-bus sag/noise. GPIO 3.3V; USB-C free for camera) + OLED. XY-3606 (set 5.00V with meter BEFORE connecting) -> MG90 servo + ESP32 (ESP32 3V3 reg feeds mux + 7x VL53L0X). SERVO = MG90 metal-gear (user 2026-08-01, replaces SG90: metal gears + better repeatability on a gate that must not strip; VERIFY on arrival = exact variant (MG90S is the common metal-gear part, MG90D exists), MASS (metal-gear ~13g vs SG90 ~9g, both approximate, WEIGH it, payload budget ~650g) and STALL CURRENT vs the XY-3606 rating (metal-gear draws more than SG90; no verified datasheet figure, MEASURE on the bench flow test TODO 3)). SERVO SIGNAL = PIXHAWK AUX OUTPUT PIN (default ch9/AUX1, CHANNEL UNCONFIRMED), **NOT** UNO Q GPIO. RE-EXAMINED 2026-08-01 (user asked for the signal to come from the Arduino instead; ANSWER = the "valid reason" clause in his own instruction applies, four load-bearing reasons, all still true): (1) Linux userspace soft-PWM is jittery (not an RTOS) so the Linux side is out on its own; (2) that leaves the UNO Q STM32, but the stock Arduino Servo lib has NO Zephyr support (verified 2026-07-31) so it needs a board-specific PWM impl written from scratch; (3) reaching that impl needs the Linux->STM32 Bridge = TODO 12 = BLOCKED and not resolvable without bench time; (4) the Pixhawk already has hardware PWM idle on AUX, powered, wired, talking MAVLink to us, motors on MAIN1-6 so AUX free, and AUX5-6 NODMA blocks only bidir-DShot not plain PWM. Net: Arduino route = 2 unwritten firmwares + 1 open hw question, Pixhawk route = 0 new code, 14 days to deadline. ALSO signal level: **"Pixhawk servo rail is 5V logic" is RETRACTED as UNVERIFIED (2026-08-06, user asked directly whether MAIN/AUX signal pins are 3.3V or 5V and I could not confirm it; it was an assistant memory claim, exactly what truth rule 9 forbids as a wiring basis).** What is actually true: the PWM outputs originate at 3.3V MCU pins (MAIN from the IO co-processor, AUX from the FMU), and whether they reach the connector at 3.3V or 5V depends on whether this board buffers/level-shifts them and what supplies those buffers, which has NOT been checked against the schematic or a scope for THIS board. CONSEQUENCE WORTH TESTING EARLY: if the outputs are buffered from VDD_SERVO and this build never powers the Pixhawk's servo rail (servo power comes from the USB-A buck, by design), the AUX signal could be weak or absent entirely; the cheap empirical test is the existing `wiring_check.py --wiggle` with the servo connected and grounds common, watching whether the gate moves. If it does not move, escalate to a scope on the pin before adding a level shifter or powering the rail. NOTE ON THE DECISION THIS CLAIM SUPPORTED: even if AUX turns out to be 3.3V, the servo-source verdict does NOT flip, because the load-bearing reasons were the two missing firmwares (Zephyr PWM impl + the blocked Bridge), not the logic level. UNO Q GPIO is 3.3V and NO consulted datasheet states an MG90 input-high threshold (user's "3.3V should not be an issue" reasoning was about POWER, which was never the objection; the objection is LOGIC LEVEL + firmware). If ever revisited: VERIFY the threshold on a scope, do not assume. Servo POWER = XY-3606 always, on both routes (a stalled servo still cannot brown out the companion). Old UNO Q GPIO note (marginal 3.3V logic, salvage cap if it misbehaved) is DEAD, do not reinstate. Common grounds. XT60 ~60A continuous (full-punch bursts may touch it; known).
VERIFY-BEFORE-WIRING (2026-07-27 sweep under CLAUDE.md truth rules 8+9; each = memory-based claim, damage-if-wrong; check datasheet/meter BEFORE connecting): (1) UBEC output: many hobby UBECs are 5V/6V jumper-selectable; 6V into UNO Q 5V pin may kill the board. Confirm jumper position + meter the output UNDER LOAD before first connect. UPDATE 2026-07-27: user metered 5.3V (no trim pot; presumed UNLOADED = floating-artifact suspect). Spec VERIFIED same docs page: 5V-pin window 4.5-5.5V max-not-exceeded -> 5.3V in-spec for the BOARD but only 0.2V ripple headroom, and same 5V_SYS bus back-drives camera VBUS (USB nominal max 5.25V, camera margin flagged not cleared). RULING: re-meter AT THE 5V PIN under real load (UNO Q + camera); <=~5.2V loaded = wire as planned; if it holds 5.3V, optional margin fix = 2nd adjustable buck (XY-3606-class) set to 5.00V by meter replacing the fixed UBEC feed. WIRING RULE (2026-07-27): UNO Q 5V feed must NOT be a dupont jumper (~1A class, I2R drop at 3A peaks -> droop -> reset; docs warn droop=resets and docs' >=3A = peak budget incl camera VBUS, not continuous draw). UBEC has NO output leads (user 2026-07-27; earlier "keep its own lead gauge" advice was an assistant form-factor assumption, retracted): user supplies ~18-20 AWG stranded, short run, soldered both ends (NOT servo-lead offcuts, 22AWG or thinner), MULTIPLE GND returns (per docs), and bench-measure actual loaded current (continuous draw unverified, expected well under 3A). Acceptance test stays: loaded voltage AT the 5V pin. UBEC output = SCREW TERMINALS (user 2026-07-27). Header-end ruling: NO bare stranded wire into female headers (poor contact geometry, stray-strand shorts to adjacent pins, vibration walk-out on THE critical rail). Preferred = Uno-R3 screw-terminal shield (UNO Q is R3 form factor; Robu.in/Amazon.in std part; VERIFY listing breaks out 5V/GND) -> both ends screwed. Fallback = wire soldered/crimped to male pin strip (2-3 pins grip better), hot-glue + zip-tie strain relief, 2x GND pins. Underside header-tail soldering only if board actually exposes tails (LOOK first, not asserted). (2) XY-3606: 5.00V setting (already ruled) AND output polarity metered before servo/ESP32 connect. (3) OLED SH1106 module: confirm the specific module's VCC range accepts 5V and its I2C lines are compatible with the Pixhawk 3.3V bus (module-dependent; check markings/listing). (4) TF-Luna: pinout per batch (already ruled), confirm 5V supply + 3.3V UART logic from the Benewake manual of THIS unit; pin5 float=UART. (5) ESP32 dev board 3V3 regulator: confirm headroom for TCA9548A + 7x VL53L0X before trusting the ring (measure current or check reg rating). (6) Power module: meter its 5V-ish FC output before first Pixhawk connect. (7) UNO Q 5V-pin feed: VERIFIED 2026-07-27 (docs: cover 5V load with 3A margin, short leads; VIN rejected because VIN power disables USB VBUS out = dead camera).

OLED DARK 2026-08-02, NARROWED (Pixhawk on USB + battery, ESP32 REAL mode): NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE read back from the board = 2.0 (confirmed present, cause 2 ELIMINATED) and COMPASS PASS on the same spliced bus (cause 5 ELIMINATED, the bus and splice work). Remaining, ranked: (1) OLED has no VCC/GND (splice was "compass wires + 2 dupont female wires" = SDA/SCL only; an SH1106 has no backlight so unpowered looks identical to misconfigured; METER its VCC pin); (2) NOT REBOOTED since the push (ArduPilot initialises the display driver at BOOT only, so a freshly-set NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE does nothing until a power cycle) - free to rule out, do it first; (3) controller mismatch SH1106(2) vs SSD1306(1), externally identical modules -> try 1, reboot, look; (4) module address 0x3C vs 0x3D (whether ArduPilot probes both is UNVERIFIED, do not assert; some modules have a solder-jumper to change it); (5) dead module. DEFINITIVE TEST if 1-4 fail: I2C scanner sketch on a spare MCU to see whether the module answers at all and at which address. UPDATE same day, causes 1-3 ALSO ELIMINATED: user metered the module VCC at 5.25V (powered), rebooted (driver init happens at boot, so that path is exercised), and tried NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE=1 with another reboot (both controller types now ruled out). User states the screen WORKED EARLIER with this exact hardware, so this is a REGRESSION, and the question becomes what changed since. Candidates, none verified: (a) FIRMWARE 4.6.3 -> 4.7.0 upgrade on 2026-07-25 plus the factory reset - if the 4.7 build for this board is feature-trimmed (1MB-class boards get --enable-minimize-features) the display driver may simply not be compiled in while NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE remains settable; I CANNOT VERIFY from memory whether 4.7's Pixhawk1 build includes it, check the boot banner/ArduPilot source before believing it; (b) the module's GND leg: a metered 5.25V on VCC proves supply presence, NOT a return path, and a broken GND on the OLED stub leaves the compass (its own GND intact) working - continuity-test OLED GND to Pixhawk GND; (c) the OLED stub's SDA/SCL solder joints specifically (compass PASS only proves the bus reaches the COMPASS, both hang off the same splice); (d) module died. **SOLVED 2026-08-02: cause (b), a LOOSE GND WIRE on the OLED stub.** User replaced the wire and set NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE back to 2 (SH1106 confirmed correct, so the temporary 1 was a red herring): screen works perfectly. Firmware feature-trim theory (a) was WRONG and is now dead, do not repeat it. LESSON worth keeping: a metered VCC proves supply only; on a shared-splice bus a device-specific GND break is invisible to every software check and to the other devices on the same bus (the compass stayed healthy throughout). Continuity-test BOTH legs per device next time a spliced I2C device is silent. NOTE: the board's NTF_DISPLAY_TYPE is back to 2 and matches pixhawk_full_setup.param, no re-push needed.
BATTERY MONITOR CALIBRATION GUIDANCE (2026-08-02, asked by user; NOTHING MEASURED YET): BATT_VOLT_MULT is trivial (meter the pack at rest, type the reading into QGC's Power setup, it computes the multiplier). BATT_AMP_PERVLT needs a KNOWN current, and the load choice is the whole question. Rules recorded: (a) METER TYPE - a handheld multimeter's 10A range is FUSED and flight currents are 20-60A, so inline is out; the safe instrument is a DC-capable CLAMP meter (Hall effect) around the battery positive lead. An AC-only clamp reads nothing on DC. (b) LOAD - do NOT use props for this; options are a resistive dummy load (car headlight bulbs, ~4.5A each at 12V 55W, parallel to reach a flight-like 15-20A) or motors WITHOUT props (only a few amps, and a calibration taken at 2A extrapolates badly to 20A, offset error dominates). Calibrate near HOVER current, not at idle. (c) Two points (low + high) beat one, because they separate slope from BATT_AMP_OFFSET. (d) GOLD STANDARD CROSS-CHECK, needs no ammeter at all: fly/hover a pack down, compare ArduPilot's logged consumed mAh against what the charger puts back in, scale BATT_AMP_PERVLT by that ratio. (e) F550 hover current is UNMEASURED in this project, so "15-20A" above is an expectation, not a fact. TODO for the user: RECORD THE POWER MODULE'S MODEL/RATING (nowhere in this file); its datasheet A/V figure is the correct starting value before any of the above refines it. (f) USER ASKED FOR "2 PINS" TO CLIP AN AMMETER TO: NO SUCH PIN PAIR EXISTS and none can be added - an ammeter must CARRY the current, i.e. sit in series in the main pack lead. The Pixhawk's 6-pin power connector carries only the FC's own ~0.5A, so measuring there answers a different question. Valid options: DC CLAMP around the pack positive (no disconnection, no extra parts, the only route that fits "use what I have"), or a two-lead meter inserted in pack positive between battery and power module - which with XT60s needs a temporary pigtail because unmating the connector breaks BOTH conductors. Hard safety notes: never put a meter in current mode across the pack (zero-ohm short on a LiPo), and check the meter's fuse rating first (a hexa passes tens of amps at modest throttle; F550 hover current still UNMEASURED here).
BENCH ACCEPTANCE TEST PLAN (2026-08-02, user-run; tools/wiring_check.py extended for it): the script now reports UP-SENSOR as its own PASS/FAIL line (was folded into the ESP32 line), --wiggle takes a bare flag defaulting to ch9, and NEW --motor-test spins each of the 6 motors in ArduPilot test order at 8% for 2s with a typed "spin" confirmation (PROPS OFF; proves ESC signal wiring + motor order + direction, all three verified BY EYE, software only commands the spin; motor 1 = front-right then clockwise, wrong order = swapped signal lead, wrong direction = swap two PHASE wires never the signal leads). Coverage note kept honest in the docstring: SiK, buzzer, safety switch, OLED and the UNO Q link are NOT provable over USB and each has its own separate check.
TWO REAL BUGS IN wiring_check.py FOUND + FIXED 2026-08-02 (both caused the "no ack" storm the user hit; source-verified in the venv's pymavlink, not guessed): (A) TARGETING. mavutil.wait_heartbeat() returns the FIRST heartbeat of any kind, and this bus has two senders (FC sys1 comp1, ESP32 sys1 comp195). pymavlink only locks its sysid onto a heartbeat that passes probably_vehicle_heartbeat(), which REJECTS the ESP32 twice over (MAV_TYPE_ONBOARD_CONTROLLER is in its excluded-type list AND MAV_AUTOPILOT_INVALID is in its excluded-autopilot list, mavutil.py:359-370). So when the ESP32's heartbeat arrived first, wait_heartbeat returned it while target_system stayed 0 = BROADCAST (target_component defaults to 0 too, mavutil.py:182/223) -> commands went to nobody in particular and acks did not come back. THE BANNER PRINTED THE EVIDENCE ALL ALONG: runs saying "heartbeat from system 0" are the broken ones, "system 1" the good ones. Fix = wait_autopilot(), which keeps reading until autopilot!=INVALID and component==AUTOPILOT1, then pins target explicitly (the filter mavlink_io.py already had). (B) ACK MATCHING: acks were matched by type only, with no drain of the 4Hz stream backlog, so an ack could be missed behind stale telemetry or confused with the ack of this script's own stream requests. Fix = send_and_ack() drains first, matches on ack.command, 5s timeout.
MOTOR TEST NEEDS LIVE RC (empirical, 2026-08-02): identical commands at the same 8% throttle did NOTHING with the transmitter off and SPUN THE MOTORS with it on, reproduced across three runs. The user's hypothesis that 8% was too low is DISPROVEN BY HIS OWN DATA (the run that spun used the same 8%). The exact firmware gate has not been read in ArduPilot source, so this is recorded as observed behaviour, not as a quoted rule. wiring_check.py now refuses to run the motor test without live RC, so a TX-off run can never masquerade as dead ESCs.
RUNNING THE TOOLS OVER THE SiK RADIO (user asked 2026-08-06; aircraft on battery, ground radio on the PC): SUPPORTED, use --conn /dev/ttyUSB0 --baud 57600 (that is the radio's PC-side rate and has NOTHING to do with the aircraft's SERIAL2_BAUD; ls /dev/ttyUSB* to confirm the device). Changes made for it: push_params.py gained an optional 2nd positional arg for baud (was hard-coded 115200, so it could never have worked over the radio); wiring_check.py halves its stream request to 2Hz on any link <=57600 (4Hz of MAV_DATA_STREAM_ALL does not fit through a SiK air link and the dropped msgs would print as spurious FAILs), and now counts RADIO_STATUS/RADIO for rssi figures. SiK DETECTION BUG FIXED SAME DAY (user: "why does SiK say untested? This was over the radio"): keying the verdict on RADIO_STATUS was WRONG, because a SiK only injects those when its MAVLink framing mode is on, so absence proves nothing. Now the verdict keys on the LINK: autopilot telemetry cannot arrive on a non-ttyACM serial port unless it crossed the radio (the Pixhawk's own USB is a ttyACM; the ESP32's USB port carries no autopilot traffic), so a non-ACM --conn that produced heartbeats IS the proof, with rssi added only when RADIO_STATUS happens to be present. Radio link CONFIRMED WORKING 2026-08-06 by that run. Bandwidth note from the same run: the ESP32's 1Hz heartbeat came through only once in 12s while the FC's came 12/12, so the air link is dropping the low-rate traffic under the ring's 10Hz load; not a fault, but a reason to keep the stream request low over radio. So the radio route is the ONLY way to test the SiK link, and USB remains the right route for param pushes (many small round trips, retries cost air time). SPEED (user 2026-08-06, the 25s wait was too slow): --seconds is now a MAXIMUM (default 12) and the listen loop BREAKS as soon as every check is satisfied, so a healthy aircraft finishes in ~2-3s and the full window is only ever spent waiting for something that never arrives. Satisfaction requires >=2 heartbeats from BOTH the FC and the ESP32 (one proves the sender exists, two prove it is still sending) plus GPS msgs, downward+upward rangefinder, ring, SYS_STATUS and live RC. Deliberately does NOT wait for a GPS FIX, which indoors never comes. Consequence today: with UP-SENSOR still silent, runs will use the whole 12s until ch6 is fixed.
UP-SENSOR (ch6) STATUS = UNTESTED SINCE IT WAS CONNECTED (corrected 2026-08-06 after the user challenged me, twice, correctly). What is actually known: every wiring_check run that reported 0 upward DISTANCE_SENSOR msgs was on 2026-08-02, and the user connected the 7th sensor "sometime between the last few tests", so those zeros may simply predate the sensor existing. NO run has been done since. TWO ASSISTANT ERRORS IN A ROW HERE, both from filling a gap instead of asking: (1) claimed the sensor must have been hot-plugged after boot, wrong because the aircraft is unplugged many times a day across a multi-day build; (2) then claimed ch6 "has failed init on every cold boot", which no measurement supports at all. The theory that begin() only probes at boot is still TRUE as code (source-read below) but it explains nothing until there is a fresh measurement. Code facts, source-read not guessed (proximity_sensors.cpp): begin() probes each mux channel ONCE at boot, prints "[sensors] chN: VL53L0X OK" or "chN: VL53L0X init FAILED (check wiring/mux)" per channel, sets _ok[ch]=false on failure, and readAll() then returns SENSOR_MM_ERROR for that channel forever; there is NO retry path. config.h UP_SENSOR_CHANNEL=6, NUM_SENSORS=7. Since ArduPilot demonstrably forwards the ESP32's broadcast OBSTACLE_DISTANCE through to USB/radio, a working ch6 would show up the same way, so 0 upward msgs = no data at the source. FRESH RUN DONE 2026-08-06 over the SiK radio, WITH the sensor connected: UP-SENSOR still 0 (everything else PASS). So the fault is now MEASURED, not inferred. User asked whether "nothing above it" explains 0: NO, and this is source-verified, not reasoned: sendDistanceSensorUp() only returns without transmitting when classifyCm() gives SECTOR_NO_DATA, which happens for exactly two inputs, SENSOR_MM_ERROR (0xFFFF = init failed or read timeout) and a literal 0 mm (VL53L0X signal-fail). An empty ceiling gives mm >= VL53L0X_MAX_VALID_MM -> SECTOR_NO_OBSTACLE -> the message IS sent as max+1 cm. Therefore 0 messages = the READ is failing at the sensor, and the ESP32 boot print ("[sensors] ch6: VL53L0X OK" vs "init FAILED") splits init-failure from persistent-0mm.
ROOT CAUSE MEASURED 2026-08-06 (ESP32 boot log, user-run): **TWO channels fail, not one** -- "[sensors] ch1: VL53L0X init FAILED" and "ch6: VL53L0X init FAILED", each preceded by `Wire.cpp requestFrom(): i2cRead returned Error 263`. 263 = 0x107 = ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT in arduino-esp32, i.e. nothing answered on that mux channel. Every live cycle then prints `s1[90]:ERR` and `UP:ERR` while ch0/2/3/4/5 report sane distances, so the ring has been flying on FIVE sensors with a hole at 90deg (starboard). ASSISTANT BLIND SPOT THIS EXPOSED, now fixed: wiring_check.py called ESP32 PASS purely because OBSTACLE_DISTANCE was arriving, but a dead ring sensor still occupies its slot filled with SECTOR_NO_DATA (65535), so the message streams happily with a hole in it. The check now scores each of the 6 sectors and prints a separate RING line naming any dead sector by bearing; had it done so on 2026-08-02 the dead ch1 would have been caught then. NEXT (user, bench): the two failures are independent channels sharing only the mux, so the top suspect is a loose leg per sensor (exactly the OLED's fault mode), then a dead sensor, then damaged mux pairs, then the POWER MAP's open VERIFY (5) 3V3 headroom for mux + 7 sensors (least likely, since five work). CLEANEST ISOLATION = swap test: move the ch6 sensor's 4-wire bundle onto a known-good channel's pads (e.g. ch2 with its sensor unplugged) and reboot; init OK there = sensor+wires fine, mux channel at fault; still FAILED = that sensor or its wires. Repeat for ch1.
ch6 CAUSE FOUND 2026-08-06 (user): the upward sensor's VIN was never connected to 3V3 (friend's wiring). Same fault family as the OLED's loose GND: a missing power leg, invisible to every software check, on a device whose neighbours all work. THIRD one this week, so the standing bench rule is now: when one device on a shared bus is silent, meter ITS OWN VIN and GND to the source before anything else. ch1 STATUS AFTER THAT FIX = UNKNOWN, not fixed and not broken: the user reports checking VIN/GND on the other sensors, which is not the same as a fresh boot log showing "ch1: VL53L0X OK". Do not record ch1 as healthy until a boot log or a RING 6/6 line says so.
"CAN I POWER THE UP SENSOR FROM THE PIXHAWK INSTEAD OF OPENING THE DRONE?" (user 2026-08-06). ANSWER = NOT VERIFIED SAFE, do not do it without checking two things first, and the default remains the ESP32 3V3/GND hub. (a) NO 3.3V SUPPLY PORT IS CONFIRMED on this Pixhawk: its external connectors (I2C, TELEM1/2, GPS, SERIAL4/5) carry 5V, and the "ADC 3.3" connector is an ANALOG INPUT, not a rail; its exact pinout is NOT verified here, so it must not be used as a supply on my say-so (truth rule 9). Check the board's official pinout before believing any 3.3V exists. (b) 5V FROM THE I2C PORT IS ONLY AN OPTION IF THE MODULE REGULATES: common VL53L0X carriers (Pololu, Adafruit, GY-530-style) accept ~2.6-5.5V VIN because they carry an onboard regulator, but bare 3.3V-only modules exist and 5V destroys them; identify THIS module before connecting. (c) EVEN IF IT REGULATES, there is a second hazard: a module fed 5V typically references its I2C pull-ups/level-shifters to its own VIN, which would put 5V on that TCA9548A channel while the other six sit at 3.3V. Whether that is safe depends on the mux's own VCC and its per-channel translation limits, which are NOT verified here. (d) GND, CORRECTED 2026-08-06 after the user pushed back and was RIGHT: I wrote that the sensor "cannot talk until its ground is bonded to the mux's", implying a floating-reference problem. There is none. Every buck here is non-isolated, so all GND traces back to battery negative and it is ONE NET; ground for this sensor may be taken wherever is convenient. What actually survives is a RETURN-PATH argument, which is weaker but real: with GND landing at the Pixhawk and SDA/SCL returning to the mux, the 400kHz I2C return current detours sensor -> Pixhawk -> power module -> battery negative -> buck -> mux, sharing conductors with ESC currents, so ground bounce under motor load is the risk (this is why the plan puts a GND rail next to the mux). The sensor does still need SOME ground wire; it currently has none, which alone explains its I2C timeout. RECOMMENDATION: run the two wires to the same 3V3/GND distribution hub the other six use (TODO 13 already specifies that perfboard hub). If the drone must be opened anyway to reach any of this, opening it once and doing it properly beats a mixed-rail workaround that risks the mux.
RING ORIENTATION CORRECTED 2026-08-06 (user, measured on the airframe): the ring is mounted BETWEEN the arms, sensor 0 pointing straight out the NOSE, so bearings are 0/60/120/180/240/300. config.h SENSOR_ANGLE_OFFSET_DEG 30.0f -> 0.0f, plus the ASCII wiring map, the module README (3 places) and this file. NOT COSMETIC: angle_offset is what tells ArduPilot where an obstacle lies, so the old 30 would have reported every obstacle 30deg clockwise of its true bearing and avoidance would have slid the aircraft along the wrong vector. The 30 was written when the mounting was still assumed to be arm-mounted (hexa X arms sit at 30/90/...), which is the mistake to notice: a plausible default outlived the assumption that produced it. REQUIRES A RE-FLASH of the ESP32 to take effect (nothing on the Pixhawk side changes; PRX1_YAW_CORR stays 0 because the message carries the offset). tools/wiring_check.py's SECTOR_BEARING table updated to match, so its "DEAD s1(60deg)" naming stays truthful.
ch6 STILL FAILS AFTER POWERING IT (2026-08-06, two consecutive boot logs): ch1 AND ch6 both "init FAILED" with `Wire.cpp requestFrom(): i2cRead returned Error 263` (0x107 = ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT), ch0/2/3/4/5 OK. So connecting 3V3 was necessary but not sufficient, and the two failures are still unexplained. NEW SUSPECT RAISED BY THE CODE ITSELF: config.h ships I2C_CLOCK_HZ 400000 with the comment "drop to 100000 if bus is flaky" and TCA_SETTLE_US 0 with "raise to ~50 only if you see bus errors" -- we ARE seeing bus errors, and long hand-made 4-wire ring bundles are exactly what makes 400kHz marginal. That is now a first-class hypothesis alongside broken wiring, and it is cheap to test.
DEBUG TOOL WRITTEN 2026-08-06 (user asked for ESP-side debug code): esp32_obstacle_avoidance/i2c_diag.{h,cpp} + `#define RUN_I2C_DIAG` in config.h (0 = fly, 1 = bench report). When 1, setup() runs the report and returns BEFORE the Pixhawk link is opened, so a diagnostic build physically cannot transmit; loop() idles so the output stays readable. What it prints per bus config: mux ACK + control-register READBACK (proves the mux actually switches, else every "sensor fault" is one mux fault), a full 0x08-0x77 device scan per channel (a VL53L0X answers at 0x29), an idle SDA/SCL level check on any channel that scans empty (LOW = short or a device clamping the bus, which the mux confines to that channel and which no retry can fix), then a real driver init plus one range reading. It sweeps four configs, 400kHz/0us (today's settings), 400kHz/50us, 100kHz/50us, 50kHz/200us, and ends in a channel-by-config matrix. Reading rule recorded in the tool's own output: fail everywhere with nothing at 0x29 = power/wire/dead sensor; fail everywhere with a LOW line = short; OK only at slower speeds = signal integrity, so change I2C_CLOCK_HZ/TCA_SETTLE_US and shorten leads; 0x29 present but init fails = marginal comms or XSHUT held low; ALL channels fail = the mux or its supply. DIAG RUN 2026-08-06 (user, two boots) SPLITS THE FAULT IN TWO, and neither is what the sweep was designed to catch: (1) **ch6 = absent from the bus**, "nothing answers" at 0x29 in all four configs of both boots, with idle SDA/SCL HIGH (so no short) and the mux select+readback correct. That is not timing and not the mux: the sensor is not electrically present. Causes left: 3V3/GND not actually reaching THAT sensor's pins (the wire was connected at the hub end, which is not the same test), a broken SDA/SCL leg, XSHUT held low (a VL53L0X in hardware standby does not ACK at all, and would look exactly like this while metering fine for power), or a dead module. (2) **ch1 and ch3 = INTERMITTENT, not speed-dependent**: across the two boots ch1 read OK/OK/OK/fail then OK/fail/OK/OK, and ch3 OK/fail/fail/fail then fail/fail/fail/fail, i.e. the same channel passes and fails at the SAME bus speed on different runs, and slowing the bus does not help. That kills the signal-integrity hypothesis (which would fail fast and pass slow, monotonically) and points at a loose contact or a sagging supply. Supporting evidence: the user reports ch3 "randomly started failing" and confirmed it in normal (RUN_I2C_DIAG 0) operation too, and the count of bad channels has grown over the build (ch1 -> ch1+ch6 -> ch1+ch3+ch6) as more sensors were added to the same 3V3 rail. THE POWER MAP'S OPEN VERIFY (5) IS NOW THE PRIME SUSPECT: nobody has ever measured whether the ESP32 dev board's 3V3 regulator carries the mux + 7 VL53L0X, nor what the hand-made hub wiring drops under load. MEASURE 3V3 AT A FAILING SENSOR'S OWN PINS WHILE IT RUNS, not at the hub. Sensor readings themselves are healthy where they work (8190/8191 = "no target", ch5 gave 457mm), so the working channels are genuinely fine. DIAG EXTENDED same day: a REPEAT TEST now runs 20 tries per channel at the configured settings and prints answers/init/read counts, because a single sample per config cannot describe an intermittent fault; 0/20 answers = not on the bus, 20/20 answers with init below 20/20 = intermittent contact or supply. DIAGNOSIS CHANGED 2026-08-06 (third diag run + repeat test): the fault is SYSTEMIC BUS INSTABILITY, not per-sensor wiring. Evidence: (a) WHICH channels fail now MOVES between runs and even between passes of the SAME boot -- ch6 (the one we spent a day on) initialised fine and returned 8191mm this run, while ch1/ch2/ch3 failed in varying combinations; earlier boots had ch1+ch6 failing consistently; (b) the repeat test's signatures are diagnostic in themselves: ch3 answered 20/20 but initialised 0/20, i.e. the ONE-BYTE address probe succeeds every single time while the LONG multi-register init sequence fails every single time, which is what marginal signal integrity looks like and NOT what a loose wire looks like (a loose wire would drop the probe too); ch1 answered only 8/20; ch2 failed 1/20; (c) slowing to 50kHz with a 200us settle does not reliably fix any of it, so it is not simple clock speed. USER'S METER READING DOES NOT CLEAR THE SUPPLY: "every sensor's VIN and GND read a stable 3.3V" was taken with a DMM, which averages and cannot see a 1ms sag or a noise burst; only a scope can, so the rail is still a live suspect (POWER MAP VERIFY (5) remains open). LIKELY CAUSES, in order: aggregate bus loading and crosstalk on seven long hand-made dupont runs (rise-time degradation, SDA/SCL coupling with no adjacent ground return), missing decoupling (the POWER MAP's "optional 10-100uF across rails" is no longer optional, and each sensor wants a 100nF close to its own VIN/GND), then transient supply sag, then a genuinely marginal individual module. DECISIVE NEXT TEST (cheap, do this before buying or resoldering anything): BISECT BY LOAD. Disconnect all but two sensors, run the diag, and add one back at a time. If two are rock solid and reliability degrades as sensors are added, it is aggregate loading/supply and the fix is decoupling plus shorter/paired wiring; if a specific channel misbehaves regardless of how few are connected, that one module or its leads are at fault. RING REDUCED TO 3 SENSORS 2026-08-06 (user removed the three REAR-facing ones, ch2/ch3/ch4 = bearings 120/180/240; fitted now = ch0 at 0deg, ch1 at 60deg, ch5 at 300deg, plus ch6 up). This doubles as the bisect-by-load test. Firmware updated: config.h gains RING_SENSOR_FITTED[] + UP_SENSOR_FITTED, proximity_sensors.begin() skips unfitted channels entirely (each absent channel otherwise burns ~1.2s of boot on an I2C timeout AND logs a "FAILED" that camouflages the real failures), the banner lists the fitted bearings and says the rest are unknown, and the live line prints "----" for unfitted vs "ERR" for broken. WIRE BEHAVIOUR DELIBERATELY UNCHANGED and this is the safety-critical part: an unfitted channel yields SENSOR_MM_ERROR -> classifyCm -> SECTOR_NO_DATA -> transmitted as 65535 = UNKNOWN. ArduPilot treats unknown as NO INFORMATION, never as clear, so avoidance simply has no opinion about the rear. NEVER "fix" the gap by sending max range: that would assert the rear is proven clear and is exactly how an avoidance system flies backwards into something. Consequence to remember at flight time: with the rear unmonitored, rearward motion has no sensor protection at all and the pilot is the only backstop. **RING PARKED 2026-08-06 (user's call, correct one).** Final state of the evidence: with only 3 ring sensors + up fitted, ch1 and ch6 still failed init, and the two that DID init report CLEAR even with a finger directly in front of them, with occasional random values. A finger at <5cm must clamp to RANGE_MIN_CM and print 5cm; printing CLEAR means the sensor is returning >=VL53L0X_MAX_VALID_MM, i.e. it is not really ranging even though init succeeded. So reducing the load did NOT stabilise anything, which kills the aggregate-loading hypothesis too, and the failure has now progressed from "some channels" to "the surviving channels do not measure". Something systemic degraded across this build (candidates never separated: the mux itself, the shared 3V3 rail under transient load, cumulative handling damage to modules, or a marginal ESP32 supply). NOT SOLVED, and deliberately abandoned rather than solved: the ring is DESCOPE-LADDER rung (c), the drop mission does not depend on it, and it has consumed several days against the 2026-08-15 deadline. ESP32 to be unplugged; the Pixhawk keeps PRX1_TYPE=2 which makes the ESP32 an ARMING DEPENDENCY, so **PRX1_TYPE must be set to 0 (+reboot) before arming without the ESP32** (this gotcha is already recorded under PIXHAWK CONFIG and is now live).
CHANNEL MAP NEVER VERIFIED (assistant admission, user asked directly 2026-08-06): the assumed map is ch0=0deg nose, ch1=60, ch2=120, ch3=180, ch4=240, ch5=300, ch6=up, which comes from config.h's SECTOR_FOR_CHANNEL identity map and NOT from any measurement. Nobody has ever waved a hand in front of one physical sensor and checked which sX moved. If the ring is ever revived, that 2-minute test comes FIRST, because every bearing in the OBSTACLE_DISTANCE message depends on it and a scrambled map would make avoidance push the aircraft the wrong way.
PROJECT-LEVEL NOTE: the obstacle ring is a PoC and sits at rung (c) of the DESCOPE LADDER. It has now consumed several days. If the bisect does not resolve it quickly, descoping the ring is the correct call against the 2026-08-15 deadline; the drop mission does not depend on it. **THE CODE IS UNCOMPILED** (no Arduino toolchain on the laptop's PATH; PlatformIO lives in the user's VSCode extension), so the first PlatformIO build is also its first syntax check.
OLD CANDIDATE LIST (kept, still the search space): sensor physically on a mux channel other than 6; a bad leg in its 4-wire bundle (VIN/GND/SDA/SCL); XSHUT held low; dead sensor; damaged ch6 pair on the TCA9548A; or the POWER MAP's open VERIFY (5), the ESP32 3V3 regulator not having headroom for mux + 7 sensors (ring works, so this is the least likely).
BATTERY MONITOR CALIBRATION (started 2026-08-02, user in QGC Power setup): the REPLACEMENT power module's MODEL IS UNRECORDED anywhere in the repo (Build Log only says the original failed in the crash and was replaced), so no preset can be recommended on evidence -> guidance given = choose "Other" unless the module's own label matches a listed preset ("Power Module 90A" is the standard Pixhawk-kit part and a reasonable STARTING point if it is that module), then calibrate voltage from a multimeter reading typed into QGC (it computes BATT_VOLT_MULT). WHY THIS MATTERS: BATT_LOW_VOLT 10.8 / BATT_CRT_VOLT 10.2 are already on the board and drive RTL/Land, so a wrong multiplier makes the failsafe fire early or never. Current (BATT_AMP_PERVLT) needs a known load or clamp meter and is deferred: our failsafes are voltage-based, so uncalibrated current costs mAh-consumed accuracy only. ANALOG PIN PRESET (2026-08-02): QGC's second dropdown sets BATT_VOLT_PIN/BATT_CURR_PIN from a board list; correct entry for this 2.4.8 (Pixhawk1-class, module on the main POWER port) = "Pixhawk/Pixracer/Navio2/Pixhawk2_PM1", which QGC already had selected. NOT Pixhawk2_PM2/CubeOrange/Durandal (different boards) and NOT PX4-v1 (older PX4FMU v1). Acceptance: a charged 3S should read ~11-12.6V; 0V or nonsense means the preset did not take. TODO-small: record the module's model + calibrated VOLT_MULT here once known.
SERIAL4/5 split cable (one 6pin DF13): pin1 5V->TF-Luna only (UNO Q powered by UBEC, never this pin); pin2 TX4->TFLuna RXD; pin3 RX4<-TFLuna TXD; pin4 TX5->UNOQ D0(RX); pin5 RX5<-UNOQ D1(TX); pin6 GND->both. TF-Luna pin5 float=UART (verify pinout per batch); its UART is 3.3V logic.
UNO Q D0/D1 = STM32 USART1 (PB7/PB6, 3.3V). Linux<->STM32 via Bridge RPC; plan = STM32 byte-shovel sketch, pymavlink unchanged on Linux; measure latency in SITL.

## PIXHAWK CONFIG (done state)
All 55 chosen params PUSHED+VERIFIED on board (tools/push_params.py, pymavlink per-write ack). Board exposes 1054 params; full backup pixhawk_every_param.param (regen: tools/pull_params.py, QGC closed). ALL param files live in param_dumps/ (user-created 2026-07-25); tools updated to that path; gitignore: param_dumps/* except pixhawk_full_setup.param (tracked = hand-maintained config; dumps regenerable).
TOOLING RULES: NEVER trust QGC bulk load (silently drops writes; also only reads its own tab format). Writes = push_params.py. Dumps = pull_params.py or QGC save. make_complete_params.py builds QGC-format complete file from a QGC dump (float32-aware).
4.7 renames encountered: ARMING_CHECK->ARMING_SKIPCHK (0=all checks); RTL_ALT->RTL_ALT_M (m); RTL_ALT_FINAL->RTL_ALT_FINAL_M; RNGFND1_GNDCLEAR->RNGFND1_GNDCLR (m, set 0.13 placeholder, MEASURE on airframe).
Notch: first hovers throttle mode (loaded: INS_HNTCH_REF from learned MOT_THST_HOVER, FREQ/BW from log FFT, INS_LOG_BAT_MASK=1 active); then adopt FFT mode (FFT_ENABLE 1, FFT_MINHZ/MAXHZ ~50/200, INS_HNTCH_MODE 4, REF 1; commented block in setup file; HAL_GYROFFT_ENABLED confirmed in build). "Chan 13 to 14 PWM failed no DMA" boot msg = expected, harmless.
Failsafes on board: FS_THR_ENABLE 1 (RTL), BATT low RTL / crit Land, ARMING_SKIPCHK 0. FENCE: deferred by user (commented block ready).
PRX gotcha: PRX1_TYPE=2 makes ESP32 an ARMING DEPENDENCY. Field recovery: PRX1_TYPE=0 + reboot.

## ESP32 OBSTACLE MODULE (esp32_obstacle_avoidance/)
7x VL53L0X via TCA9548A 0x70: ch0-5 ring 60deg (OBSTACLE_DISTANCE angle_offset 0 as of 2026-08-06, was 30), ch6 up (DISTANCE_SENSOR orient 24). 10Hz + heartbeat, sysid1 compid195. GPIO21/22 I2C; GPIO17->PixRX, GPIO16<-PixTX. Compiles clean both modes (MAVLink@2.0.31 okalachev, VL53L0X@1.3.1 pololu; pin versions). NEVER FLASHED yet.
Fake-mode flight guard: USE_FAKE_SENSORS=1 transmits only with GPIO4 jumpered to GND; else TX 0 B + warning. Preflight: banner must say REAL.
Array = reinstated PoC (never call rejected; don't dwell sunlight). Mount check: 25deg FOV must not see legs/props.

## DROPPER: VARIABLE DOSE + REVERSED TRAVEL (2026-08-10, user-directed)
GATE TRAVEL REVERSED AND SHORTENED. Bench observation 2026-08-10: 1000us -> 1900us (900us) swung the horn 90 deg CLOCKWISE, implying ~10us per degree ON THIS SERVO (a calibration ratio, NOT a datasheet figure; MG90 travel per us varies by unit). User wants 60 deg COUNTER-CLOCKWISE instead, so open must sit BELOW closed in pulse width: defaults are now closed_us=1600, open_us=1000 (600us = ~60 deg CCW), both inside the 800-2200 guard. US_PER_DEG=10.0 is exposed as a class constant so the throw is re-derived rather than re-guessed. VERIFY BY EYE on the bench: if the throw is not 60 deg adjust US_PER_DEG; if it turns the wrong way swap the two values. The old 1000/1900 defaults are gone, so anything that relied on them must be re-pushed/re-read.
VARIABLE DOSE IMPLEMENTED. The gate is a FIXED APERTURE, so the only quantity available to vary is TIME OPEN. dropper.trigger(dwell_s=None) now takes a per-drop override on all three dropper classes; mission.dose_for(area) maps estimated puddle area to seconds via MissionConfig dose_s_per_m2=0.4 clamped to [0.3, 3.0], with dose_s_default=1.0 when the area is UNKNOWN (deliberately the default and not the maximum: over-dosing an unmeasurable puddle empties the hopper on one site and strands later ones). Verified at the edges: None->1.00s, 0.1m2->0.30s clamped, 2.5m2->1.00s, 12m2->3.00s clamped.
AREA ESTIMATE ADDED (camera_geom.ground_area_m2), which closes one of the six area-dosing conflicts and leaves the rest open. It projects the box's two CORNERS to the ground and multiplies the sides, rather than scaling pixel area by one factor, because ground_offset is linear in TANGENT space and a box off-centre covers more ground per pixel. THREE ERRORS IT CARRIES, all documented in the docstring and repeated wherever it is shown: a bounding box overestimates non-rectangular water without bound (honest area needs segmentation, a different training run); FOV error enters SQUARED (10% FOV = ~20% area); and height must be true AGL, which above the Luna's range is the EKF above-home figure. **DOSE IS PROPORTIONAL, NOT CALIBRATED IN GRAMS: the granule flow rate (TODO 3 bench test) has still never been measured, so seconds cannot yet be converted to a dose.** Detection gained area_m2 (None when geometry or height is missing), missionlog.drop gained dwell_s and area_m2 (absent in pre-2026-08-10 logs, which the dashboard treats as "not recorded" rather than zero).
DASHBOARD shows it: drop marker RADIUS encodes dose (8px at the 0.3s minimum to 14px at the 3s maximum, old dwell-less drops keep the fixed 8px so they are not rendered as minimum-dose), the table detail line carries dose and area, and the tooltip states the dose plus the bounding-box caveat so nobody reads the area as measured.
**.venv-tools IS COMMITTED TO GIT (1215 files, 74 MB) and needs a decision, NOT a unilateral fix.** It arrived in Raghav's 069634e alongside his four corrections, so it is on both machines via git. A virtualenv must never be tracked: it bloats the repo (.git is already 28 MB), it is machine-specific, and a sed over the repo edits 30 of its files by accident, which is exactly what happened here and was reverted. REMOVED FROM GIT 2026-08-10 on the user's instruction: untracked with `git rm -r --cached` and added to .gitignore. **RAGHAV MUST BE TOLD**: his next pull DELETES .venv-tools from his Mac, so he recreates it as .venv (`python3 -m venv .venv && ./pip install pymavlink pyserial`). When it goes: untrack, add to .gitignore, and he recreates a local .venv (the same one-liner the Mac setup already needs). History will still carry the 74 MB, and that is acceptable because rewriting pushed history is forbidden by this project's own rules.
SINGLE VENV AT .venv (2026-08-10, user: "all python venv stuff in .venv only"). training/.venv was MOVED to .venv (5.5G, same filesystem so instant); the Mac renames .venv-tools to .venv. Every repo path reference updated to ./python and ./pip.
  TWO THINGS THE MOVE BROKE, both fixed, both worth knowing before anyone moves a venv again: (1) **a repo-root SYMLINK cannot activate a venv.** CPython looks for pyvenv.cfg beside the executable AS INVOKED, and there is none beside <repo>/python, so it silently fell back to sys.prefix=/usr and imported nothing from the venv (`import torch` simply said "no module"). ./python and ./pip are therefore one-line WRAPPER scripts that exec the real interpreter, not symlinks. My first "verification" only printed sys.executable and never imported a venv-only package, which is why the breakage shipped. (2) moving a venv leaves EVERY console script's shebang pointing at the OLD absolute path: 52 scripts in .venv/bin (pip, yolo, ultralytics, torchrun, all the mav* tools) were dead until rewritten. `python3 -m venv .venv` over the existing directory refreshes pyvenv.cfg and the activate scripts but does NOT rewrite those shebangs.
  VERIFIED AFTER THE MOVE: torch 2.13.0+cu130 with cuda:True on the RTX 3050, ultralytics 8.4.107, pymavlink 2.4.49, pyserial 3.5, cv2 5.0.0, `pip check` clean, the yolo entry point runs, and all training/tools/uno_q modules compile.

## FIRST FLIGHTS ANALYSED 2026-08-08 (logs 34-38 from the SD card; assistant-run analysis, pymavlink)
**THE LOITER CLIMB WAS NOT AN EKF PHANTOM CLIMB. It was RTL, triggered by BATTERY FAILSAFE.** Log 37 at t=1508.5s: MSG "Battery Failsafe", MODE -> RTL with Rsn=4, then a climb 4.3m -> 12m in 6s. Evidence it was commanded, not corrupted: CTUN.CRt tracked CTUN.DCRt within 0.1 m/s the whole way (+2.2 vs +2.2), DAlt rose with Alt, and EKF Alt vs baro BAlt agreed within 0.5m throughout. A vibration-driven phantom climb looks the opposite: EKF alt diverging from baro with climb rate NOT matching demand. Pilot took Stabilize at 1514.5s and landed. RTL_ALT_M is 15, which is where it was heading. So "it goes up like it usually does" = the aircraft doing exactly what it was configured to do on a low battery.
**VIBRATION PASSES ON THIS FLIGHT**: log 37 IMU0 VibeZ median 7.5, max 37.2, clip delta +16 over the flight. Gate is <15, so THE historic blocker is clear on this airframe/flight. Only IMU0 logs VIBE on this board (no IMU1/2 instances present), so the audit's IMU-pooling bug does not bite here, though the Clip0/1/2 bug did hide the +16 clipping.
**THE REAL PROBLEM IS POWER.** Hover current ~105 A sustained, pack sagging 11.66V -> 10.72V under load, RemPct 35% -> 12% in about 70 seconds. BATT_LOW_VOLT is 10.8, so the failsafe fired on sag. UNRESOLVED AND LOAD-BEARING: is that 105 A real? Learned hover throttle is only ThH 0.383, which does NOT look like an aircraft straining, so a mis-scaled BATT_AMP_PERVLT is a strong suspect (user calibrated the battery monitor on 2026-08-08 but it is not recorded whether the CURRENT half was done against a meter). If the current IS real the aircraft is drawing ~1.1 kW in hover and endurance is a couple of minutes; if it is not, the failsafe threshold is being hit on genuine voltage sag anyway. Either way mission flights are blocked until this is understood. Cross-check: 0.9V sag over ~70A implies ~13 milliohm pack resistance, which is plausible for 3S 8000, so the sag itself looks real.
**2026-08-10 THEORETICAL CURRENT, AND WHY 105 A CANNOT BE TRUE.** User supplied the missing models: power module = APM/3DR-style "Power Module 90A, 3A BEC, XT60, 28V" (robu.in); ESCs = 6x EMAX Formula 45A BLHeli_32; props 1045; motors DJI A2212 per Build Log (KV NOT stated anywhere, still unknown); AUW estimated 2.5 kg (all-up weight = total flying weight, unmeasured, TODO weigh it). Momentum theory, 6x10in props = 0.304 m2 disc, T=24.5 N, ideal induced power 141 W; divided by figure of merit 0.5-0.7 and motor+ESC efficiency 0.75-0.85 gives **22-34 A at 10.9 V, i.e. 4-6 A per motor**. Those two efficiency constants are TYPICAL VALUES, NOT MEASURED for this build, so treat the range as an order-of-magnitude check, not a precise prediction. Inverting the logged figure: 105 A = 1144 W electrical, which on this disc area would hover ~6.2 kg. **THE DECISIVE ARGUMENT NEEDS NO WEIGHT AT ALL**: ArduPilot computes amps as (pin_volts - BATT_AMP_OFFSET) * BATT_AMP_PERVLT, the Pixhawk analog input saturates at 3.3 V, and the board has BATT_AMP_PERVLT=17 / BATT_AMP_OFFSET=0, so the LARGEST current it can report is 3.3*17 = 56 A. The log holds 105 A sustained and a 167 A peak. Both are ARITHMETICALLY UNREACHABLE at that setting, so either PERVLT differed during log 37 or the sensing path is not what is assumed; either way the amps in log 37 are not physical and consumed mAh / burn rate / the 1990 mAh-min endurance figure are ALL UNUSABLE. check_log.py now captures BATT_AMP_PERVLT/OFFSET/VOLT_MULT/CURR_PIN/CAPACITY/MONITOR from the log's own PARM records (board state is not evidence about an old flight) and prints this saturation ceiling, flagging any peak above it as IMPOSSIBLE. RESOLVED SAME DAY by that very check: **log 37 flew with BATT_AMP_PERVLT=90.6866** (not the 17 now on the board), which allows up to 299 A before saturation, so 105/167 A were readable after all and the "arithmetically impossible" claim above was wrong - it was computed from the BOARD's present setting instead of the log's, exactly the mistake the new PARM capture exists to prevent. 90.6866 is almost certainly the module's "90 A" RATING typed into QGC's amps-per-volt field, a classic and easy slip. IF the true constant is the documented 17.0 for this APM/3DR-style module (NOT VERIFIED for a Robu clone), everything rescales by 5.33x: hover 20 A, peak 31 A, consumed 1317 mAh, burn 373 mAh/min, **~21 min hover on a full 8000 mAh pack, ~17 min to a 20% reserve**. That 20 A sits inside the 22-34 A the momentum-theory estimate predicted independently, which is real corroboration, not circular. SAFE TO FLY WITHOUT FIXING IT: BATT_LOW_MAH and BATT_CRT_MAH are both 0 and BATT_FS_VOLTSRC=0, so both failsafes are VOLTAGE-driven and PERVLT cannot affect them; it only scales reported mAh. Deliberately NOT changing PERVLT before the 2026-08-10 evening flight - the charger cross-check recovers the true constant by ratio whatever it is set to, and a param push 10 minutes before takeoff is pure added risk. BATT_VOLT_MULT=10.8025 as flown; voltage is the half that drives the failsafes, so it is the half worth metering.
**2026-08-10 EVENING FLIGHT GOES WITH NO COMPUTER AT ALL** (user: neither the gaming laptop nor Raghav's macbook is coming). Consequences, all load-bearing: (a) wiring_check.py and bench.py CANNOT RUN, so RC/GPS/compass/TF-Luna go unverified at the field - acceptable only because all of them PASSED on the bench earlier today; (b) no ground station means NO audible/visible low-battery callout; (c) **THE OLED WILL NOT COVER FOR THIS.** Build Log lists it under Parts Chosen as a Pixhawk status display, but NOTHING IN THIS REPO DRIVES IT - grep for OLED/SSD1306/SH1106 across all firmware and python hits only a comment in wiring_check.py saying "look at it", and the ESP32 that would have driven it is unplugged and parked. Treat the OLED as an unbuilt feature, not a fallback. So the ONLY field readiness indicators are the Pixhawk's own LED and buzzer: do not arm until the LED is flashing GREEN (flashing blue = no GPS lock). LED meanings are ArduPilot standard but UNVERIFIED against the docs this session - confirm before relying on them. (d) **NEW ENDURANCE MEASUREMENT DEFINITION**: BATT_FS_LOW_ACT=2, so at BATT_LOW_VOLT 10.8 the aircraft SWITCHES ITSELF TO RTL and climbs to RTL_ALT_M 15. With no telemetry that autonomous mode change is the only low-battery signal there is, which makes it the clean stopwatch endpoint: time from takeoff to the aircraft going to RTL on its own. Pilot then takes STABILIZE and lands. This is a better-defined measurement than "when the warning sounds" and needs no instrument.
**2026-08-10 PROP DECISION (user asked carbon-nylon vs ABS):** recommended CARBON/GLASS-REINFORCED NYLON 1045 over ABS, primary reason CONSISTENCY not performance - ABS warps in heat and sun (August, India) and loses balance with age, and an endurance figure plus a vibration baseline are only comparable between flights if the props do not change shape. Secondary: stiffer blades flex less under load and hold balance longer, on an airframe whose historic blocker was vibration (VibeZ 46 -> 20.6 -> 7.5, clipping still +16). HONEST COUNTERPOINT recorded so it is not lost: cheap ABS props sacrifice themselves in a crash and protect the motor shaft/bell, which matters on an airframe with 3 crashes; reinforced props transmit that shock instead and are far more dangerous to people. Buy a MATCHED set of 6 plus spares so any field replacement is identical in weight. **DO NOT FIT THEM BEFORE THE ENDURANCE FLIGHT** - new props change the number being measured and need balancing first. No measured data for this airframe backs the efficiency claim; it is general propeller reasoning, not a test result. RETRACTED same day, said one message earlier and wrong: "105 A would destroy any normal 30 A ESC" - these are 45 A ESCs, 6x45=270 A, so 17.5 A per motor is comfortably inside rating and proves nothing. ALSO SOFTENED: the ~13 milliohm figure above divides a sag that includes genuine DEPLETION over 212 s by a current that is itself suspect, so it is not independent evidence. The charger cross-check (fly one pack from full, compare mAh returned) remains the only decisive test and needs no instrument.
USER CORRECTION 2026-08-08: only log 37 is a PROPER FLIGHT. Everything before it ran with a DAMAGED PROP that went unnoticed for a while, and logs 35/36/38 never left the ground (0 throttled samples). That diagnoses log 34 rather than leaving it a mystery: a bad prop is exactly what produces VibeZ 46 with 5927 clip events, and ArduPilot's own "Potential Thrust Loss (3)" NAMES MOTOR 3, which is worth cross-checking against whichever prop was actually replaced. It also explains the crash-check disarms. Judge log 37 only; treat pre-37 numbers as the bad-prop era, not as airframe characteristics.
**LOG 34 IS THE BAD ONE, KEEP IT** (it is also the docs/07 "failing vibration_log" deliverable): IMU0 VibeZ median 46.0, max 74.4, clip delta +5927, plus MSG "Potential Thrust Loss (3)", "PreArm: AHRS: DCM Roll/Pitch inconsistent 38 deg", "Compasses inconsistent", "EKF3 IMU0 forced reset". Near-zero acceleration with large attitude error is the signature of the airframe being handled/tipped while armed on the ground rather than an in-air crash, but it is UNCONFIRMED and worth asking the pilot what was happening at those moments.

## CRASH LESSONS (permanent)
C1 prop nut backed off -> vibration -> alt corrupt -> prop departed (fix: handedness+Loctite). C2 AltHold wind drift into tree. C3 armed seconds post-power-on HDOP 65-99, vibration broke EKF, phantom-fall climb to 47m, pilot disarmed DELIBERATELY (drift to unrecoverable area); procedural, GPS healthy.
Rules: abort=flip Stabilize NOT disarm (disarm only imminent person-strike/unreachable flyaway). GPS modes: 10+ sats, HDOP<1.5, no EKF complaints, wait 2-5min. VibeZ median<15 hover gate before AltHold/Loiter. Insulate bare connectors; smoke stopper on repaired wiring first power-up.
VIBRATION = THE unsolved blocker (was median 20.6, peaks 60+; gate <15). Rebuild countermeasures: good props only, rigid motors, service loops, FC foam near CG, baro covered, GPS cable tied, notch, compass recal.

## ML / TRAINING
Pipeline (training/): merge_datasets.py (single class "puddle"; single-class sets kept whole; multi-class filtered to puddle/water-named; dropped boxes -> negatives; run with .venv python) -> train.py (yolo26n 640 batch8, ckpt/epoch, Ctrl+C pause, auto-resume last.pt; run1 v8n weights kept at runs/puddle_v1/weights/best.pt as fallback) -> export.py (ONNX for UNO Q onnxruntime).
Dataset v1 = 11725 train / 3069 val from: mosquito v1 (5069, puddle class only, CC BY 4.0, CITE BibTeX from its Universe page), puddle_Detect 4.93k, hanyang puddle-detection 1.5k + puddle 1.5k, water 1k, yinjia-part2. mosquito v4 EXCLUDED (no puddle class = poison; in training/excluded/). DATASET V2 QUEUE (researched 2026-07-25, all CC BY 4.0): PRIMARY = user nadir phone photos + drone stills + close-range frame-filling water (targets known failures); PLUS (user downloading 2026-07-25 night) thesis mosquito-breeding-grounds-2 ONLY (universe.roboflow.com/thesis-kjmym/mosquito-breeding-grounds-2, 3.47k, 7 classes incl Temporary Water Sites; CORRECTION: v3/v4/v5 spin-offs are container-only, NO water class = poison, do NOT use; never multiple versions of one pool = train/val leakage) + fumigation habitats (1.06k) + habitats2 (1.29k) (puddle + probable-stagnant-water-waist classes, ground-robot view). SKIP (researched): 82myj (112 tiny), Eds breeding-detection (556 container-heavy), Fumigation vol3/4/5 (no puddle class), all insect close-up sets, aedes/MBG unresearched-but-superseded. Verdict: NO retrain until v2 assembled (retrain ~7h GPU; these sets do not fix sheet-water/glare failures). merge KEEP_NAMES already extended for v2 class names; "pond" deliberately NOT kept (permanent water, not a drop target).
RUN1 (2026-07-25, ~e160/200): P 0.79 R 0.72 mAP50 0.789 mAP50-95 0.474, plateaued (v1 val set, NOT comparable to run2 numbers).
RUN2 COMPLETE 2026-07-28 20:00 (yolo26n, dataset v2 21717/4452, batch16, epochs cap 600): EarlyStopping @e322 (50-epoch plateau), BEST @e272: P 0.801 R 0.705 mAP50 0.766 mAP50-95 0.467 (v2 val). best.pt/last.pt in training/runs/puddle/weights/. BATTERY PERCENTAGE IS NOT A STATE-OF-CHARGE READING (user 2026-08-08: "says 100% even though it is measuring 11.89V"). NOT A FAULT, this is how ArduPilot works: remaining % = (BATT_CAPACITY - consumed mAh)/BATT_CAPACITY, and consumed mAh RESETS TO ZERO EVERY BOOT, so it reads 100% at power-on regardless of the pack's actual charge. It is a fuel gauge that assumes a full tank at startup, and it is only meaningful if you genuinely take off on a fully charged pack. THE PACK IS NOT FULL: 11.89V on the 3S (Build Log: 3S 8000mAh) is ~3.96 V/cell, roughly 60-70% resting state of charge (APPROXIMATE, the LiPo voltage/SoC curve is non-linear and load- and rest-dependent), versus 12.6V full. Charge before flying. What actually protects the pack is voltage-based and unaffected by the percentage: BATT_LOW_VOLT 10.8 -> RTL, BATT_CRT_VOLT 10.2 -> Land. CAVEAT ON BOTH NUMBERS: BATT_VOLT_MULT has NOT been calibrated yet (bench test B6), so the FC's 11.89V is itself unverified; check it against the multimeter before trusting either the voltage or the failsafe thresholds derived from it. PARAM GAP FIXED SAME DAY: BATT_CAPACITY was never in pixhawk_full_setup.param, only typed into QGC by hand, so a push_params run would have silently left it unset and nothing tracked it; now added as 8000. OPTIONAL, NOT SET: BATT_LOW_MAH / BATT_CRT_MAH give an mAh-based failsafe, but they are only as good as the current-sensor calibration, so do not enable them until B6 is done.
**BLOCKER FOUND 2026-08-08 IN QGC MOTOR TEST: ALL SIX MOTORS SPIN ANTI-CLOCKWISE.** Adjacent motors must alternate CW/CCW; all-same is the signature of all six ESCs soldered with the same phase order. NOT FLYABLE (uncontrolled yaw, no authority to correct). FIX IS HARDWARE: swap any TWO of the three motor PHASE wires on each motor that is wrong, never reorder the ESC signal leads (that scrambles the motor map instead). NOT fixable in params on this build: direction reversal needs DShot/BLHeli and this board runs plain PWM on MAIN (Pixhawk1-bdshot was already a recorded dead end); the only software exception would be BLHeli-programmable ESCs via their own config tool, unverified here. Which three to reverse must come from ArduPilot's Motor Order Diagram for Hexa X, NOT from assistant memory. AND match the props afterwards: a reversed motor with its old prop produces DOWNWARD thrust.

HARD CUTOFF RECORDED: if the link is not proven by Friday night, shoot the piloted version, because a failed autonomous attempt mid-take is an unusable recording and there are no cuts allowed. Running order drafted in FIELD_CHECKLIST.md.
FIELD TEST PLAN (written 2026-08-08 at user request; this is a MAIDEN FLIGHT for this rebuild, treat it as one).
  TOOL ADDED: tools/check_log.py (NEW 2026-08-08, UNRUN) parses a .bin and prints the numbers that decide whether to continue: VibeX/Y/Z median+95th+max, accel CLIPPING counters, worst sats/HDOP, learned MOT_THST_HOVER, ERR events and EKF/failsafe messages, with an explicit PASS/FAIL against the VibeZ<15 gate. It counts vibration ONLY while throttle-out exceeds --min-throttle (default 0.2), because ground samples are near zero and including them drags the median down and can turn a failing airframe into a passing number; it prints the included-sample count so an empty set is obvious.
  **PROCEDURE BUG FOUND THE HARD WAY 2026-08-08 (assistant's error): push_params.py PUT PRX1_TYPE BACK TO 2.** The sequence I gave was "set PRX1_TYPE=0, then push params", and the param file still contained PRX1_TYPE,2, so the push silently re-armed the dependency the first step existed to remove. A param FILE that contradicts the aircraft's actual fit always wins over a one-off command, so the fix belongs in the file: PRX1_TYPE is now COMMENTED OUT in pixhawk_full_setup.param with the reason and an "uncomment when the ESP32 is refitted" note. FIRST FIX WAS HALF-RIGHT AND FAILED TOO (same day): commenting the line out stopped the file re-writing 2, but could not CLEAR the 2 already on the board, so the next push+readback still showed PRX1_TYPE=2.0. **A param file only asserts what it CONTAINS**; anything that must be OFF has to state its off value explicitly or the file cannot bring a board to a known state. Now fixed properly: the file carries `PRX1_TYPE,0` with both mistakes recorded inline, and push_params enforces it. Set it back to 2 only when the ESP32 is refitted and transmitting. GENERAL LESSON for this param workflow: deleting a line from pixhawk_full_setup.param does NOT revert that setting on the aircraft, it only stops managing it.
  wiring_check ESP32/RING/UP-SENSOR checks are now OPT-IN via --expect-esp32, defaulting OFF because the ring is parked: with the ESP32 unplugged those three lines were FAILing every run and would have trained the operator to ignore red text on the one tool whose whole job is to be believed. Absence is now reported as informational; refit the ring and pass the flag to make silence fail again.
  PORT RESOLUTION (2026-08-08, after both tools threw ENOENT tracebacks): ports move constantly here (Pixhawk USB = ttyACM, SiK radio and ESP32 both land on ttyUSB, lowest free number wins), so wiring_check.resolve_link() now auto-picks when exactly one serial device is present and derives baud from the port type (ttyACM 115200, ttyUSB 57600), refuses to guess when several are present, and prints an actionable message listing what IS present instead of a traceback. bench.py shares it. MEASURED THIS SESSION: the SiK ground radio came up as **/dev/ttyUSB1**, not ttyUSB0, and the auto-pick connected and got the autopilot heartbeat over the radio first try.
  **tools/ AUDIT 2026-08-08: STOPPED EARLY, SALVAGED, NOTHING FIXED YET -> see tools/AUDIT_2026-08-08.md.** A multi-agent audit was launched after the RC false-pass and killed partway to save usage. 4 of 7 reviewers finished (66 raw findings, deduped to ~35 distinct defects) and only 8 of ~66 adversarial verdicts completed, so EVERY item in that file is UNVERIFIED BY A SECOND OPINION AND UNFIXED. Highest-consequence claims, all with source citations from the finders but none confirmed against hardware: check_log.py's clipping detection is DEAD CODE (AP 4.7 logs `Clip` per IMU, not Clip0/1/2, so it always reports zero); check_log.py pools all IMU instances into one VibeZ median so one bad IMU is diluted into a PASS; check_log.py reads hover throttle from PARM which never carries the LEARNED value (that is CTUN.ThH); check_log.py always exits 0 even on GATE FAIL; wiring_check --wiggle can leave the DROPPER GATE OPEN (no confirmation, no try/finally, close ack unchecked); wiring_check's motor-test and wiggle results never reach `ok` so "ALL WIRED CHECKS PASS" prints after motor failures; bench.py get/setparam accept ANY PARAM_VALUE as the answer; push_params.py still uses the naive wait_heartbeat this repo documented as broken. Two systemic patterns name the whole set: testing that a MESSAGE EXISTS rather than that the thing is TRUE, and a printed verdict that never reaches the EXIT CODE. RE-RUN IS CHEAP NOW: the workflow script was rewritten to Sonnet finders + Haiku verifiers, 2 verifiers not 3, 5 lenses not 7, capped findings, and no giant context repeated into every verifier prompt.
  **FALSE-PASS BUGS IN wiring_check.py, FOUND BY THE USER 2026-08-08 ("RC passes with the transmitter off"), ALL FIXED.** The class of bug is the same each time: testing that a MESSAGE EXISTS instead of testing the thing the message is supposed to prove. (1) RC was the dangerous one: it passed if any channel value sat between 800 and 2200, but a receiver in failsafe keeps emitting perfectly in-range values (held last-known or programmed failsafe positions), so a dead TX link read as PASS. Now keyed on the autopilot's own MAV_SYS_STATUS_SENSOR_RC_RECEIVER HEALTH bit, which ArduPilot clears on link loss or failsafe; channel movement is reported as extra colour, never as the verdict. (2) GPS passed on GPS_RAW_INT message count, but ArduPilot publishes that message with fix 0 / 0 sats whether or not a receiver is attached, so an unplugged GPS read as PASS; now requires the GPS-driver-present bit as well, with sats still reported and NOT required (0 indoors is normal). (3) TF-LUNA passed on message count for the same reason; now requires a reading INSIDE the sensor's own advertised min/max, since a disconnected serial rangefinder still gets a dutifully-published 0/out-of-range value. Early-exit and the motor-test RC guard were updated to the new signals too. LESSON WORTH KEEPING: for every check in this tool, ask what it would report if the thing under test were UNPLUGGED; if the answer is PASS, the check is decorative.
  ALL FIELD TESTING RUNS OVER THE SiK RADIO (user 2026-08-08): tools/bench.py NEW (mode/battery/failsafe/gps/rng/getparam/setparam subcommands, defaults --conn /dev/ttyUSB0 --baud 57600, imports wait_autopilot from wiring_check so the ESP32-steals-the-heartbeat fix lives in ONE place); wiring_check.py ack timeout now 12s on a <=57600 link because radio latency/loss made a working command print a false "NO ACK", and the fix is patience not teaching the operator to ignore the ack line. **LOG DOWNLOAD OVER THE RADIO IS NOT VIABLE**: a flight .bin is megabytes and a SiK link is a few kB/s, so the vibration-gate step would take hours and eat the battery. Pull logs by USB cable or by taking the SD card out. That is a change to the field sequence, not an optimisation.
  TAKE: aircraft, 2+ charged packs, charger, TX (charged), laptop + USB cable + SiK ground radio, multimeter, hex drivers + spare prop nuts + LOCTITE (C1 was a prop nut backing off), spare props, zip ties, tape, the phone/camera for footage, sunshade for the laptop, and a printed or offline copy of this gate list.
  SITE: open, no trees downwind (C2 was AltHold wind drift into a tree), no people within the fall radius, no metal fence for the compass, low wind. FENCE is still DISABLED in params (commented out), so the only flyaway backstops are the pilot's mode switch and the battery failsafe.
  SEQUENCE, each step gated on the previous one: (1) power on, DO NOT ARM for 2-5 min, wait for 10+ sats and HDOP<1.5 (C3 was armed seconds after power-on at HDOP 65-99); (2) Stabilize, props on, spin up to just under lift-off and check for unusual noise/wobble, disarm, re-check every prop nut by hand; (3) FIRST HOVER in Stabilize only, ~1-2m, 60-90s, unloaded, land, disarm; (4) PULL THE LOG AND RUN check_log.py BEFORE FLYING AGAIN, this is the vibration gate and the whole point of the trip; (5) only on PASS: notch tuning from the log FFT (INS_LOG_BAT_MASK is already 1), then AltHold hover, then Loiter; (6) only after Loiter is stable: loaded hover with ballast to payload weight, relearn MOT_THST_HOVER (MOT_HOVER_LEARN=2 already set), recheck VibeZ WITH the payload, since payload changes the resonance; (7) only then payload integration and mission flights.
  ABORT RULE (CRASH LESSONS, do not improvise): abort = flip to STABILIZE, never disarm. Disarm in the air ONLY for an imminent person-strike or an unrecoverable flyaway.
  STOP CONDITIONS: any clipping, VibeZ median >=15, any EKF variance message, any unexpected yaw/toilet-bowl in Loiter, or a learned hover throttle above ~0.5 at payload -> land, log, fix, do not proceed down the sequence.
  UNO Q IN THE FIELD (2026-08-08): the board is NOT connected to the Pixhawk (TODO 7 blocked), so it cannot fly a mission; what it CAN do, and what the docs/07 "unoq_detection" evidence needs, is run detect_worker.py while the aircraft flies over water and record what it saw. detect_worker.py gained --save-dir: it writes an annotated JPEG per frame that has a detection, un-letterboxing the boxes through camera_geom.letterbox_to_frame (the SAME conversion the flight code uses to locate a puddle, reused deliberately so the picture cannot disagree with the mission's maths). OnnxDetector.infer_rows() now also returns the BGR frame so the annotation uses the exact image that was inferred, not a second grab. Off by default because it writes to disk on every hit.
  NETWORKING THE BOARD IN THE FIELD: there is no wifi at a test site, so the UNO Q must join an Android hotspot, and **the credentials have to be stored and TESTED at home first** because nothing on the board will join an unknown SSID by itself. Whether NetworkManager/nmcli is present on this image is UNVERIFIED, check before relying on it. Find the board's hotspot IP from the phone's connected-devices list. Tailscale over the hotspot also works but adds a dependency on phone internet at the moment you need it.
  MACBOOK AS THE FIELD MACHINE (user 2026-08-08, taking Raghav's Mac): CODE BUG FOUND AND FIXED BY THE QUESTION ITSELF -- the port auto-detection globbed only /dev/ttyACM* and /dev/ttyUSB*, which are LINUX names, so on macOS every tool would have reported "nothing plugged in" while the hardware sat there working. serial_candidates() now also globs /dev/cu.usbmodem* (Pixhawk CDC), /dev/cu.usbserial* (FTDI SiK) and /dev/cu.SLAB_USBtoUART* (CP210x SiK), and is_usb_cdc() picks the baud. NOTE for macOS: use /dev/cu.* and never /dev/tty.*, because opening a tty.* device blocks waiting for carrier detect that a USB serial adapter never asserts, so the tool would HANG rather than fail.
  MAC SETUP STILL REQUIRED (all UNVERIFIED on that machine, and the Mac's recorded state is 2026-07-26, i.e. two weeks stale and predating the board reflash): git pull the repo; create a python venv with pymavlink AND **pyserial** for tools/ (2026-08-08: `pip install pymavlink` alone is NOT enough, pymavlink does not pull pyserial in, and if the unrelated PyPI package `serial` is present instead you get `module 'serial' has no attribute 'Serial'` from mavutil; fix = `pip uninstall -y serial; pip install -U pyserial`) (this repo's training/.venv is Linux-only and gitignored, the Mac has only the separate SITL venv); write PRIVATE.md from PRIVATE.sample.md with the board's CURRENT tailnet IP; re-run ssh-copy-id because the 2026-07-28 reflash wiped authorized_keys AND changed hostname to arduino-drone and the tailnet IP; install QGC for macOS; VERIFY whether the SiK radio needs a CP210x/FTDI driver on that macOS version (chip type not recorded here, check the radio). STANDING RULE CONFLICT TO SETTLE BEFORE THE TRIP: "Friend's machine: no training, no param pushes" (multi-machine coordination rules). If the Mac is the ONLY field machine it may need to push params, so either the user grants an explicit exception or ALL params are pushed from the Linux laptop beforehand and the Mac stays read-only. Do not let a tool decide this silently.
  PHONE-ONLY OPERATION (user asked 2026-08-08, has QGC + Termius on Android): PARTLY. QGC on Android can do the FC side (calibrations, params, motor test, flight display) IF the SiK ground radio is on a USB-OTG adapter, and Termius can SSH the UNO Q over the hotspot. What the phone CANNOT do: run this repo's python tools (push_params, wiring_check, bench, check_log) or analyse a .bin. The vibration gate therefore REQUIRES the laptop, so the laptop is not optional for the first field trip.

FOV endgame: coin method AT FLIGHT CONFIG (720p, focus 0), coins slid until half-cut by each frame edge, tape read on the floor, more light. AWAITING D + W. Coin-on-tape fallback given (coins as edge markers, read tape on the floor, no image readability needed). AF allowed for the reading only IF focus breathing proven negligible by a paired capture (same unmoved scene, locked vs AF; identical edge extent = AF-read W valid for flight config). CALIBRATION AT 1080p ALLOWED with crop-equivalence check: capture same scene 720p AND 1080p camera unmoved; if scene extent matches, read W off the 1080p shot and it applies to the 720p flight config (verifies the per-mode-crop concern empirically instead of trusting memory). USER Q&A saved (why does inference "stop everything"): it is the single-thread ARCHITECTURE (deliberate: no locks, SITL/board identical), not hardware; during poll() the same thread that pumps MAVLink also does pilot-override/arrival/freshness checks, so those go blind for TOTAL ms; the AIRCRAFT keeps flying (position targets persist in ArduPilot) and DESCEND/DROP never poll the detector, so the cost is companion reaction latency (mainly STANDDOWN response), not a mid-air stall. Frame<->fix pairing stays consistent during the block (tel freezes with the frame since no step() runs). MITIGATION IF S ADOPTED: move inference to a worker process/thread with poll() collecting results = new concurrency code + re-test of the SITL-proven loop, cost it against Aug 10 before committing.

## MICRO-FACTS (small but load-bearing)
UNO Q status LEDs (docs-verified 2026-07-28, user-manual): 4 RGB LEDs, 2 MPU-driven; status LED segments in /sys/class/leds = red:panic (the actual trouble light), green:wlan, blue:bt; solid blue=BT up, cyan-ish flashing=wlan activity on top of blue = NORMAL. Inspect: for f in /sys/class/leds/*/brightness; do echo "$f = $(cat $f)"; done. LEDs say nothing about supply quality; power acceptance = loaded meter at 5V pin.
Camera test cmds (UNO Q): lsusb | grep -i logitech; v4l2-ctl --list-devices; v4l2-ctl -d /dev/video0 --set-ctrl=focus_automatic_continuous=0 --set-ctrl=focus_absolute=0; capture: v4l2-ctl -d /dev/video0 --stream-mmap --stream-count=1 --stream-to=test.jpg.
UNO Q: Debian 13 (PEP668: pip only in ~/venv), sudo NEEDS password (never ask for it; give user the command), docker installed, /usr/lib/chromium 361M (do not remove, App Lab may need), ~ has ArduinoApps/, board hostname "drone".
Laptop training/.venv also carries: pymavlink 2.4.49, pyserial, yaml (via ultralytics), ultralytics 8.4.105, torch 2.13.0+cu130.
ESP32 build env: PlatformIO NOT globally installed on laptop; user has PlatformIO VSCode extension (their build path); Claude's route = python venv with platformio pip pkg (toolchains cached in ~/.platformio, survive). Build outside the repo or .pio is gitignored anyway.
Loaded-hover rule: if learned MOT_THST_HOVER > ~0.5 at full payload, trim payload (thrust margin thin; topic is user-settled, raise only via measured data).
Thrust math never measured on F550: treat loaded hover log as the authority.
gh CLI authed (account in PRIVATE.md). Git commits: NO Co-Authored-By trailer (user rule 2026-07-25). Commit authorship (2026-07-29): repo-local user.email = the GitHub noreply address (exact string + user's real email in PRIVATE.md ONLY; noreply chosen because repo will go PUBLIC for judges = harvest-proof, identical attribution; pre-2026-07-29 commits author sleuther@localhost, left as-is, history never rewritten). RULE: no real personal emails in any committed file.
QGC path + SSH key path: PRIVATE.md.
mosquito dataset page has BibTeX for citation; license CC BY 4.0 requires attribution in docs.
ESP32-as-USB-serial trick (for SERIAL5 sniff): ESP32 EN pin to GND holds chip in reset, its USB-UART bridge becomes passthrough; external TX -> board's TX0 pin -> PC.
3V3 sensor-rail budget VERIFIED 2026-07-27 from datasheets: TCA9548A Icc 20uA typ/35uA max (TI DS, 3.6V 400kHz); VL53L0X ~19mA typ ranging/~40mA peak/uA standby (ST DS); mux sequences so ~1 sensor ranges at a time; even impossible all-7-peak = 280mA. ESP32 dev-board 3V3 pin = onboard 5V->3.3V reg shared w/ ESP32 (WiFi unused): design fine on paper; VERIFY remaining = which regulator the actual board carries -> meter in series on 3V3 feed during 7-sensor bench test.

2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) RAGHAV'S SUMMARY TRIAGED (user forwarded it asking what still matters): every item is already recorded in state (camera_geom + site-dedup fix = TODO 11; predict.py = TODO 14; run_mission = TODO 13/10; decision 1 = TODO 12 blocked, needs the board test; decision 2 = servo on Pixhawk AUX, kept after 2026-08-01 re-litigation, waiting on the user's AUX-channel confirm + SERVO9_FUNCTION=0 staging per TODO 8/10d; decision 3 = FOV, measurement procedure handed to the user this session).
