2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) **DETECTOR MOVED OFF-THREAD (user-directed): detect_worker.py + FileDetector, in-loop inference kept as --inline-detector fallback.** The user's file-handoff design, implemented as specced because it is sound: uno_q/detect_worker.py owns camera+ONNX in its OWN process and atomically rewrites one JSON (tmp + os.replace; default /tmp/monsoonready_det.json, tmpfs so no SD wear) every cycle even with zero rows, so mtime doubles as the liveness heartbeat; detector.FileDetector reads it from the mission loop (stat per tick, JSON parse only on new seq). THE LOAD-BEARING SUBTLETY: a result read now is for a frame captured one inference ago (~5m of travel at 3 m/s with s), so FileDetector keeps a ~20s deque of telemetry snapshots and resolves the puddle against the snapshot nearest t_frame (wall clock, same machine), incl rangefinder freshness judged at frame time (at_t param). Refactor: shared row->Detection logic (geom-size fallback, best-first rows, site dedup, _locate/_height_agl) extracted to detector._RowResolver, used by both; OnnxDetector gains infer_rows() (grab+preprocess+run, no telemetry) which IS the worker's engine, so preprocessing cannot diverge. run_mission.py: spawns the worker (start_new_session, log ~/detect_worker.log, atexit terminate) or REUSES one whose file is fresh (<5s) = no double-spawn camera fight after a SIGKILLed runner; FileDetector.preflight waits <=30s for fresh output + frame-size check, camera_ok:false in the payload = camera dead vs file stale = worker dead. mission.py UNTOUCHED (poll interface preserved; SITL regression unaffected). Also fixed: ort.set_default_logger_severity(3) kills the /sys/class/drm warning (bench_models.py + OnnxDetector); basestation summarize() 'drops' now excludes ok:false (the flagged over-count). UNVERIFIED (no-commands rule), user must run: py_compile all of uno_q/; board dry-run of the split (worker spawn, preflight, staleness warn on kill -9 of worker, detection latch); set_default_logger_severity exists in ort 1.28 (API from model memory, VERIFY on first board run, symptom = AttributeError at startup); the 2026-07-29 laptop 6/6 OnnxDetector test predates this refactor and is STALE, re-run it plus a FileDetector variant (fake worker file) before flight. Consequence for model choice: with the pump no longer blocked, yolo26s's 1518ms stops being a safety argument and becomes only a cadence/coverage cost; interval_s stays as the worker --interval (default 1.0).
2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) MODEL-SIZE VERDICT **REVERSED** by the user, and he is right. My "no" assumed the detection is only ever used as a CENTROID to fly to. User's actual plan: estimate puddle AREA from the detection box plus a height measurement, and size the Bti dose from it. Under that plan box QUALITY is load-bearing, not incidental, so mAP50/mAP50-95 buy something real and a bigger backbone can be worth its latency. New position: MEASURE FIRST, do not decide from either of our arguments. New files for that: training/export_variants.py (laptop; exports stock PRETRAINED yolo26s/m to ONNX at imgsz 640 purely for timing, COCO weights so their detections are meaningless, only the architecture/op-mix/shape transfer) + uno_q/bench_models.py (board; same byte-identical letterbox as detector.py, warmup then median/min/max, reports preprocess vs inference vs TOTAL and an x-slower ratio). The number that decides it is TOTAL, not fps, because mission.py is single-threaded and TOTAL is how long poll() blocks the MAVLink pump; compare it to detector.interval_s=1.0.
2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) **AREA-BASED DOSING: DESIGN CONFLICT, NOT YET RESOLVED** (surfaced by the user's dosing plan; nothing implemented). Six problems, all load-bearing: (a) TF-Luna range is 0.2-8m but detection happens at SURVEY altitude (default 15m), where the Luna cannot read, so "area from the box + TF-Luna height" cannot work as stated at detection time; (b) mission.py DELIBERATELY does not re-detect during descent ("close-range frames unreliable, RUN1 spotcheck"), so there is no detection at any altitude the Luna can measure; (c) that leaves rel_alt (above HOME) as the only height available at detection, which is the AGL-datum error already flagged in TODO 10f; (d) a bounding BOX overestimates the water area of any non-rectangular puddle, and the error is unbounded (an L-shaped or diagonal puddle can be a fraction of its box); dataset v2 HAS polygons for ~3001 of 3881 val labels but ultralytics dropped them because we train a DETECT model, so segmentation is the honest route to area and is a different training run; (e) camera_geom.py has no area helper, only point offsets (ground_offset) and full-frame footprint; the correct area is not w_px*h_px*scale^2 because tangent-space scaling is nonlinear away from the image centre; (f) the dropper is a FIXED-dwell gate, so a variable dose means a variable dwell, which needs the granule flow rate from the TODO 3 bench test. RESOLUTION OPTIONS (pick before building anything): re-detect once at low altitude with a Luna-valid height (contradicts (b), needs a new decision + SITL work), or accept rel_alt-based area at survey altitude with the datum error documented, or descope to a fixed dose and report area as an ESTIMATE in the log/dashboard only. This must be settled before it is claimed in docs/01 or to a judge.
2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) SERVO SILICON CORRECTION (user challenged with a datasheet, correctly): the STM32U585 DOES have hardware PWM timers, and nothing in this project's record ever claimed otherwise. The blocker was never the chip. It is (1) the Arduino UNO Q ships a ZEPHYR core and the stock Arduino Servo library does not build for it, so the pulse train needs an implementation written against Zephyr's PWM API + devicetree rather than Servo.h, and (2) the Linux side has to reach that sketch across the Bridge, which is TODO 12 and BLOCKED on the Serial1 contradiction. Plus the unverified 3.3V-into-MG90 logic threshold. Recorded because "the UNO Q cannot do servo PWM" would be a FALSE compressed version of this decision and would fail truth rule 8 on re-reading.

2026-08-01 (Reyansh/laptop) MODEL SIZE QUESTION (user: is yolo26s/m worth it at 2 fps?): NO, and the reason is that fps is not the binding constraint, coverage is. At 15m survey alt with the 60deg placeholder FOV the along-track footprint is ~6.5m, so 2.05 fps supports up to ~13 m/s of survey speed, far above anything this aircraft will fly; the detector already throttles itself to 1 fps deliberately (interval_s=1.0) because the mission loop is single-threaded. The real limit on detection quality is recall 0.70 on frame-filling sheet water, which is a DATA failure mode (documented in the run2 spotcheck) that a bigger backbone does not fix. Cheaper wins in order: dataset v2 nadir/frame-filling images (TODO 9), then measuring HFOV, then int8 quantisation if speed ever does bind. IF the question is revisited, do NOT guess the fps cost: export a pretrained yolo26s to ONNX and benchmark it on the board first (no training needed for a speed number), then decide.

PROP MATERIAL, ASKED 2026-08-10 (carbon-nylon vs ABS 1045): RECOMMENDATION = carbon-nylon, but NOT before the competition submission. Reasoning: this project's documented failure mode is VIBRATION (crash #1 a nut backing off, crash #3 vibration corrupting the altitude estimate into a 47 m climb), and a glass/carbon-filled nylon blade is stiffer, so it holds its designed pitch under load instead of flexing and flapping, which is a genuine vibration source. It is also more durable and marginally more efficient. THREE HONEST CAVEATS, none of which I can quantify from a verified source: (a) BALANCE DOMINATES MATERIAL - an unbalanced carbon-nylon prop is worse than a balanced ABS one, so buy a prop balancer with them or the upgrade can backfire; (b) stiffer blades transmit more shock to motor bearings and the frame in a strike, and cut people rather than slapping them, which matters on a machine flown near judges; (c) carbon-nylon is typically slightly heavier. THE TIMING ARGUMENT IS THE DECIDING ONE: log 37 PASSED the vibration gate on the current ABS props (VibeZ median 7.5 vs <15), so the aircraft has a flight-proven prop configuration RIGHT NOW. Swapping material changes the airframe's dynamics and invalidates that evidence, requiring a fresh hover-and-check-log cycle nobody has time for. So: fly ABS through the submission, buy carbon-nylon as the post-submission upgrade, and bring it forward ONLY if clipping or VibeZ gets worse. Availability in 1045 from the approved Indian suppliers is NOT verified - check Robu/FlyRobo before assuming.

**2026-08-10 EVENING FLIGHT: THE 4.5 MINUTE "ENDURANCE" IS AN ARTEFACT OF THE CURRENT SENSOR, NOT THE BATTERY.** Flight ended on a LOW BATTERY CAPACITY (mAh) trigger while the pack read ~11.8 V on BOTH the FC and a multimeter, i.e. ~3.93 V/cell, roughly 65-70% charged. The arithmetic is conclusive: log 37's counted burn rate is 1990 mAh/min, and 4.0 min x 1990 = 7960 mAh against BATT_CAPACITY 8000. The FC's phantom mAh counter reached the pack's rated capacity at almost exactly four minutes and terminated a flight that had most of its energy left. THIS ALSO INVALIDATES TODAY'S ENDURANCE MEASUREMENT - the stopwatch number is real but it timed an artificial cutoff, not the pack. NOTE FOR THE RECORD: earlier param dumps showed BATT_LOW_MAH=0 and BATT_CRT_MAH=0, which would mean no mAh failsafe exists; the flight proves something capacity-based DID fire, so those dumps are stale or another mechanism is in play. VERIFY the mAh-failsafe params from the new log's own PARM records before changing anything. NEW HYPOTHESIS FOR THE TRUE CONSTANT, stronger than the earlier 17.0 guess and cleanly explaining the wrong value: 90.6866 is the module's "90 A" RATING typed into QGC's AMPS-PER-VOLT field. For a 90 A full-scale sensor on the Pixhawk's 3.3 V input the correct figure is 90/3.3 = 27.3 A/V. Correction ratio would be 3.32x (true consumed today ~2700 mAh); the rival 17.0 gives 5.33x (~1680 mAh). DECIDED BY MEASUREMENT, NOT ARGUMENT: charger mAh returned divided by the FC's reported consumed for this flight gives the ratio directly. Measurements ordered before charging (per-cell balance-lead voltages expire on charge): all three cell voltages, rested pack voltage, and the log's consumed figure for this flight alone.

**2026-08-10 LOG 39 (the evening flight; log 38 is NOT it, it is a 405 KB stub. LASTLOG.TXT said 40; 39 is the 11.7 MB file timestamped 18:53).**
GOOD NEWS FIRST, AND IT IS REAL: **VERDICT PASS.** VibeZ median 7.4, max 13.1, and **clipping +0** against log 37's max 37.2 and +16. Hover ThH 0.370-0.397, GPS 10-11 sats. The airframe is healthy and the historic vibration blocker is not biting. Nothing about the airframe stopped this flight.
**MY 3.3 V SATURATION CHECK WAS WRONG AND HAS BEEN REMOVED.** It declared log 39's 143 A "IMPOSSIBLE" for PERVLT=17 while the aircraft was demonstrably flying. The 3.3 V analog-input ceiling was my assumption, never read from a datasheet, and it produced a false alarm in the one tool whose job is to be trusted about airworthiness. Replaced with a check that assumes no hardware at all: counted consumption exceeding BATT_CAPACITY (log 39: 8376 mAh counted from an 8000 mAh pack) proves the scaling is wrong and proves any battery failsafe fired on arithmetic rather than on the cells. LESSON, same class as the PRX1_TYPE and hardcoded-1900/1000 bugs: do not encode a guessed hardware constant as a hard verdict.
**THE FINDING THAT MATTERS, AND IT IS NOT A CALIBRATION PROBLEM.** Log 37 flew PERVLT=90.6866 -> 1990 mAh/min, 167 A peak. Log 39 flew PERVLT=17 -> 1764 mAh/min, 143 A peak. PERVLT changed by 5.33x; the reported current changed by 1.13x. Had the reading tracked the constant, log 39 would have shown 373 mAh/min and 31 A. **The reported current is essentially INDEPENDENT of BATT_AMP_PERVLT**, so no value of that parameter will fix it and the whole "which constant is correct, 17 or 27.3" line of reasoning is moot. Two candidate explanations, UNRESOLVED and not decidable from logs: (a) the current sense path is faulty (open, shorted or the module's current output dead) so the pin reads something fixed and large regardless; (b) one of the two PARM values was not actually in force during its flight, since check_log takes the LAST PARM record in a log and a mid-log change would mislead it. DECISIVE BENCH TEST, 30 seconds, props off, disarmed, motors stopped: read the reported current at zero load. True idle draw is under ~1 A; tens of amps at zero throttle proves the sense path, not the constant. `./python tools/bench.py battery`
USER'S CELL MEASUREMENTS, POST-FLIGHT: 3.95 / 3.94 / 3.97 V, resting pack 11.9 V. Max spread 0.03 V = a WELL-BALANCED, healthy pack, which independently clears the battery of blame. ~3.95 V/cell resting is roughly 65-75% state of charge (LiPo resting-voltage-to-SOC is nonlinear, so treat as approximate), i.e. about 2000-2800 mAh actually used against 8376 counted, an error around 3-4x. FLIGHT TIME 4.5 min was NOT endurance: it was the phantom counter reaching 8000.

**2026-08-10 CURRENT SENSOR SOLVED, AND ENDURANCE IS NOW A MEASURED NUMBER. Charger returned 2279 mAh against 8376 counted.**
RETRACTION FIRST, because a wrong claim sat in this file for one message: the entry above saying "the reported current is essentially INDEPENDENT of BATT_AMP_PERVLT" and "no value of that parameter will fix it" is **WRONG and withdrawn**. It came from a bug I introduced in check_log.py the same afternoon: the tool took the LAST PARM record in a log, and log 39's PERVLT=17 was written at t=758s, 131 s AFTER the motors stopped at t=627s. **Both flights actually flew at PERVLT=90.6866.** Verified by timestamping every PERVLT record against the throttle window in both logs. check_log.py now resolves every battery parameter to its value AT MOTOR START and separately prints anything changed afterwards, so this class of error cannot recur silently. Same lesson a third time today: state the evidence's TIME, not just its value.
THE ARITHMETIC, ALL CONSISTENT: over-count ratio 8376/2279 = **3.68x**. Correct **BATT_AMP_PERVLT is about 24** (raw ratio gives 24.7; allowing 3-5% charger inefficiency gives 23.7, so 23-25 is the honest band, and 24 is a sound value to fly). This also confirms the earlier reading of the fault: 90.6866 was the module's **90 A rating typed into QGC's amps-per-volt field**, and the theoretical figure for a 90 A full-scale sensor on a 3.3 V input is 90/3.3 = 27.3, close to the measured 24.
**REAL PERFORMANCE, measured not modelled**: 2279 mAh over 285 s of motor time = **480 mAh/min**, i.e. **28.8 A hover current** and **16.7 min endurance on a full 8000 mAh pack, 13.3 min to a 20% reserve**. The independent momentum-theory estimate made before any of this was 22-34 A for a 2.5 kg AUW. It predicted the answer. The airframe was never the problem and never drew a kilowatt.
**SAFETY, ACT BEFORE THE NEXT FLIGHT: BATT_MONITOR was set to 0 after landing** (log 39, same moment as PERVLT->17). 0 disables the battery monitor entirely, so there is currently NO voltage sensing and NO battery failsafe of any kind on the board. It must go back to 4 and the board must be REBOOTED for a BATT_MONITOR change to take effect. Unclear whether this was deliberate (silencing the phantom failsafe) - ask the user before assuming.
STILL WORTH DOING, lower priority now: the zero-load current reading (`./python tools/bench.py battery`, props off). One flight total cannot separate a scale error from an offset error, and a residual BATT_AMP_OFFSET would still distort low-current readings even with PERVLT correct.

**2026-08-10 TOOLS REFACTOR: ONE TOOL PER AREA OF EXPERTISE (user's instruction, and it is now a standing rule in CLAUDE.md).**
NEW `tools/mavlink_link.py` - a LIBRARY, no CLI, refuses to run as a script. Holds the four things every tool must get right: port/baud resolution across Linux AND macOS, autopilot targeting (the ESP32 at compid 195 is the reason), stream rates a SiK link can carry, and ack matching. WHY THIS MATTERED MOST: these lived inside wiring_check.py, so bench.py, flow_test.py and push_params.py all imported a 623-line PASS/FAIL test just to open a serial port. Importing a test to get a library is backwards and coupled the wiring check to the param push. wiring_check.py is now 490 lines and is a wiring VERDICT only.
NEW `tools/parameters.py` - get / set / push / pull / merge. REPLACES AND DELETES push_params.py, pull_params.py, make_complete_params.py, and bench.py's getparam/setparam. There were THREE separate copies of "write a value and check the echo" and only one checked for a silently CLAMPED value; now there is one, `write_param()`, and everything uses it.
`tools/bench.py` is probes only: mode / battery / failsafe / gps / rng.
TWO OPEN AUDIT ITEMS CLOSED IN PASSING, both from tools/AUDIT_2026-08-08.md:
- pull backup overwrite: raw dumps are now `board_dump_<timestamp>.param`, so a bad pull (board half awake, link dropping) can no longer overwrite the last good snapshot with a short one. param_dumps/* is gitignored so keeping every pull is free. Also warns explicitly when fewer params arrived than the board reported, instead of writing a silently incomplete "backup".
- merge truncation: `make_complete_params.py` wrote `str(int(float(new)))` for every non-float param type, so a fractional override such as 0.14 on a param QGC typed as an int was written to the loadable file as **0**. Silent, and exactly the kind of thing that gets loaded and flown. Fixed: integer-ify only values that really are integers, and print a NOTE when an override is fractional but the dump types it otherwise.
NEW in parameters.py: a NEEDS_REBOOT table. `set BATT_MONITOR 4` now says out loud that nothing changes until the board is rebooted. Explicitly documented as NOT exhaustive - ArduPilot marks many parameters this way and there is no way to ask the board which.

2026-08-10 BATT_MONITOR=4 and BATT_AMP_PERVLT=24 are WRITTEN AND ECHOED on the board; PERVLT re-read as 24 after a retry. BATT_MONITOR still needs the REBOOT to take effect. For the record because the tool's own wording confused the user and could confuse anyone: **BATT_MONITOR 4 = "Analog Voltage and Current", which is the WORKING setting and the value both flights actually ran on. 0 is the one that means no sensing**, and 0 is what the board was left at after landing on 2026-08-10. Two tool fixes from this exchange: (a) the NEEDS_REBOOT note for BATT_MONITOR read "no sensing at all until rebooted", which parses as "you just set it to no sensing" rather than "the change is inert until restart"; reworded. (b) `parameters.py get` asked ONCE, so a dropped request printed "does the parameter exist on this firmware?" and accused the user of a typo - which is exactly what happened on the first PERVLT read-back, with the second identical command succeeding. It now retries 3 times and distinguishes a lost packet from a wrong name.

**2026-08-10 BATTERY MONITOR FULLY VERIFIED ON THE BENCH, THREAD CLOSED.** After reboot with BATT_AMP_PERVLT=24 and BATT_MONITOR=4: **12.62 V and 0.14 A at idle**, pack connected, props off, disarmed, sustained over ~2 minutes. Reads: (a) 12.62 V is 4.207 V/cell on a full 3S, so BATT_VOLT_MULT=10.8025 is CORRECT and the failsafe thresholds (LOW 10.8 / CRT 10.2) mean what they say; (b) 0.14 A is the FC + GPS + SiK + receiver at rest, so **BATT_AMP_OFFSET=0 is correct and there is no offset error hiding under the scale error**; (c) it is NOT 0.00 A, which is the reading that would have meant a dead sense line, so the current path is genuinely sensing. The monitor is now trustworthy for the first time in this project. Remaining uncertainty is only in the 3.68x ratio's own precision (charger inefficiency puts the true PERVLT somewhere in 23-25); the next full-pack flight refines it for free by the same charger cross-check.
Small tool fix from this run: bench.py let Ctrl-C raise, printing a pymavlink traceback that looks exactly like a crash at the end of a run that worked perfectly. It now exits cleanly. Ctrl-C is the normal way to end these probes, since they all run until --seconds expires.

**HANDOVER.md WAS STALE AND SAID SO ITSELF WHILE BEING IT** (fixed 2026-08-11): its LIVE THREAD asked the next session to request a `tools/bench.py battery` result that had ALREADY been taken and recorded, in this very file and in commit ea16e3d (12.62 V, 0.14 A at idle, BATT_AMP_OFFSET cleared, thread closed). LESSON, and it is the same class as the PRX1_TYPE param-file bug: **a handover's LIVE THREAD must be rewritten in the SAME commit that closes the thread**, because a stale handover reads as current and the next session acts on it. Its own header says exactly this; the discipline failed at the moment of closing the thread, which is precisely when it is hardest to remember.

**ANOTHER ENDURANCE FLIGHT IS REJECTED as today's use of the pack**, and the reason is that the question it would answer is already answered: with BATT_AMP_PERVLT corrected to 24 and verified (12.62 V / 0.14 A idle, offset clear), endurance is MEASURED at 16.7 min full pack / 13.3 min to a 20% reserve, hover 28.8 A. The video take is 5-10 min, so the margin is roughly 2x and a repeat buys one confirmation of a number that is not close to binding. Cost of the repeat is the whole evening plus a pack cycle.
**THE ARDUINO/UNO Q LINK (TODO 7) IS ALSO NOT TODAY'S WORK**, and this is not a fresh judgement: Plan B was pre-committed when the link missed its recorded Friday deadline, so the video does not depend on it. Reopening it now trades a certain deliverable for an uncertain one four days out.
**THE ACTUAL CRITICAL PATH IS THE DROPPER**, because it is the only subsystem the video must show that HAS NEVER WORKED ONCE: the gate opens the wrong way (needs the mechanical horn reseat 60 deg on the spline THEN the pulse swap in uno_q/dropper.py, in that order) and the granule flow rate has never been measured (tools/flow_test.py), so the variable-dose seconds are proportional to nothing. In a single unedited take, a gate that fails is the failure the camera is pointed at. This work needs the laptop + Pixhawk on USB + a --wiggle, i.e. roughly 30-60 min of BENCH time, and therefore CANNOT be started in a 15-minute window before leaving; a half-reseated horn is worse than an untouched one because the gate can end up resting OPEN over a loaded hopper.
**IF THE AIRCRAFT FLIES TODAY IT SHOULD FLY THE VIDEO, NOT A TEST**: a timed dress rehearsal of the docs/04 running order (takeoff, survey pattern, hover at drop height, land) proves the take fits inside the measured endurance and rehearses the two-person one-camera choreography, which is the part with no software backstop. Endurance data falls out of it for free via check_log.py.

## PLAN A RESTORED BY USER OVERRIDE (2026-08-11 ~16:45)
**THE PLAN B PROMOTION IS REVOKED.** User, explicitly: full autonomous detect-and-drop is the GOAL for the Saturday 2026-08-15 farmland shoot, and piloted flight + Arduino/ground detection is the BACKUP. This restores TODO 134's original framing, which the 2026-08-11 morning session had overwritten by treating the recorded Friday cutoff as already fired. Per CLAUDE.md an explicit user contradiction wins over any recorded decision; nothing else does. TODO 7 (UNO Q<->Pixhawk link) is therefore back ON the critical path, not out of scope.
**PORT BUDGET KILLS THE NAIVE DONGLE FALLBACK (user, correcting the assistant 2026-08-11).** The assistant proposed buying a USB-serial adapter as same-day insurance against D0/D1 being claimed by the router, WITHOUT checking where it would plug in. There is nowhere: the UNO Q has ONE USB-C port, it goes to the ETZIN EPL-1207CC splitter, the splitter's single USB-A socket is occupied by the B525 camera, and the camera cannot be given up because onboard detection IS the demo. So the dongle fallback is not one part, it is TWO (a hub downstream of the splitter's A port, plus the adapter), each unverified on this board, plus weight. That changes its cost enough that it is no longer obvious insurance.
**CONSEQUENCE: D0/D1 MUST BE TESTED ON ITS OWN MERITS, TONIGHT, AND IT NEEDS NO PARTS.** D0/D1 is the STM32 USART1 on the JDIGITAL pin header, not a USB port, so the primary plan never touched the USB budget in the first place. The correct sequence is test first, and only shop if it fails.
LESSON (assistant error, same family as the earlier ones in this file): a part recommendation is a WIRING claim and falls under truth rule 9. "Buy X" is not safe advice until it is checked against where X physically connects on THIS build. The port budget was recorded in this very file and was not consulted.
**TODO 7 PROBE SKETCH WRITTEN 2026-08-11: uno_q/sketch_serial1_probe/.** Physical loopback test, jumper D1->D0, writes 0x5A on Serial1 and checks whether it returns. Verdict is on LED_BUILTIN and NOT on a serial monitor, deliberately, because the monitor may itself be routed through the peripheral under test: SOLID = Serial1 is ours and the D0/D1 plan lives; FAST BLINK = opened but nothing returned, which is either the router holding the pins or a jumper not seated (re-seat before believing it); SLOW BLINK = setup() never completed. Probe byte is 0x5A for alternating bits, so a stuck-low or shorted line cannot fake a pass the way 0x00/0xFF could; the RX buffer is drained before the write so boot noise cannot be read as an echo. SCOPE OF A PASS, stated so it is not overclaimed: it proves the UART is reachable from sketch code and unclaimed, NOT that the Pixhawk link works, which needs the byte-shovel plus real SERIAL5 wiring. UNVERIFIED AND THE LIKELY SNAG: the exact toolchain for getting a sketch onto the UNO Q's STM32 (App Lab vs arduino-cli on the board) is NOT recorded anywhere in this repo and the assistant has not confirmed it; if upload is the thing that blocks, that is a separate problem from the Serial1 question and must not be read as a FAIL of this test.
**D0/D1 ARE ALREADY WIRED TO THE PIXHAWK (user, 2026-08-11).** The SERIAL MAP's split-cable plan (pin4 TX5 -> UNOQ D0, pin5 RX5 <- UNOQ D1) is BUILT, not pending. TODO 7's "must be tested on the board" is therefore a SOFTWARE question only; no wiring work remains before it can be answered.
**ASSISTANT ERROR, HARDWARE-DAMAGING, CAUGHT BY THE USER: the first probe sketch told him to jumper D1 to D0 for a loopback.** With the Pixhawk connected that ties the Pixhawk's TX5 output and the UNO Q's TX output onto one net, i.e. two push-pull drivers in contention whenever they disagree. This is truth rule 9 missed AGAIN in the same session as the USB-port miss: a test procedure that touches pins is a wiring instruction, and the wiring state was recorded in this file and was not consulted. The rule that would have caught both: before recommending any physical action, read what is already connected.
**PROBE REWRITTEN AS LISTEN-ONLY, WHICH IS ALSO A BETTER TEST.** ArduPilot streams heartbeats on any MAVLink-configured serial port unprompted, so the Pixhawk is already sending into D0 and the sketch needs no jumper, no parts, and never transmits (so it cannot contend with TX5). It listens 3s and reports on LED_BUILTIN: SOLID = MAVLink framing byte (0xFD v2 / 0xFE v1) seen, so Serial1 is unclaimed AND the wire is good AND the baud is right, D0/D1 plan LIVES; FAST BLINK = bytes arriving but no framing byte, so the UART and wire work and the suspect is SERIAL5_PROTOCOL/SERIAL5_BAUD (wrong baud looks exactly like this); SLOW BLINK = zero bytes, which is router-holds-Serial1 OR SERIAL5 unconfigured OR TX5 not landing on D0, three causes this test cannot separate, so check the params first because that is free. A pass proves the pins are USABLE, not that the link is flight-worthy; that still needs the byte-shovel plus a soak.

**2026-08-11 TODO 7 PROBE REDESIGNED, because the old one could not be READ.** User confirmed the UNO Q is SEALED INSIDE THE AIRFRAME and is reachable over SSH. The existing probe reported its verdict only on LED_BUILTIN, which nobody can see with the aircraft shut: a verdict you cannot read is not a verdict. It also only LISTENED, so it could never prove the direction that actually matters for autonomy (UNO Q -> Pixhawk commands).
NEW DESIGN, and it answers from the far end of the wire: the sketch TRANSMITS a real MAVLink HEARTBEAT on Serial1 as MAV_COMP_ID_ONBOARD_COMPUTER (191). If the link works the PIXHAWK sees a new MAVLink node, which is readable from the LAPTOP over USB with the airframe shut, via the new `./python tools/bench.py nodes`. A row for sys 1 comp 191 IS the pass. The ESP32 already proves the channel: it shows up the same way at compid 195.
TRANSMITTING IS SAFE AND IS **NOT** THE RETRACTED LOOPBACK IDEA. D1 is the UNO Q's TX feeding the Pixhawk's RX5, which is what the wiring is for. The retracted hazard was a JUMPER tying D1 to D0, shorting the UNO Q's TX to the Pixhawk's TX5 output (two push-pull drivers fighting). Nothing in the new sketch does that; do not fit that jumper.
THE LED IS KEPT as a second, independent channel reporting the RECEIVE direction only, because the two channels fail independently and that is what localises a fault: nodes PASS + LED solid = both directions work, TODO 7 SOLVED; nodes PASS + LED blinking = our TX arrives but TX5->D0 does not, so ONE BROKEN CONDUCTOR, not software; nodes FAIL + LED solid = we hear the Pixhawk but D1->RX5 is the suspect conductor; nodes FAIL + LED slow-blink = Serial1 is not ours or SERIAL5 is unconfigured, check params first because it costs nothing.
IF OPENING Serial1 KILLS THE LINUX BRIDGE, THAT IS THE ANSWER, NOT A CRASH: it would mean the Bridge really does fall back to Serial1, the two cannot coexist, and the byte-shovel architecture (Linux -> Bridge -> STM32 -> Serial1 -> Pixhawk) is impossible as drawn. SSH is unaffected either way; it runs over the network on the Linux side and never touches this UART.
NO INVENTED WIRE FORMAT: the heartbeat's 21 bytes were generated by pymavlink on the laptop and pasted in, and the sketch's CRC-16/MCRF4XX was verified against pymavlink's own output (reproduces 0xC6AE) with seq-bumped frames parsed back by a real MAVLink parser, 4/4, before being written into C. crc_extra=50 was READ from pymavlink's message map, not recalled. Bumping the sequence byte requires recomputing the CRC; a stale CRC is dropped silently by the receiver and would look exactly like a dead wire.
STILL UNKNOWN AND IT IS THE NEXT QUESTION: how a sketch is BUILT AND UPLOADED to the STM32 from the UNO Q's Linux side over SSH. Nothing in this repo records the toolchain. Discover it before writing any more sketch code.

**2026-08-11 BOARD FACTS, MEASURED NOT ASSUMED (user ran the discovery over SSH).** `arduino-cli` IS installed at /usr/bin/arduino-cli, and /opt holds `arduino` and `openocd`, so an STM32 build+flash path exists on the board itself; no laptop toolchain is needed. Linux-side serial ports are **/dev/ttyS0..S3 ONLY** - no ttyACM, no ttyUSB. Camera CONFIRMED WORKING: B525 HD Webcam at /dev/video0 and /dev/video1 (plus the Venus codec nodes at video2/video3, which are not cameras). Home directory holds loose copies of some project scripts and models (uno_q/, bench_models.py, spotcheck_onnx.py, best.onnx, the yolo26n_*.onnx size variants, yolo26s/m); USER SAYS THE REPO IS NOT CLONED THERE, so treat anything on the board as a possibly-stale copy and never as the source of truth.
**NEW AND POTENTIALLY ARCHITECTURE-CHANGING: the /dev/ttyS0..S3 shortcut has never been tested.** The recorded plan routes Linux -> Bridge RPC -> STM32 sketch -> Serial1 (D0/D1) -> Pixhawk, which stacks THREE unknowns (Bridge works, Serial1 unclaimed, byte-shovel correct). But if the Pixhawk's SERIAL5 TX lands on any Linux-side ttyS, all three vanish and pymavlink just opens the device. The recorded claim "UNO Q D0/D1 = STM32 USART1 (PB7/PB6)" implies it cannot, but THAT CLAIM HAS NEVER BEEN VERIFIED AGAINST THE BOARD and is exactly the kind of unchecked inherited fact this session is auditing. `uno_q/find_pixhawk_uart.py` written to settle it: strictly READ-ONLY, listens on every ttyS/ttyUSB/ttyACM at 115200 and 57600, counts MAVLink framing bytes, and names the port if one carries MAVLink. Costs one command and either deletes the entire sketch route or confirms it.

**2026-08-11 BOARD AUTO-SYNC SERVICE (user asked for it): uno_q/deploy/board_sync.sh + monsoonready-sync.service.** Polls `git fetch` every 10s and fast-forwards when main moves. WHY IT WAS NEEDED: the board carried loose scp'd copies of a few scripts, so "what is on the board" and "what is in git" were different things and nobody could say how different; every debugging session began by wondering whether the board had the current file. The board is now a mirror of main.
THREE DESIGN RULES, each tested locally against a real bare repo before shipping, not just reasoned about: (1) **FAST-FORWARD ONLY.** If the checkout has commits the remote lacks it STOPS and logs loudly rather than merging or resetting, because a `git reset --hard` puller silently destroys work that exists nowhere else. Verified: a board-local commit survived and the loop refused to pull. (2) **SELF-UPDATE.** If a pull changes board_sync.sh itself, the running copy is stale, so it exits 0 and systemd's Restart=always launches the new version; without this the board would keep running whatever shipped first forever. Verified: exit 0 with the right log line. (3) **IT NEVER RUNS WHAT IT PULLS.** Syncing code and executing code are different jobs, and a puller that restarts services turns a bad commit into a flying aircraft's problem.
OPERATIONAL NOTES: logs to journald (`journalctl -u monsoonready-sync -f`). On a PHONE HOTSPOT IN THE FIELD it polls every 10s and costs mobile data for nothing, so stop it before leaving (`sudo systemctl stop monsoonready-sync`). Repo path /home/arduino/monsoonready. The loose copies already in ~ are NOT deleted and the .onnx models are gitignored so they stay put; run scripts from ~/monsoonready/uno_q/ from now on and treat anything loose in ~ as stale. AUTH: a private repo needs credentials that survive non-interactive fetch (credential.helper store after one manual clone, or a deploy key); a public repo needs nothing.

**2026-08-12 FULL-REPO READ (fresh session bootstrap, read-only, nothing executed beyond git). FOUR CONTRADICTIONS FOUND, one of them flight-affecting.**
(1) **RUN_MISSION.PY STILL CARRIES THE OLD, NOW-INVERTED GATE PULSES, AND THIS IS THE SERIOUS ONE.** `run_mission.py` hard-codes `--servo-closed-us` default **1000** and `--servo-open-us` default **1900**, the pre-2026-08-10 values. dropper.py's reversal made 1600 = CLOSED and **1000 = OPEN**. So the flight runner, launched as documented with no servo flags, would (a) drive the gate the OLD clockwise direction the user rejected, and (b) command **1000us as its resting CLOSED position, which under the new calibration is the OPEN position** - the pre-arm "initial close" would open the gate over a loaded hopper and leave it there. This is the identical bug class already recorded for wiring_check.py ("wiring_check used to hard-code 1900/1000 of its own, so changing the dropper's travel silently did nothing"); wiring_check.py and flow_test.py were both fixed to read `_Gate.DEFAULT_CLOSED_US/_OPEN_US`, run_mission.py was missed. FIX = make its two defaults read the same class constants. UNFIXED as of this entry (nothing was changed this session beyond this record).
(2) dropper.py's module docstring still states "Pixhawk servo rails are 5 V logic" as a settled fact. That claim was **RETRACTED AS UNVERIFIED on 2026-08-06** (see POWER MAP): the outputs originate at 3.3V MCU pins and whether they are buffered to 5V on THIS board has never been checked. A live file asserting a retracted claim is exactly what truth rule 8 forbids; the docstring must be corrected to match, and the surrounding decision does NOT change (the load-bearing reasons were the two missing firmwares, not the logic level).
(3) SERIAL4/5 ARE SWAPPED IN THREE DOC LOCATIONS relative to the SERIAL MAP. Root README.md line 34-35 says the UNO Q is on SERIAL4 and the TF-Luna on SERIAL5; mavlink_io.py's docstring says "SERIAL4 via the STM32 byte-shovel"; uno_q/README.md line 4 says the loop runs over SERIAL4. The map (final, after the 2026-07-27 swap) is **SERIAL4 = TF-Luna, SERIAL5 = UNO Q**. Harmless to code (nothing reads these strings) but it is wiring prose, and TODO 7 debugging is about to be done against exactly these port numbers.
(4) CHECKED AND CLEAN, recorded so nobody re-audits them: basestation `summarize()` now filters `ok:false` out of its drop count (the 2026-08-01 flagged over-count is fixed, app.py:72); wiring_check.py and flow_test.py both source the gate pulses from dropper.py; check_log.py resolves battery params at motor-start and no longer carries the retracted 3.3V ceiling check; parameters.py `write_param` is the single write-and-echo path.

**2026-08-12 SERVO ORIENTATION CHANGED AGAIN (user), SO EVERY RECORDED GATE PULSE IS VOID. NEW TOOL: tools/servo_jog.py.**
The horn/servo mounting on the gate has been changed, so the 2026-08-10 pair (closed 1600 / open 1000) no longer describes the hardware and must not be used or quoted. Written to replace them by measurement: `./python tools/servo_jog.py`, an interactive jogger on ch9 that moves the gate by a typed amount and direction (`+15`, `-7.5`, `+30us`, `=1600`, bare `+`/`-` for one --step), marks the two positions with `c` and `o`, and prints them at exit.
WHAT IT CAN AND CANNOT KNOW, recorded because a future session will otherwise read "shows the current angle" as a measurement: an MG90 has NO position feedback and the Pixhawk cannot read a servo's angle, so NOTHING in this system can measure where the horn is. The PULSE WIDTH is the only real number. The displayed angle is arithmetic, (us - reference)/US_PER_DEG, with US_PER_DEG=10.0 from the 2026-08-10 bench observation (900us swung the horn 90 deg on this servo). That ratio survives a remount because it is a property of the SERVO; what a remount changes is where zero is, which is why the tool's reference is simply wherever it started and is re-zeroable with `z`. If a commanded 90 deg does not look like 90 deg, the ratio is wrong for this unit: pass --us-per-deg, do not invent compensating pulse numbers.
DIRECTION CONVENTION KEPT FROM THE BENCH: increasing pulse width swings the horn CLOCKWISE AT THE SPLINE. Whether clockwise now opens or closes the gate is exactly what the new mounting decides and exactly what the by-eye run establishes.
DESIGN CHOICES WORTH KEEPING: starts at 1500us (mid travel) because with an unknown mounting no position is known safe and mid travel is least likely to be hard against a stop, with --start to begin somewhere known instead; keeps the 800-2200us guard from mavlink_io.py; warns on any single move over 45 deg, since driving a metal-gear servo into a mechanical stop at full speed is how the gears this servo was chosen for get stripped; and DELIBERATELY DOES NOT CLOSE THE GATE ON EXIT, because after a remount the tool has no idea which end "closed" is - it prints the final position loudly instead and says to check by eye before loading the hopper. Requires HOPPER EMPTY (it drives to positions nobody has verified, half of which may be open) and SERVO9_FUNCTION=0, since ArduPilot acks ACCEPTED and moves nothing otherwise.
ONE-TOOL-PER-AREA JUSTIFICATION (the standing rule): this is not a second home for anything. bench.py is read-only probes and adding a servo mover would break its stated contract; wiring_check.py is a wiring VERDICT and its --wiggle is a pass/fail cycle between two KNOWN pulses, not a search for unknown ones; flow_test.py measures grams per second and needs the pulses as an input. servo_jog.py is the tool that PRODUCES those pulses, and it is the only interactive positioner.
CONSEQUENCE STILL PENDING: when the user reports the new c/o values they must land in BOTH uno_q/dropper.py (DEFAULT_CLOSED_US/DEFAULT_OPEN_US) AND uno_q/run_mission.py, whose --servo-closed-us/--servo-open-us still hard-code the pre-reversal 1000/1900 instead of reading dropper.py (the live bug recorded in the entry above). wiring_check.py and flow_test.py already read dropper.py and follow automatically.

**2026-08-12 servo_jog.py GUARD REMOVED ON USER INSTRUCTION, AND A CLAMP HAZARD FOUND WHILE DOING IT.** Raghav's first jog run (MacBook, /dev/cu.usbmodem1101, Pixhawk on USB) walked 1500 -> 2070us fine and was then REFUSED at 2370 and 2570 by the tool's own 800-2200us check. User: remove it, new limits to follow. Done: servo_jog.py now refuses NOTHING and only warns. Two things deliberately NOT changed with it: (a) mavlink_io.PWM_MIN_US/PWM_MAX_US is still 800-2200 and still enforced at dropper.py construction and in MavIO.set_servo, because servo_jog sends MAV_CMD_DO_SET_SERVO directly and does not go through either path, so widening the FLIGHT guard stays a separate deliberate decision; (b) the tool now prints, at exit, whether the marked values fall outside that guard, since dropper.py would otherwise refuse to construct with them.
**THE HAZARD, which is the reason this entry exists: DO_SET_SERVO IS SUBJECT TO SERVO<n>_MIN/_MAX, so an out-of-window command can be ACCEPTED AND THEN CLAMPED**, leaving the servo somewhere other than the pulse width printed on screen. That would silently corrupt the very calibration the tool exists to produce, and it is invisible from the ack. servo_jog now READS SERVO9_MIN/_MAX at startup (read-only, no push, so it is inside Raghav's machine scope) and warns on every command outside them. OPERATOR RULE RECORDED IN THE TOOL: when that warning appears, believe the GATE, not the number; a gate that stops moving while the printed pulse keeps climbing has hit the CLAMP, not a mechanical stop. NOT INDEPENDENTLY VERIFIED from ArduPilot source that DO_SET_SERVO clamps to SERVOn_MIN/MAX; it is treated as a live possibility and warned about rather than asserted, which costs nothing either way. Also warns outside a 500-2500us band, explicitly labelled a typo catcher and NOT an MG90 spec (no datasheet consulted), plus a stall warning: buzzing and not moving = driven into a stop, back off, and that is now the only way this tool can damage hardware.
NOTE FOR WHOEVER FIXES THE PULSES: SERVO9_FUNCTION=0 is evidently already correct on the board, since the jog commands were ACCEPTED and moved the gate. No param push is needed for this work, which keeps it inside the standing "no param pushes from Raghav's machine" rule.

**2026-08-11 BOARD SUDO PASSWORD IS LOST (user), AND IT TURNED OUT NOT TO MATTER.** PRIVATE.md's UNO_Q_SUDO line is still the template's "password required (user types it; never ask for it)", so it was deliberately never recorded and cannot be recovered from this repo. NOT WORTH REFLASHING FOR: the only thing that wanted root was installing a systemd unit, and **a user-scope service needs no privilege at all**. Added uno_q/deploy/monsoonready-sync.user.service (`systemctl --user`, installs under ~/.config/systemd/user/) plus uno_q/deploy/README.md documenting three install routes in order of privilege: user service, user crontab with @reboot + flock, and only last the system service. USER-UNIT CONSTRAINTS ARE FORCED, NOT PREFERENCES: a user unit must NOT set User= (systemd refuses to load it), has no multi-user.target so it uses default.target, and drops the sandboxing directives because those only mean something when the alternative is running as root. REBOOT PERSISTENCE is the one real gap: a user service stops when the user's last session ends unless `loginctl enable-linger arduino` is set, and that may itself demand authentication; the crontab route is the fallback that needs no privilege of any kind. GENUINE PASSWORD-RECOVERY OPTIONS IF ROOT IS EVER ACTUALLY NEEDED, none of them cheap: another account already in the sudo group; a physical console (HDMI or serial) into recovery/single-user to run passwd, which means opening the airframe; or a reflash, which on 2026-07-28 wiped authorized_keys and changed both hostname and tailnet IP. Four days from the deadline, all three are worse than simply not needing root.

**2026-08-13 (Reyansh) BOARD REFLASHED, DRONE OPEN FOR THE LAST TIME, 1 HOUR.** Two days to the deadline, not four; the 2026-08-11 entries saying "four days out" are stale by two days.
**CRITICAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED IN THAT HOUR: run_mission.py would have EMPTIED THE HOPPER ON THE GROUND.** Its `--servo-closed-us` default was 1000 and `--servo-open-us` 1900, hard-coded, while dropper.py had been reversed to 1600 closed / 1000 open. So run_mission's "closed" WAS THE OPEN POSITION: the gate would swing open the instant the servo was initialised, before takeoff, and dump the entire Bti load. Fixed by importing PixhawkServoDropper.DEFAULT_CLOSED_US/DEFAULT_OPEN_US instead of repeating the numbers. THIS IS THE THIRD TIME the same class of bug has appeared (wiring_check's hard-coded 1900/1000, then this): any file that restates a hardware constant instead of importing it WILL drift, and the drift is silent until it is expensive. Grep for repeated pulse numbers before trusting any new gate code.
**uno_q/deploy/board_setup.sh written** for the post-reflash rebuild: idempotent, root-free, creates ~/venv and installs pymavlink pyserial numpy opencv-python-headless onnxruntime, then VERIFIES each import, the camera via v4l2-ctl, the serial ports, the repo and the model. It deliberately does NOT clone the repo (operator syncs separately) and does NOT copy the ONNX models, because they are gitignored and CANNOT ARRIVE BY GIT PULL - they must be scp'd from the laptop, and a fresh flash has none. opencv-python-HEADLESS specifically: the board has no display and the GUI build pulls X libraries that are not present.

**2026-08-13 models/best.onnx IS NOW TRACKED IN GIT (Reyansh asked why it was not, and the ignore rule was simply wrong for this file).** It was swept up by the blanket "training artifacts are regenerable" rule, which is true of the VARIANTS and false of the DEPLOYED model: reproducing best.onnx needs the exact dataset plus ~12h of GPU. THE RISK THIS CLOSED WAS REAL AND ACUTE: the file existed only on the owner's laptop and the board, the board had just been REFLASHED, so it was down to a SINGLE COPY two days before the deadline, and a dead SSD would have ended the project. It was also the one file the board could never obtain by git pull, which defeated the entire point of the auto-sync service and forced a manual scp into every recovery. 9.4 MB against a 31 MB repo is not a cost worth the exposure. Variants stay ignored and correctly so: yolo26s (37 MB) and yolo26m (79 MB) were rejected on measurement and are genuinely regenerable. board_setup.sh now looks for $REPO/models/best.onnx FIRST and treats a loose ~/best.onnx as a possibly-stale leftover.

**GROUND IS COMMON. NEVER ASK ABOUT IT AGAIN (user, 2026-08-13, after the assistant raised it more than once).** EVERYTHING on this aircraft is powered from the ONE battery through the PDB, so every ground is bonded by construction and a separate signal-ground wire is neither missing nor needed, on SERIAL5 or anywhere else. Any future "check the grounds are bonded" suggestion is WRONG BY CONSTRUCTION on this airframe: do not raise it, do not add it to a checklist, do not list it as a suspect when a serial link misbehaves. Suspect instead: SERIALn_PROTOCOL, SERIALn_BAUD, TX/RX swapped, or the peripheral itself.

**2026-08-13 ALL FIVE CRITICAL PARAMS VERIFIED ON THE BOARD, over the SiK radio (ttyUSB0/57600, so the aircraft was on battery and the UNO Q was powered): SERIAL5_PROTOCOL=2, SERIAL5_BAUD=115, SERIAL4_PROTOCOL=9, PRX1_TYPE=0, SERVO9_FUNCTION=0.** All correct, nothing to push. CONSEQUENCE: the Pixhawk side of the D0/D1 link is FULLY CONFIGURED, so if the UNO Q still cannot see MAVLink the fault is the wire or the UNO Q side, never the parameters. PRX1_TYPE=0 also confirms the parked ESP32 CANNOT block arming, which was the only way the abandoned obstacle ring could still hurt the demo flight.
**BOARD REBUILD AFTER THE REFLASH IS COMPLETE**: ~/venv has pymavlink, pyserial, numpy, cv2 and onnxruntime all importing, /dev/ttyS0..S3 present, and models/best.onnx (9.4 MB) arrived BY GIT PULL rather than scp, which is the tracked-model change paying off immediately on its first real use. Repo lives at ~/monsoonready-drone (NOT ~/monsoonready); board_setup.sh now derives the repo path from its own location so the name no longer matters.
**DISCONNECTED AT TEARDOWN AND MUST GO BACK BEFORE FLIGHT (user, 2026-08-13): GPS and RC RECEIVER.** Only telem radio, power, Arduino, TF-Luna, safety switch and buzzer were attached during this session, so any wiring_check run from this session will FAIL GPS and RC and that is expected, not a fault. Neither can be omitted: no GPS means no Loiter/AUTO and therefore no autonomous mission, and no RC means no manual abort.

**2026-08-13 ttyS SHORTCUT IS DEAD, BUT NOT FOR THE REASON THE TOOL CLAIMED.** All four /dev/ttyS0..S3 failed with `Could not configure port: (5, 'Input/output error')` - they could not be OPENED at all. find_pixhawk_uart.py counted zero MAVLink bytes and printed "NO MAVLINK ... that CONFIRMS the recorded claim that D0/D1 belong to the STM32", which was **a conclusion drawn from a failure mode the script never distinguished**. An unopenable port is the ABSENCE of evidence; only a port that opens and stays silent is evidence. Tool fixed to count unopenable attempts and report INCONCLUSIVE instead. WHAT IS ACTUALLY ESTABLISHED: those ttyS nodes exist in /dev but are not usable UARTs from userspace (no hardware behind them, or not pin-muxed), so **Linux cannot reach the Pixhawk directly and the STM32 sketch route is the only one left**. The D0/D1-is-USART1 claim remains UNVERIFIED either way; it just no longer matters, because the alternative is gone.
**TF-LUNA IS HEALTHY.** Constant 0.13 m on the bench with the aircraft on its legs, flagged OUT OF RANGE only because RNGFND1_MIN is 0.20. It is reading a real surface at a plausible distance; below-min on the legs is expected and is not a fault. One 1.0s dropout at stream start, not repeated.

**2026-08-13 UNO Q SKETCH TOOLCHAIN, MEASURED: FQBN `arduino:zephyr:unoq`, core `arduino:zephyr` 0.90.0 already installed, and arduino-cli sees the board on a NETWORK port (192.168.68.109), not a local serial one** - so uploads take `-p <ip>` rather than a /dev node. Build and flash both run ON THE BOARD over SSH; no laptop toolchain and no physical access are needed, which is why the sketch work did NOT have to happen while the airframe was open.
**IT IS A ZEPHYR CORE, NOT THE CLASSIC ARDUINO STM32 CORE, and that is load-bearing for TODO 7.** The whole Serial1 contradiction (user manual says the router claims Serial1; RouterBridge README says the Bridge uses a core-routed UART "falling back to Serial1 if the core does not provide it") is a statement about THIS core. USEFUL CONSEQUENCE: `arduino-cli compile` is itself a free test. If Serial1 is not exposed by the Zephyr core the build FAILS, and that answers the two-week-old question without flashing anything or touching the aircraft. A successful compile does not prove the pins work, only that the API exists; the flash plus `tools/bench.py nodes` is what proves the wire.

**2026-08-13 THE PROBE SKETCH COMPILES, SO `Serial1` EXISTS ON THE ZEPHYR CORE.** `arduino-cli compile --fqbn arduino:zephyr:unoq` succeeded: 63860 bytes (8% flash), 22848 bytes (8% RAM). This settles HALF the two-week contradiction - the core does expose Serial1 and the API is real, so the user-manual line "do not attempt to open Serial1 in your own code" is not enforced at compile time. IT DOES NOT SETTLE THE RUNTIME HALF, and one detail in the build log sharpens the risk: **Arduino_RouterBridge's singletons.cpp is linked in AUTOMATICALLY** (the sketch never includes it; the core/variant pulls it), so the Bridge object is constructed whether or not the sketch asks for it. That is exactly the collision the RouterBridge README implied with "falling back to Serial1 if the core does not provide it". Only flashing and observing decides it.
BUILD TIMING NOTE so nobody diagnoses a non-problem again: the FIRST compile takes minutes because Zephyr builds the whole RTOS core; the SECOND takes ~10s from `~/.cache/arduino`. A fast second run is the cache working, not a skipped build. User reasonably read the speed-up as "something went wrong the first time".

**2026-08-13 *** TODO 7 IS SOLVED. THE UNO Q TALKS TO THE PIXHAWK OVER D0/D1 -> SERIAL5. *** ** `tools/bench.py nodes` heard **sys 1 comp 191 MAV_TYPE_ONBOARD_CONTROLLER, 21 heartbeats in 20 s** alongside the autopilot's own 20. That is the programmed 1 Hz with no drops, so the link is not marginal. This was the two-week blocker on autonomous flight and it is gone.
**IT ALSO SETTLES THE DOCUMENTATION CONTRADICTION EMPIRICALLY: the router does NOT claim Serial1 at runtime.** The sketch opened Serial1 and transmitted while Arduino_RouterBridge's singletons were linked into the same binary, and the transmission arrived. The user manual's "the router claims Serial1 ... do not attempt to open Serial1 in your own code" is NOT enforced on core arduino:zephyr 0.90.0, and the RouterBridge README's "falling back to Serial1" did not happen here. Recorded as MEASURED BEHAVIOUR OF THIS CORE VERSION, not as a general truth: a core update could reintroduce it, so re-run the probe after any core upgrade.
UPLOAD RECIPE THAT WORKED, for the record: `arduino-cli upload -p 192.168.68.109 --fqbn arduino:zephyr:unoq <sketchdir>`, which prompts for the board's LOGIN PASSWORD and flashes over SWD via openocd + libgpiod bitbang (no USB cable, no physical access, aircraft closed). STM32U585, TrustZone disabled, RDP level 0, 2048 KiB dual-bank.
**WHAT IS PROVEN AND WHAT IS NOT, because the difference is the remaining work:** PROVEN = STM32 -> Serial1 -> Pixhawk (the TX direction). NOT PROVEN = (a) the RX direction, Pixhawk TX5 -> D0, which the sketch reports only on LED_BUILTIN and the airframe is now shut; (b) **Linux -> STM32 via the Bridge**, which is the half the mission actually needs, because the ONNX detector runs on LINUX and its drop commands must reach the Pixhawk. The current sketch never touches the Bridge. NEXT: a byte-shovel sketch that pumps Bridge <-> Serial1 in both directions, after which pymavlink on Linux opens the Bridge and the full loop closes.

**THE ONLY THING LEFT ON THE AUTONOMY CRITICAL PATH IS LINUX -> PIXHAWK.** Restating precisely so it is not re-litigated: the ONNX detector runs on the UNO Q's LINUX side; its drop/guided commands must reach the Pixhawk; Linux cannot reach the Pixhawk directly (all four /dev/ttyS* fail to configure, `find_pixhawk_uart.py` INCONCLUSIVE 2026-08-13); only the STM32 has D0/D1 -> SERIAL5, and that path is proven in the TX direction only.

**TWO ROUTES, AND THE CHEAP ONE WAS NOT CONSIDERED UNTIL NOW.**
(A) **USB CABLE, UNO Q USB HOST -> PIXHAWK USB.** Gives Linux `/dev/ttyACM0` speaking MAVLink2 on the Pixhawk's SERIAL0 with NO firmware work, NO Bridge, NO byte-shovel. `MavIO.__init__` already takes a plain pymavlink connection string, so `MavIO('/dev/ttyACM0')` is a DROP-IN and every existing tool works unchanged. EVIDENCE IT IS FEASIBLE, not assumption: the USB camera already enumerates through a hub on that port, so the UNO Q's USB host mode is a measured fact. UNVERIFIED AND MUST BE CHECKED BEFORE RELYING ON IT: (i) a free port on the hub, (ii) the Pixhawk's micro-USB physically reachable with the airframe closed, (iii) a USB-A -> micro-B cable on hand, (iv) SERIAL0_PROTOCOL = 2 (ArduPilot default, but verify). RISK: nothing in ArduPilot objects to USB and battery power at once; this is how every bench session has been run all project.
(B) **BYTE-SHOVEL over Arduino_RouterBridge.** Still the fallback if (A) is physically impossible. BLOCKED ON A FACT NOBODY HAS YET: what the LINUX side uses to call the MCU's RPC methods. If there is no Python binding for the Bridge on the Qualcomm side, route (B) IS DEAD and only (A) remains, so that discovery must happen before any firmware is written.
BRIDGE API, from `bridge.h` on the board (partial, grep only): `class BridgeClass` with `bool begin(unsigned long baud = DEFAULT_SERIAL_BAUD)` (it calls `serial_ptr->begin(baud)` and writes a banner, so THE BRIDGE OWNS A SERIAL PORT and which one is not yet known), `bool is_started()`, `bool getRouterVersion(MsgPack::str_t&)`, `bool provide(...)` (signature truncated at line 224). `class RpcCall` carries `result()`, `isError()`, `getErrorCode()` and a read/write mutex pair. So it is **MsgPack-RPC, not a raw byte pipe**: a shovel would be MCU-side `provide("mav_write", ...)` writing to Serial1 and `provide("mav_read", ...)` draining it, with Linux polling. NOTE THE HAZARD: if `serial_ptr` turns out to BE Serial1, calling `Bridge.begin()` collides with the proven comp-191 path and would break TODO 7's result.

## 2026-08-15 06:40 FULL CODE REVIEW OF EVERYTHING OUTSIDE tools/ (user-requested, 6431 lines)

**BUG 1, THE WORST ONE IN THE PROJECT, FIXED: a coin-flip at connect time made the mission command AUTOTUNE instead of GUIDED and DRIFT instead of RTL.** `MavIO.wait_ready` used `conn.wait_heartbeat()`, which returns the FIRST heartbeat of ANY component. The ESP32 ring is comp 195 on the same vehicle, beats at ~1 Hz like the autopilot, and ArduPilot forwards it to us over SERIAL5, so it wins that race about half the time (the 2026-08-14 e2e run showed comp-1 and comp-195 heartbeats alternating almost 1:1). pymavlink refuses to lock `target_system` onto a heartbeat it does not judge to be a VEHICLE, and `mavutil.probably_vehicle_heartbeat` explicitly excludes MAV_TYPE_ONBOARD_CONTROLLER, so an ESP32-first connect leaves target_system 0, whose default `mav_type` is **1 = FIXED WING**. `mode_mapping()` then returns the PLANE map and every mode name silently means something else: **GUIDED -> 15 = AUTOTUNE on Copter, RTL -> 11 = DRIFT on Copter, and custom_mode 4 reads back as "ACRO"** so the pilot-override test stands the mission down instantly. REPRODUCED both orderings through real pymavlink (plane map 26 entries, copter map 27). COULD NOT HAVE APPEARED BEFORE 2026-08-14 (the ring was silent) AND CANNOT APPEAR IN SITL (no second component), which is exactly why every prior test passed. FIX: wait_ready now requires a heartbeat from **component 1**, forces the target_system lock, and REFUSES TO RUN if the resulting map has no GUIDED. Callers updated to catch RuntimeError alongside TimeoutError.
**BUG 2, FIXED: the mission stood down before it began, on a race.** `tel.mode` only advances on a HEARTBEAT (1 Hz) but `set_mode`'s COMMAND_ACK returns in tens of ms, so on the first loop pass `tel.mode` still held the pre-takeoff mode and the pilot-override test fired => STANDDOWN, which is NOT in the `('DONE','STOPPED')` branch, so the aircraft was left armed and climbing with the mission process gone. FIX (two layers): `set_mode` now pumps until the heartbeat confirms and returns bool; the override test is gated on `_seen_guided`, so it can only fire after GUIDED has actually been observed. REGRESSION-TESTED both ways (lagged start -> DONE; genuine pilot takeover -> STANDDOWN).
**BUG 3, FIXED (found by review agent): the board's git auto-sync has been DEAD since the 2026-08-13 reflash.** board_sync.sh defaulted to `$HOME/monsoonready` and both .service files hardcoded it, but the clone is `~/monsoonready-drone`. board_setup.sh had been patched for this rename; these three never were. board_sync.sh now self-resolves from its own location like board_setup.sh; both unit files corrected.
**BUG 4, FIXED: board_setup.sh never installed flask or msgpack.** flask = the base station dashboard, which the mission auto-launches and which is FILMED TWICE in the demo video; msgpack = the Linux half of the byte-shovel. Both had been hand-installed after the reflash, so a rebuild would have silently produced a board that cannot serve the dashboard or reach the Pixhawk.
**BUG 5, FIXED: board_setup.sh printed "no serial devices at all" directly under a list of serial devices.** `ls a* b* c* | sed || bad` with `set -o pipefail`: ls exits nonzero when ANY glob misses even though it printed the matches, which is the board's normal state (ttyS0-3, no ttyUSB/ttyACM). Reproduced, rewritten without the pipeline.
**BUG 6, FIXED: predict.py scored an undated site at exactly 0.00, below a site never seen at all.** `last_t` initialised to 0.0 = epoch, so `recency = 0.5**(20000/14)` underflowed to zero and zeroed the product. `stamp()`'s own docstring already said missing times must be ignored; the initial value disagreed with it. Now None, with recency dropping out of the product (weight 1.0) when nothing is dated. Verified: 5 undated flights now score 5.00, span still 0.
**ALSO FIXED:** spotcheck_onnx crashed on a directory with no matching images (`cv2.imwrite` on a zero-height grid) - now reports why, and names the .bmp/.webp filter gap; benchmark_onnx crashed on `--runs 0` and drew confidence labels off-canvas near the frame top; gen_fake_mission never wrote dwell_s/area_m2 so the dashboard's dose rendering had no test data; **sketch_serial1_probe.ino now opens with a DO-NOT-FLASH banner** (it lacks mav_read/mav_write, so flying it kills the whole autonomy chain with no obvious cause, and it is the folder muscle memory reaches for); run_mission's `--model` default was the vanished `~/uno_q/best.onnx` and now resolves `<repo>/models/best.onnx` from its own location; run_mission's docstring still described the link as unresolved TODO 12.
**THE BIGGEST NON-BUG RISK, AND IT IS NOT CODE: HFOV HAS NEVER BEEN MEASURED, SO EVERY DETECTION RESOLVES TO NADIR.** run_mission only builds a CameraGeometry when `--hfov-deg` is passed; without it `detector._locate` returns the AIRCRAFT'S OWN lat/lon and the aircraft descends and drops where it was standing when it first saw the water, which can be half a camera footprint away. NEW `uno_q/calibrate_camera.py` turns this into a 10-minute tape-measure job (wall method or known-object method), self-checked (90deg in -> 90.00deg out) and it also prints how many model-input pixels a given target spans. SECOND HALF OF THE SAME PROBLEM: a 0.6 m tray at 15 m is only ~13-25 px in the model's 640 input depending on the true FOV, and YOLO is unreliable below ~40 px. The farm has a water pump and dirt: a **2-3 m wet patch** gives 80-150 px AND looks far more like the training data than a plastic tray. Both are now checklist items. CORRECTION TO MY OWN 2026-08-14 ENTRY: I quoted "58.2deg HFOV" as if measured; it is only an example number in a docstring. Corrected in place.
**ESP32 FIRMWARE REVIEWED AGAINST THE VENDORED MAVLINK SOURCE AND ARDUPILOT'S AP_Proximity_MAV.cpp: NO FALSELY-CLEAR PATH EXISTS.** Dead ch2 -> init fail latches _ok=false -> SENSOR_MM_ERROR -> SECTOR_NO_DATA 65535, and runtime timeouts (ch5) land on the same sentinel. Indices consistent (7/6/72), PITCH_90 for the up sensor is right (checked against ArduPilot's own constant), diag and fake paths compile out, MAVLink packing is library-generated not hand-rolled. **AND IT ANSWERS THE USER'S QUESTION "why do more ring sensors keep failing by one per test?": the ESP32 probes each channel ONCE AT BOOT and latches a failure for the whole session, with no retry anywhere in readAll(). The board stayed powered for hours, so those were RUNTIME read timeouts on a hot sealed frame, and nothing re-initialises them while power is on. A COLD BOOT re-runs init and may recover channels; added to the checklist as a 30-second pre-take action.** Open gap, low severity, NOT fixed (no bench access, and AVOID_ENABLE=0 makes it cosmetic): tcaselect() discards Wire.endTransmission()'s result, so a NACKed mux switch would read the previously-selected channel believing it read the new one.
**REVIEWED AND CLEAN, recorded so it is not re-litigated:** mission.py state machine (full detect->approach->descend->drop->climb->done cycle exercised with the real dropper and real PWM guards: gate 560 -> 1760 -> 560, ends CLOSED, one success counted), camera_geom.py (all 20 numeric assertions re-checked, letterbox undo is the exact inverse of infer_rows), detector.py (dedup is on the SITE not the aircraft; an aborted target stays in _fired, which is the documented "skip target" behaviour, so no abort/retry loop), missionlog.py schema matches app.py AND index.html exactly incl. the absent-means-true conventions, detect_worker.py, dropper.py, training/*, val_compare, merge_datasets (polygon labels are converted by ultralytics at load, not dropped). models/best.onnx independently confirmed as input (1,3,640,640) / output (1,300,6) with conf in [0,1] in letterbox pixel space, matching every consumer.

**CRITICAL CATCH FROM THE SAME QUESTION, FIXED IN THE RUNBOOK: a wifi dropout would have RTL'd the take.** Runbook 6b/6d had the pump and run_mission in FOREGROUND SSH sessions; the drone leaving hotspot range kills the SSH session, the shell delivers SIGHUP, and run_mission's handler treats SIGHUP as "wind up -> RTL" (the pump would just die, stranding GUIDED until GUID_TIMEOUT hover). Both now launch with setsid nohup + log files (pump.log / mission.log), watched via tail -f (a dying tail is harmless). Deliberate stop = pkill -f run_mission.py (SIGTERM -> graceful RTL). run_mission's own docstring always demanded setsid; the checklist had dropped it.

**2026-08-15 ~12:40 NETWORK PLAN v2 (user override + actual research): THE TP-LINK TL-MR3020 CARRIES THE FIELD LAN; the iPhone is only the internet source.** Researched facts, not vibes: MR3020 transmits 20 dBm @ 2.4 GHz (router-class, regulatory ceiling), USB-powered (power bank OK; v1 mini-USB vs later micro-USB - check label for the cable), and its modes (3G/4G router / WISP / AP) run a DHCP LAN with no internet. iPhone hotspot: defaults to 5 GHz (shorter range); 2.4 GHz requires "Maximize Compatibility"; single band at a time; phone APs are pocket-distance devices. NO honest open-field metre figure is claimable for either; the board's own antenna is the other half of every link, so plan for dashboard pauses at the survey's far edge with automatic catch-up. PREFERRED TOPOLOGY = MR3020 in WISP MODE with the iPhone hotspot (Maximize Compatibility ON) as upstream: board + laptop stay on the TP-Link ALL DAY, internet (git pull, on-camera Google-date) flows through it, iPhone dying costs internet but never the LAN, ZERO network switches. FALLBACK = MR3020 standalone: internet phase on iPhone first, then board (`sudo nmcli dev wifi connect ...`, SSH drop mid-command is normal, NM auto-rejoins) + laptop move to TP-Link; the take's date-proof happens with the laptop briefly on iPhone, laptop switches during the 0:30-3:00 components walk (off-camera) so the dashboard is up by the 3:00 AI segment. Dashboard: http://arduino-drone.local:8080 (mDNS is how arduino-cli discovered the board - VERIFY once) or IP from the MR3020 client list.

**FALSE ALARM RESOLVED, AND IT WAS MY DOCUMENTATION BUG: "NO component 191" from bench.py nodes DOES NOT MEAN THE LINK IS BROKEN.** The link was working the whole time - the same session's test_mission_link got its SET_MESSAGE_INTERVAL **ACKed**, which is direct proof of the UNO Q -> Pixhawk TX path, plus live telemetry (GPS fix 3, 13 sats, lat 12.8288867 lon 77.9544641). ROOT CAUSE: the old probe sketch heartbeat FROM FIRMWARE, so comp 191 was always on the bus; sketch_mav_shovel replaced it and only forwards bytes, and MavIO sent commands but never a heartbeat. An idle board is therefore silent BY DESIGN. I had left checklist step 4b telling the user to confirm comp 191 with nothing running, which cost time at the farm on shoot day. FIXED THREE WAYS: (1) MavIO.step() now emits a 1 Hz comp-191 heartbeat (also just correct MAVLink citizenship for a companion; verified on the wire: HEARTBEAT sys1 comp191), so the nodes check is meaningful again WHILE a MavIO process runs; (2) bench.py's verdict text no longer claims a broken link, and explains that nothing heartbeats unless a MavIO process is up; (3) checklist step 4b rewritten. VERIFY IF EVER RELEVANT: FS_GCS_ENABLE is not in pixhawk_full_setup.param and was assumed 0/default; with heartbeats now flowing on SERIAL5, a non-zero FS_GCS_ENABLE would make losing the pump a GCS-failsafe event. Not checked at the farm, not believed to be set.
**PUMP EXIT 1 EXPLAINED: `udp.bind` on 127.0.0.1:14556 raised OSError because A PUMP WAS ALREADY RUNNING** (which is why test_mission_link worked at all: the earlier pump was doing the job; the `[2]` job number showed a job [1] existed). Behind setsid nohup the traceback went to a log nobody had opened, so it read as a failure. The pump now exits with a plain explanation naming `pgrep -af mav_shovel_pump.py` and `pkill -f mav_shovel_pump.py`. ONE PUMP IS ALL THAT IS EVER NEEDED.

Enabled only by `app.py --enable-control` (plus --waypoints/--conn/--camera/--no-drop/--mission-python), because these endpoints arm and fly an aircraft and anyone on the LAN can reach the server; it prints a warning on startup. `find_pids()` reads /proc so the answer survives a base-station restart and sees hand-started processes, AND REQUIRES 'python' IN THE CMDLINE - without that an editor open on run_mission.py or a `grep run_mission.py` counts as a running mission and the START button refuses with "already running" mid-take. Status pills for pump/waypoints/gate, live log tail, auto-disarm on start.

**2026-08-15 ~16:20 GEOFENCE ENABLED (user question: "how do I create it, and how do I know the arduino will not tell the pixhawk to override it?"). Added to param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param, so it goes on with the same `parameters.py push` as everything else: FENCE_ENABLE 1, FENCE_TYPE 3, FENCE_ACTION 1, FENCE_ALT_MAX 30, FENCE_RADIUS 100, FENCE_MARGIN 2. File now parses to 72 params (was 66); parse VERIFIED with the real load_param_file logic, inline `#` comments strip correctly.**
WHY TYPE 3 AND NOT THE 7 THAT WAS SITTING COMMENTED OUT IN THAT FILE: 3 = alt-max + circle; 7 adds the POLYGON bit, and a polygon is a mission-item upload that has to be present and valid or the prearm check fails. On a one-run day that is a new failure mode bought for nothing, because a cylinder already contains a flyaway. RADIUS 100 is measured FROM HOME (= wherever it arms), not from the plot; the survey reaches ~25 m from its start corner, so 100 m absorbs the walk from the ground station and still traps a runaway. ALT_MAX 30 = 2x the 15 m survey altitude and 2x RTL_ALT_M.
THE ANSWER TO "CAN THE ARDUINO OVERRIDE IT" IS STRUCTURAL, AND IT WAS AUDITED RATHER THAN ASSUMED. Two independent reasons:
(1) LOCATION. The fence is evaluated inside ArduPilot, downstream of every MAVLink message that arrives. The companion only ever REQUESTS a position or a velocity; the autopilot decides what to do with the request. There is no message that means "go there and ignore the fence".
(2) VOCABULARY. Grepped every `*_send(` in uno_q/ (flight path): heartbeat_send, command_long_send (only DO_SET_MODE, COMPONENT_ARM_DISARM, NAV_TAKEOFF, DO_SET_SERVO, SET_MESSAGE_INTERVAL), set_position_target_global_int_send, set_position_target_local_ned_send. NO PARAM_SET ANYWHERE and NO MAV_CMD_DO_FENCE_ENABLE ANYWHERE, so no board process can disable, resize or exempt the fence even in principle. The only `fence` match in the whole of uno_q/ is the word "defence" in a run_mission.py docstring. Parameter writing lives exclusively in tools/parameters.py, run by hand from the laptop. (`param_request_read_send` exists in sitl_test.py; it is a READ and it is SITL-only.)
ON BREACH, THE TWO SYSTEMS DO NOT FIGHT: ArduPilot changes mode to RTL, mission.py's next loop pass sees mode != GUIDED, calls that a pilot override and goes STANDDOWN, which stops commanding. STANDDOWN is deliberately excluded from the end_mode branch (the ('DONE','STOPPED') test), so the mission does NOT re-command a mode on the way out and the fence's RTL is left to run. Same mechanism that protects a manual RC takeover; the fence just triggers it from inside the autopilot.
CAVEAT RECORDED HONESTLY: AVOID_ENABLE stays 0 (set today to kill the failing proximity ring) and its bit 1 is FENCE avoidance, i.e. the soft "slide to a stop at the boundary" behaviour. So this fence is a FAILSAFE, not a wall: the aircraft can cross the line and then turns around. Turning avoidance back on is an untested change and was NOT made today.
NEW PREARM RISK, MUST BE CLEARED BEFORE THE TAKE: FENCE_ENABLE=1 adds a prearm check (needs a good 3D fix and home set; a bad fix now blocks arming where it did not before). Arm on the ground BEFORE recording. If it blocks and GPS is fine, the escape is FENCE_ENABLE=0 and fly with the RC pilot as the only backstop.

### .BIN log analysis, logs 42-45 (2026-08-15, user-requested)
- **42 (15:49): the aircraft was DROPPED, deliberately.** Timeline: armed 123.8s, took off, LOITER, RTL at 156.1s, then at 175.3s **RC8 MotorEStop HIGH at about 2 m** cuts the motors; 3 s later at -2.59 m the crash check disarms (`AngErr=165>30, Accel=0.1<3.0`). The 165 deg angle error is the CONSEQUENCE of stopping the motors in the air, not a cause. IMU0 clipping +49 in the same log is the impact. Nothing mechanical failed.
- **43 (15:57): compass, and it is unresolved.** `PreArm: Check mag field: 1038, max 875, min 185`, `PreArm: EKF compass variance`, `EKF3 IMU0 MAG0 ground mag anomaly`, then GPS glitch + 4 EKFCHECK/FAILSAFE_EKFINAV pairs and `AHRS: EKF3[1] vel error 2.0`. Mag field magnitude 1038 against a 875 ceiling means the measured field is ~19% too strong: interference near the compass or a stale/absent compass calibration.
- **45 (16:49): same compass complaint, 1058 vs max 875.** So it is repeatable across power cycles, not a one-off.
- **44 (16:01): armed at 8 sats / HDOP 1.66**, below the project's own 10-sat / 1.5-HDOP arming rule. It recovered to 14 sats in flight, but that arm should not have happened.
- All four logs: `PreArm: Rangefinder 2: No Data` throughout, the known-dead second rangefinder.
- Vibration is fine everywhere (VibeZ median 8.4-9.0 vs the <15 gate). Hover throttle steady at 0.390. Burn rate ~500 mAh/min, so ~12.6 min to a 20% reserve on the 8000 mAh pack.

### Camera failure narrowed (2026-08-15 evening)
- CORRECTION of my earlier guess: calibrate_camera ran ON THE BOARD (user). So /dev/video0 at index 0 OPENED AND CAPTURED midday, then failed all three dashboard launches after ~15:45. Index 0 is CORRECT on the board.
- detect_worker.log: `cap_v4l ... VIDEOIO(V4L2:/dev/video0): can't open camera by index`, 0.07-0.14 s after process start, identical x3. Fails at open(), before any format negotiation. cv2 does not print errno, so cause unproven.
- Topology verified on the board: lsusb shows Logitech B525 (046d:0836); v4l2-ctl --list-devices: B525 = video0 (capture) + video1 (metadata) + media0; video2/3 = Qualcomm Venus encoder/decoder, NOT cameras. Nodes dated Aug 15 11:25 = no re-enumeration all day.
- Candidates: (a) a holder process left from midday (V4L2 capture is exclusive at stream time; a stuck consumer blocks later opens), (b) permission/group difference between the SSH session that ran the FOV test and the process tree that spawned the dashboard. Diagnostics issued: fuser on video0/1, id, v4l2-ctl one-frame grab (real errno), cv2 open one-liner.

## 2026-08-16 (past midnight after the farm day)

### FARM CAMERA ROOT CAUSE, now the leading explanation with direct evidence
V4L2 INDEX SHUFFLING. Tonight the user replugged the camera and the Qualcomm Venus codec devices took /dev/video0+1 while the camera landed on video2+3; cv2.VideoCapture(0) then failed with EXACTLY the farm error ("can't open camera by index"), because index 0 was suddenly the video ENCODER. Which driver gets video0 is a boot/enumeration race. Farm mechanism: plug loosened by vibration (user's theory), camera re-enumerated mid-day, lost the race, all three launches opened the codec instead of the camera. Not provable retroactively (no farm dmesg), but reproduced end to end tonight. FIX (to be coded): open the camera BY NAME via /dev/v4l/by-id, never by bare index.
- dmesg tonight: clean. No over-current, no disconnect storm; hub + camera (1-1.1, at boot+15s) + Pixhawk ACM (1-1.2, boot+69s) all enumerate fine. One unexplained line "usb_vbus: disabling" at boot+33s, noted, not chased.
- PIXHAWK OVER USB THROUGH THE HUB: PROVEN. Hexarotor heartbeat solo and 8/8 heartbeats under attempted camera load. /dev/serial/by-id/usb-ArduPilot_Pixhawk1-bdshot_...-if00.
- Board clock runs ~6 h behind real time (no NTP sync, no RTC battery). Fix commands + NTP explanation given. Logging code will stamp Asia/Kolkata wall time explicitly PLUS seconds-since-boot on every line.
- STILL PENDING before shovel deletion: camera streaming (index 2 right now) SIMULTANEOUS with Pixhawk heartbeats through the hub. Then: delete shovel chain, user pulls D0/D1 and SERIAL5 wires (SERIAL4 TF-Luna untouched), SERIAL5_PROTOCOL to -1.

### 2026-08-16 midday: board proof round + three fixes (all user-ordered)
BOARD PROOF: test_everything ran on the real aircraft: camera by name PASS (video0 through the hub), Pixhawk USB PASS, battery 11.88 V, GPS correctly failing indoors. Dashboard served both laptops' era begins: manual photo button worked end to end (direct path, no worker running).
- LUNA "FAILURE" WAS MY BUG, now fixed: test_everything lacked the ON-GROUND rule that tools/wiring_check.py has carried all along ("an aircraft on its legs legitimately reads below RNGFND1_MIN"). Landed, the lens sits ~0.13 m over the floor (RNGFND1_GNDCLR) and the TF-Luna's rated minimum is 0.2 m, so 0.11-0.13 m readings are the sensor WORKING and ArduPilot flagging them out-of-range by design. test_everything now passes them as "ON-GROUND reading"; flight logic untouched (below-min stays untrusted in the air, correctly).
- RING: the 11:39 test showed bins [0,4,5,7] = 4 of ~5 achievable (6 sensors fitted; ch2 at 120 deg is a DEAD CHIP, proven 2026-08-14 at four bus speeds; software cannot revive silicon). So "most sectors failing" is really: ch2 permanently blind + ONE more sensor latched out + the up sensor silent again (RNGFND2, the known-flaky power plug). ROOT CAUSE OF THE DECAY FIXED IN FIRMWARE: proximity_sensors now has maintain() = one bounded re-init attempt per 5 s for channels that failed boot init or hit 20 consecutive read timeouts (a VL53L0X that browns out stops continuous mode and only init()+startContinuous() bring it back; nothing ever called them, hence sectors only ever DIED across a powered afternoon and revived on cold boot). initChannel() shared by begin() and maintain() so the two paths cannot diverge. Compiled clean with the repo's PlatformIO env (RAM 7%, flash 22.5%). NEEDS FLASHING over the ESP32's USB; question out on physical access with the frame closed. Failure semantics unchanged: a dead sector reports "unknown" (65535), never "clear".
- test_everything ring verdict rewritten to say all of this instead of a bare count; up sensor reported separately (orientation 24).

Endpoint tests 10/10, browser-verified live (12->15 paging, manual tab).
