- FENCE IS NOW A HAND-DRAWN POLYGON (user: "the field is not a cylinder or a rectangle, it is a weird shape I have to draw myself"). uno_q/fence.py (NEW): load/save/validate + push/read_back/clear over the MISSION-ITEM protocol with mission_type=MAV_MISSION_TYPE_FENCE(1) and MAV_CMD_NAV_FENCE_POLYGON_VERTEX_INCLUSION(5001), param1=vertex count, x/y=lat/lon*1e7 (MissionItemProtocol_Fence.cpp:100-143). The LEGACY FENCE_POINT protocol is compiled OUT in 4.7 (AC_Fence_config.h:11-20), so anything written against it would silently do nothing - that is why this went through the mission protocol. Min 3 corners, no repeated closing corner. Stored in the FC's own fence storage, survives reboots. SITL PROOF: 5 corners uploaded, read back, exact match; 4-corner round trip through the dashboard endpoint too.
- **THE DANGEROUS FINDING** (my param-file comment was WRONG until SITL corrected it): with FENCE_TYPE bit 2 set and NO polygon stored, Copter 4.7 ARMS NORMALLY. AC_Fence::pre_arm_check_polygon asks _poly_loader.loaded(), and loaded() is `_load_time_ms != 0` (AC_PolyFence_loader.h:165) - an EMPTY fence "loads" fine. Proven live: FENCE_TYPE=7, ENABLE=1, fence cleared, "Arming motors", no complaint. So a fence you drew but never pushed protects NOTHING and the aircraft says NOTHING. Guard added on our side: test_everything's new 'fence' component reads the polygon back out of the aircraft and FAILS when it is missing or differs from the drawn one. Checklist now treats a red 'fence' row as a no-fly.

Also documented WHY SERVO9_MIN=500/TRIM=560 sit below ArduPilot's documented 800-2200 range and must stay: with FUNCTION=0, DO_SET_SERVO writes the raw pulse (AP_ServoRelayEvents::do_set_servo -> SRV_Channel::set_output_pwm, no constrain), so the range is GCS slider metadata; raising MIN would misdescribe a gate whose measured closed position is 560us. A name/range audit had flagged these two as out-of-range; that is a false positive and is now recorded so it is not "fixed" into breaking the gate.
- PARAM AUDIT RESULT (2 agents, whole file vs 4.7 source): every one of the 65 parameter names exists in 4.7, every enum/bitmask value is valid, no deprecated names, no missing indices. ARMING_SKIPCHK (the 4.7 rename of ARMING_CHECK) already correct. Only findings were the two SERVO9 ones above.

### 2026-08-16 ~15:00 IST: sat-imagery root cause, one-line rows, esptool facts, ESP32 flash blocked on enumeration
- SATELLITE ROOT CAUSE FOUND (user: "take some logs and find out why. This failure has gone on too long"): the 64-tile cap. When the fitted view is WIDE (the all-flights fit spans every mission plus the route and fence, farm coords + field coords = kilometers), the tile count at the screen-matched zoom exceeded 64 and drawSat returned silently - no tiles, no message, "isn't working at all". FIX: step the zoom down (each step quarters the count) until it fits; imagery gets blurrier, never absent. Proven in the browser: a 15 km fence corner previously drew NOTHING, now draws 33 tiles. LOGGING ADDED per the user's demand: every failed tile URL console.warn'ed, satNote states the live mode/count/failure ("sat: N tiles", "direct fetch failed, retrying via board...", "FAILED direct + via board..."), board-side proxy failures already in dashboard.log. The off-state message and console explicitly name the restart-after-pull case: an old dashboard PROCESS serves the new static HTML but has no /api/tile, so the proxy path 404s until start_dashboard.sh is re-run.
- ONE-LINE ROWS (user, after the gallery refresh button wrapped): .ctlRow.oneline (flex-wrap:nowrap; text spans get .shrink = min-width:0 + ellipsis) applied to the gallery row, the self-test row (Check arming demoted from .big to fit), and both wifi rows. Browser-verified single-line at panel width; wfPass reads 0x0 in tests when a saved network is selected because it is display:none BY DESIGN then.
- ESPTOOL IS NOT DEPRECATED - the user read "DEPRECATED: 'esptool.py'" as the tool dying. Fact (from the tool's own output + setup.py): the .py SUFFIX and the underscore spellings (write_flash, --flash_mode) are deprecated in favour of `esptool` and hyphens; same package, same maintainer (Espressif), v5.3.1 current. The corrected spelling produces zero warnings. It stays installed: it is Espressif's official flasher and the only no-toolchain option on aarch64 (espflash would need Rust or a fetched binary). Explained to the user rather than deleted; if he re-affirms deletion after the explanation, espflash is the fallback.
- THE REAL FLASH FAILURE was never the tool: /dev/ttyUSB0 DOES NOT EXIST. lsusb on the board shows hub + camera + Pixhawk and NO serial chip (no 10c4:ea60 CP2102, no 1a86:7523 CH340); dmesg has zero enumeration events for it. So the ESP32 is not on the hub as data: it is powered by its USB connector FROM A 12V->5V MODULE (user correction: the 5V pin is connected to NOTHING - my back-feed warning withdrawn, recorded), and either it is still plugged into that module, or the cable used is CHARGE-ONLY (power flows, no D+/D-, produces exactly this silence; the 2026-08-14 marginal-cable history fits). To flash: move the ESP32's USB to the hub with a KNOWN-DATA cable, watch dmesg for "new full-speed USB device" + the driver line. IF the device enumerates but no ttyUSB appears, the UNO Q's custom kernel (7.0.0-g122c...) may lack cp210x/ch341 modules - UNVERIFIED on the board, check /lib/modules/$(uname -r)/kernel/drivers/usb/serial/. cdc_acm is present (Pixhawk works).
- TAILNET ONLY (user): never offer the LAN IP again. Tailnet IP is now 100.113.28.52 (third IP; proven by successful scp; PRIVATE.md updated). The scp of the four flash binaries to ~/esp32fw/ SUCCEEDED via tailnet.
- launch.json still points the fake dashboard at tcp:127.0.0.1:5760 (SITL) for conn; harmless when SITL is down (control panel shows errors only on use).

### 2026-08-16 ~15:20 IST: USB HOST CONTROLLER ABSENT AFTER REBOOT - camera + Pixhawk + everything gone, not just the ESP32
- Sequence: ESP32 plugged into the hub produced ZERO dmesg events (three tails, nothing - consistent with the charge-only-cable theory). User warm-rebooted (`sudo reboot`). After the reboot `lsusb` prints NOTHING AT ALL, even under sudo: not even the two Linux Foundation root hubs that were always there. That means the SoC's xhci/dwc3 USB host controller did not register this boot; the camera and the Pixhawk are as gone as the ESP32.
- Supporting detail: the new boot's visible dmesg tail has "l5: disabling" at ~33 s but NOT the "usb_vbus: disabling" line that every earlier boot showed alongside it - consistent with the USB path never probing (a regulator that never registered cannot be disabled as unused). The kernel cmdline has deferred_probe_timeout=30, and this SoC's USB block sits in a documented dependency web (usb@4e00000 <-> phy@1615000 <-> i2c encoder), so a deferred-probe failure stays failed for the whole boot.
- CAUSE UNVERIFIED. Candidates, none confirmed: (a) transient probe race that a boot can win or lose; (b) something about what is NOW physically plugged (the ESP32 went into the hub right before the reboot) holding VBUS or data lines in a state the controller/PHY dislikes at probe time; (c) coincidence. The previous boot RAN FINE for 2.4 h with the ESP32 experiments happening, so simple breakage of the port by the ESP32 is not established.
- PLAN GIVEN (evidence first, then cold boot): capture `dmesg | grep -iE "xhci|dwc3|usb|phy"` from THIS broken boot before touching anything; unplug the ESP32 from the hub (isolate the one variable that changed); FULL POWER OFF with power physically removed 10 s (a warm reboot does not drop the PMIC rails or reset a latched VBUS protector; this one did not), power back on with only hub+camera+Pixhawk; then lsusb + the same grep. If root hubs return, replug and proceed; if not, next suspect is the boot-time probe with the evidence grep in hand.
- Aircraft is UNFLYABLE until USB returns (no camera, no Pixhawk link from the board). ESP32 flash obviously on hold.

### 2026-08-16 ~16:45 IST: USB restored by cold boot; ESP32-in-hub-at-boot is the suspect
- Cold boot (full power removal, ESP32 unplugged) brought everything back: both root hubs, Genesys hub, camera, Pixhawk, all enumerated normally (xhci at 5.6 s as always).
- The broken boot's captured grep is conclusive about WHAT failed, not why: "platform 4e00000.usb: Adding to iommu group 7" happened, then xhci-hcd NEVER registered that boot (healthy boots register it ~5.6 s). usbcore/hub-class drivers were all fine; the CONTROLLER probe is what went missing, with the ESP32 plugged into the hub during power-up as the only physical difference.
- WORKING RULE (one occurrence, cause UNVERIFIED - do not state as fact): the ESP32 must NOT be in the hub while the board boots; hot-plug it after boot. If lsusb ever comes back empty again: unplug ESP32, FULL power cycle (warm reboot proven insufficient), then investigate with dmesg | grep -iE "xhci|dwc3|usb".

### 2026-08-16 ~17:00 IST: ESP32 FLASHED (final reliability firmware is ON the chip). "Crystal problem" = measurement artifact, not hardware

Unsupported crystal?" warning is esptool ESTIMATING the crystal from serial timing, and that estimate is garbage on this deliberately-slowed marginal-cable setup. Additionally this same module ran the previous firmware (same build config, same clock assumptions) for weeks with a working 115200 MAVLink link, so the build's crystal assumption matches the physical part. NOTHING TO FIX; warning recorded so it is never chased again.

### 2026-08-16 ~19:55 IST: THE REAL SITE-MAP ROOT CAUSE (empty board = no geo anchor); manual site anchor shipped; ARM caps
- User's screenshot ended the hunt: tab title showed ui 2026-08-16c (page CURRENT), yet map grey with "All flights (0)", "no waypoints" pill, no fence. ROOT CAUSE: this board has NO geodata of any kind, and proj.fit() with zero points sets proj.ok=false, which shows "No mission data yet" and EARLY-RETURNS before drawSat/fence/route (render() line ~1545). The imagery was never going to draw on a virgin board no matter how correct the tile code was. Every earlier server-side finding (byte-identical page, working proxy, no-cache) was true and beside the point; my earlier test data always had waypoints so the empty case was never exercised. The 12:42-18:xx "stale tab" story explained the intermediate window but NOT the final grey.
- WORSE, CONFIRMED BY 3-AGENT READ (sonnet, read-only): on an empty board "Draw fence" was a complete dead end: the #empty overlay (no pointer-events:none) swallowed every canvas click, and even without it unproject() would fabricate NaN corners because fit() bails before initializing lon0/s/ox/oy. So the user's entire planned workflow (draw fence at home BEFORE any flight) was impossible.
- FIX (index.html, UI_VER 2026-08-16d): MANUAL SITE ANCHOR. Empty map now shows, inside the placeholder, "paste its coordinates from Google Maps" + input + Show site. Parses "lat, lon", validates, stores mr-site-anchor-v1 in localStorage (try/catch pattern like mr-theme/mr-layout-v3), and feeds a 400 m box around it into the SAME proj.fit path. Anchor fills a vacuum only: any real geometry (events/route/fence/site image) excludes it, EXCEPT while edit.mode is active, where it keeps steadying the frame (without that, the first placed corner would refit to the 10 m minimum span and the view would leap mid-draw). A small bottom-left chip "site LAT, LON · change" shows while the anchor is the only source; clicking reopens the form prefilled (siteAskOpen flag; render's display toggle now honors it; cancel link when a map exists behind). Canvas mousedown now guards if(!proj.ok) so the NaN class is dead even if overlays change later.
- GOTCHA FIXED DURING VERIFY: showing the chip via style.display='' fell back to the stylesheet's display:none; must set 'block'.
- BROWSER-VERIFIED END TO END against a replica empty board (basestation-empty launch config, port 8081, empty data dir + missing waypoints file): first load shows prompt; typing coords -> 12-35 tiles drawn + chip; Draw fence -> 4 corners clicked at sane lat/lons, view stable, chip hidden while editing; Save -> fence.json written server-side (verified on disk); localStorage wiped + reload -> map fits the SAVED FENCE with tiles, no chip, no prompt (permanent handoff). Regression: data-rich fixtures render unchanged (323 events, 6 wps, 12 tiles, no chip).
- Also this turn: ARM / ARMED – click START caps (user: match sibling buttons; lowercase label predated the styling).
- Screenshot also confirmed working end-to-end: ESP32 "prx ring 4/6 up:ok" STATUSTEXT reaches the dashboard through the Pixhawk (health-based ring row live: bins [4,5,7] while 4/6 alive), prearm reasons on the control panel ("RC not found | Hardware safety switch"), wifi settings panel populated. ch1+ch2 hardware diagnosis unchanged.
- USER PATH TO IMAGERY NOW: board git pull + restart dashboard + reload page (title must say ui 2026-08-16d), then paste the field's coordinates once into the map prompt; draw + Save + Push fence as before. GPS-anchored/route data replaces the typed anchor automatically from the first save onward.

### 2026-08-16 ~20:10 IST: imagery CONFIRMED WORKING by the user; base-zoom panning enabled
- User confirms the site map now shows correct satellite imagery (typed-anchor path working on the real board). Remaining complaint: dragging did not pan at all.
- CAUSE: two deliberate old clamps from the fit-everything era: mousedown ignored drags at view.k===1, and the wheel handler zeroed tx/ty whenever zoom returned to 1. Both assumed base zoom always framed ALL data; with a typed 400 m anchor the world extends past the frame, so base zoom must pan.
- FIX (index.html, UI_VER 2026-08-16e): pan at any zoom; zoom-out no longer discards the pan; dbl-click remains the reset-to-fit gesture (legend already says so). Edit-mode semantics unchanged: at base zoom a plain click still ADDS a corner (that branch runs before pan starts), shift+click adds while zoomed.
- BROWSER-VERIFIED: base-zoom drag of 200 px moved the view center 113 m ground distance with tiles following (12 -> 21 loaded); pan survives a zoom-in/out cycle; dbl-click resets to {k:1,tx:0,ty:0}; fence-edit click at base zoom still adds exactly one corner.
- Note for the user path: HTML-only change, so board git pull + page reload suffices, no dashboard restart; their open 16d tab should show the red RELOAD PAGE banner within 3 s of the pull (first live use of the ui_mtime detector).

### 2026-08-16 ~20:35 IST: fence-drawing glitchiness fixed (click-vs-drag dead zone + frozen frame while editing)
- User: imagery works, but while building a geofence "the movement and adding points are very glitchy". BOTH causes were interactions of the new pan-at-any-zoom with the old editor:
  (1) at base zoom, mousedown ADDED A CORNER IMMEDIATELY (old view.k===1 branch), so every attempted pan during editing splattered corners and never panned;
  (2) every added corner changed the pts fed to proj.fit, so the frame refit and the map lurched under the cursor on each click (with a 400 m anchor the union-of-extents visibly jumps; with old flight-heavy data it never did).
- FIX (index.html, UI_VER 2026-08-16f):
  (1) CLICK-VS-DRAG: mousedown only records intent; a 5 px dead zone decides on mouseup. Stationary press in edit mode = corner added at the DOWN position; movement past 5 px = pan (at any zoom, edit or not). Plain click now also adds while ZOOMED (shift-click no longer needed; edHint text updated to "click map = add · drag corner = move · drag map = pan · right-click = delete").
  (2) FROZEN BASE: render() keeps a baseFit snapshot (pre-applyView fit fields); while edit.mode or a marker drag is active it restores baseFit instead of refitting, then applies pan/zoom on top, so the frame NEVER refits mid-edit; Save/Cancel unfreezes and refits to the result.
- BROWSER-VERIFIED (empty-board replica): stationary click adds with proj frame bit-identical before/after; 120 px drag pans, adds nothing; 3 px jitter still adds; zoomed (k=1.25) plain click adds; corner grab-drag moves the corner 20 m with the view untouched.
- TEST ARTIFACT worth remembering: entering edit mode reflows the map panel (canvas rect shifts ~27 px up when Save/Cancel appear), so synthetic clicks using a rect captured BEFORE fnEdit land 27 px off and "miss" corner handles. The app always reads a fresh rect per event and was never broken for a real mouse; two probe rounds chased this ghost.
- User path: board git pull + page reload only (HTML-only change); tab title must say ui 2026-08-16f.

### 2026-08-16 ~20:45 IST: map zoom-out floor 1 -> 1/64; param push moved to the BOARD CLI
- ZOOM (index.html, UI_VER 2026-08-16g): the wheel clamp was `Math.max(1, ...)`, i.e. you could never see WIDER than the auto-fit frame (with a fresh site anchor that is a 400 m box, so a neighbouring field was unreachable). Floor is now 1/64, symmetric with the existing 64x ceiling. BROWSER-VERIFIED on the empty-board replica: k 1 -> 0.01563, canvas span 440 m -> 29.5 km, back in to k 64 = 7 m across; 229 Esri tiles loaded across z12-z19 with 0 failures, so imagery follows all the way out (the zoom picker walks down to TILE_MIN_Z 3 and the 64-tile budget steps it down further). User reports fence drawing now works.
- PARAM PUSH IS NOW A BOARD COMMAND, not a laptop one, because the Pixhawk USB goes to the UNO Q since the shovel chain was deleted (2026-08-16 conn = Pixhawk USB by-id). Board interpreter is `~/venv/bin/python` (board_setup.sh installs pymavlink there); the repo `./python` wrapper is the LAPTOP venv and does not exist on the board.
      ~/venv/bin/python ~/monsoonready-drone/tools/parameters.py push
  Pushes param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param (72 params, tracked in git) with per-write echo verification; exit 1 if anything is refused or unechoed.
- GOTCHAS recorded so the push is not misread: (a) mavlink_link auto-picks the port only when EXACTLY ONE candidate is present, so if the ESP32 is plugged into the board's USB hub it will refuse and needs `--conn /dev/ttyACM0`; (b) the dashboard opens the Pixhawk link only per-action, so an idle dashboard is safe, but Test everything / generate waypoints / fence push / start mission during the push would fight for the port; (c) SERIAL5_PROTOCOL -1 and PRX1_TYPE 2 are in the NEEDS_REBOOT table, so the Pixhawk must be power-cycled after the push before those two mean anything (the tool prints this); (d) FENCE_ENABLE 1 lands with the push, so a later indoor arm check may refuse with "PreArm: Fence requires position" - that is the fence working, not a regression.

## 2026-08-17 FUNDAMENTAL-PROBLEMS SCAN (user-requested; analysis only, nothing fixed; laptop repo only, no board contact)
Scope per the user: problems of the class "vibration -> blurry photos -> AI cannot analyse", i.e. physics/system-level gaps rather than code bugs. Sources: full PROJECT_STATE read + code read (mission.py, detector.py, camera.py, detect_worker.py, run_mission.py, dropper.py, mavlink_io.py, pixhawk_full_setup.param). FINDINGS ONLY, not TODOs (user owns the TODO list). Ranked by demo consequence.
1. AERIAL DOMAIN GAP: detection from altitude is unproven. Training data is ground-level/oblique Roboflow sets; dataset-v2 nadir capture (TODO 10) never happened; recall 0.70 on val with known sheet-water/glare misses; NO in-flight image from this aircraft has ever been through the model. At 15 m a 0.6 m object is ~24 px (below reliable size). The planned demo target is a 2-3 m wet DIRT patch = damp soil, not specular standing water, and may not read as "puddle" to a water-trained model. As scheduled, the first end-to-end detection happens during the take. **RETRACTED SAME DAY (user): the training photos ARE nadir. See USER TRIAGE below; do not repeat the viewpoint-gap claim.**
2. MOTION/VIBRATION BLUR CONFIRMED UNMITIGATED (the user's example is real). camera.py locks focus only; NOTHING in the repo touches exposure/gain, so the B525's auto-exposure picks the shutter and lengthens it in dull monsoon light. Ground sampling at 15 m / 56.2 deg / 1280 px = 12.5 mm/px, so 3 m/s ground speed at a 1/30 s shutter smears ~8 px (arithmetic from measured HFOV, not a measurement); angular vibration on the hard mount (no damping, no gimbal, rolling shutter jello) multiplies it. No sharpness check exists anywhere in the detection path, so blurred frames are silently inferred on.
3. SURVEY SPEED IS UNCOMMANDED ANYWHERE. mission.py goto() sends position targets without speed, mavlink_io has no speed command, and pixhawk_full_setup.param contains NO WPNAV*/ANGLE_MAX lines, so GUIDED legs run at firmware default (Copter default WPNAV_SPEED believed ~10 m/s, FROM MEMORY, UNVERIFIED - read it from the board or a log before relying on any number). Every coverage/blur figure in this file assumed ~3 m/s. At default speed the 9 m along-track footprint at ~0.5-1 s/frame can cross a 2.5 m patch with ~0-1 frames on it, and finding 2's blur scales with speed.
4. THE TREAT STEP OVER REAL WATER IS UNSOLVED END TO END. Settled verdict: TF-Luna never works over still water, descend-beside is the only route. But lateral_offset_n/e_m default 0.0 (mission.py:82) and run_mission.py EXPOSES NO FLAG for them, so the system as launchable descends directly OVER the water, where the dropout logic (mission.py:315-343) aborts; the aborted site latches into detector._fired (8 m radius) and is deliberately never retried, so one-target demo + one abort = zero drops for the whole flight. The wet-dirt demo target sidesteps the physics (damp dirt returns a normal Luna echo) rather than proving it. And even with offsets set, the 2026-08-09 open question stands unanswered: released BESIDE the water, how do the granules reach it?
5. "TREATED" HAS NO PHYSICAL EVIDENCE. Drop success = the autopilot ACCEPTING the gate command (dropper.py:155 "gate cycles the autopilot accepted"); nothing senses granules actually leaving. Flow rate never measured, hopper+salt flow never bench-tested (TODO 3), and Bti granules in monsoon humidity are the bridging/clumping case; a bridged or empty hopper still logs ok:true, paints "treated" on the judged dashboard, and halves predict.py's revisit score for a site that received nothing.
6. TARGETING ERROR STACK IS COMPARABLE TO THE TARGET SIZE. One-shot latch from 15 m: aircraft GPS error at latch time + again during approach (each ~1-2.5 m class, approximate), HFOV error proportional in the offset, AGL from rel_alt-above-HOME (terrain datum error class, old TODO 10f), wp_radius 1.5 m acceptance, and deliberately no re-detection during descent. Total plausible miss is metres against a 2-3 m puddle, and nothing verifies the gate is actually over water at release (the drop event records the AIRCRAFT's position).
7. DOWNWASH AT RELEASE, UNQUANTIFIED. drop_alt 3 m is inside the hexa's own outwash; light granules released there can be scattered off a small target, and the surface ripples. Never tested at any height: the first live granule drop of the entire project is currently scheduled to happen on camera. **RETRACTED SAME DAY (user-settled, do not re-raise): the dropper outlet is nowhere near the wash. See USER TRIAGE.**
8. COMPASS INTERFERENCE UNRESOLVED. Farm logs 43/45: mag field 1038 and 1058 vs max 875 (~19% strong), repeatable across power cycles, plus EKF variance/glitch events. Cause undiagnosed (interference vs stale calibration after the rebuilds). It blocks arming and threatens yaw/EKF mid-flight; currently carried only as a checklist caveat.
9. RC LINK INTERMITTENCY DISMISSED, NOT RESOLVED. Three ABSENT/UNHEALTHY-with-dropout results (2026-08-14/15) were closed with "TX works we already know that" while the TX's on/off state during those runs was never established. If the intermittency is real, an RC failsafe RTLs through the take, and the manual-abort channel - the only human backstop, with avoidance off and the rear unmonitored - is unreliable. **CLOSED SAME DAY (user): the TX WAS OFF during those runs. See USER TRIAGE.**
10. PACKAGING VS ENVIRONMENT (heat, vibration, water). Both measured field failures of this project were CONNECTIONS, not code: the farm camera loss (USB plug/enumeration under flight vibration) and ring channels dying one by one on the hot sealed frame. Hand-spliced dupont/USB connectors plus a single USB hub carrying BOTH the camera and the Pixhawk link are single points of failure under exactly the conditions of use. Separately, the product premise is monsoon while the aircraft has no conformal coat yet (deliberately last), an open hopper, and a bare lens: rain = no-fly, a wet lens blinds the model. And endurance 13 min usable = one small plot per pack. Demo-compatible, but each needs honest framing for judges.

USER TRIAGE OF THE SCAN, 2026-08-17 (user, primary source; whys kept per truth rule 8):
- Finding 1 RETRACTED: the training photos ARE nadir (user knows the merged set; my "ground-level/oblique" over-generalized the 2026-07-25 note that the FUMIGATION sets are ground-robot view). What survives: no image captured by THIS aircraft in flight has been through the model yet (retire cheaply with one pre-take test flight), and the small-target floor (0.6 m at 15 m = 24 px) stands, already handled by the 2-3 m patch.
- Finding 3 STANDS but reclassified EASILY SOLVED, and was misread: "legs" = survey path segments, not landing gear. Mission code sends position targets only, never a speed; transit speed is the autopilot's WPNAV_SPEED, absent from pixhawk_full_setup.param, so firmware default applies (believed ~10 m/s, UNVERIFIED, read from board/log). Fix = user sets WPNAV_SPEED (~300 cm/s class) in the file + push, then verify actual ground speed from the next .bin. NOT added to the file by the assistant (user owns the change).
- Finding 6 DOWNGRADED (user: calibration, easily fixed; correct for the dominant terms): HFOV is now measured, and latch + approach use the SAME receiver minutes apart, so absolute GPS error is largely common-mode and cancels. Residuals only: frame<->telemetry pairing error scales with speed (shrinks with the finding-3 fix), wind position-hold error, wp_radius 1.5 m acceptance.
- Finding 7 RETRACTED, user-settled, do not re-raise: the dropper outlet is nowhere near the rotor wash (hopper at CG, wash at the rotor ring; dense granules, ~0.8 s fall from 3 m).
- Finding 8 DOWNGRADED (user: calibration issue): plan = compass recal outdoors away from metal (already bench plan D.14), then confirm mag field < 875 in the next log. Escalate to interference-hunting ONLY if it recurs after a good recal.
- Finding 9 RESOLVED AND CLOSED (user): the TX WAS OFF during those three runs, answering the 2026-08-14 open question; the ABSENT/UNHEALTHY results were the check working as designed. RC link is reliable per user. The checklist arm gate (RC PASS with TX on) stays as-is.
- Findings 2, 4, 5, 10 UNDISPUTED = the FUNDAMENTAL list: camera blur physics (2), treat-over-real-water design gap incl. unset/unexposed lateral offsets + granules-into-water-from-beside + abort-latches-site-forever (4), no physical dispense confirmation (5; the flow-rate BENCH half is the easy part, TODO 3), packaging-vs-environment class (10).

### 2026-08-17 ~18:55 IST: SiK radio degraded (param download now 10 min and incomplete) - OPEN, no evidence yet
- USER REPORT: full param transfer used to complete in about 1 minute over the SiK; now it takes ~10 minutes and ends with "vehicle failed to send all the params". Which program produced that wording, and on which machine, is NOT yet known (it is not tools/parameters.py's wording; QGC/Mission Planner phrase it that way), so the report is recorded as-is and not interpreted further.
- WHAT THE SYMPTOM DOES SAY: this is throughput/packet-loss, not a dead radio. A dead or mispaired link delivers NOTHING (mismatched NETID/air rate = no connection at all), so 10 minutes of partial params means the link is up and losing or starving packets.
- TWO FAMILIES, both unproven: (a) RF/link quality dropped - antenna loose at either end, TELEM2 connector disturbed during the ESP32 refit, ground dongle moved onto a hub, more distance/obstruction, or a sagging TELEM 5V rail; (b) the link now carries more traffic than it did - the ESP32 ring is alive again with PRX1_TYPE=2, so proximity/rangefinder telemetry may be sharing the same 57600 bps as the param transfer. WHICH stream group carries DISTANCE_SENSOR/OBSTACLE_DISTANCE, and at what SR2_* rate, is NOT VERIFIED (the ArduPilot source tree is outside this repo and the standing rule keeps assistant commands inside it), so (b) is a hypothesis to measure, never a stated cause.
- WHAT THE PUSH FILE DOES NOT EXPLAIN: pixhawk_full_setup.param contains no SR* stream-rate lines at all, and SERIAL2_PROTOCOL=2 / SERIAL2_BAUD=57 are unchanged from every previous push, so the recent param push did not alter the radio's port config or stream rates.
- NOTE ALREADY IN THE TOOL: parameters.py's docstring says push over USB, not the radio ("many small round trips and every retry costs radio time"). If the slow attempt WAS a push over the SiK, slowness is expected behaviour, not a new fault.
- PROPOSED NEXT STEP (offered, not written): a `tools/bench.py link` probe that reads RADIO_STATUS (rssi, remrssi, noise, rxerrors, fixed) and counts messages/bytes per type for 20 s, which separates family (a) from family (b) with evidence. Awaiting the user's answers first.

### 2026-08-17 ~19:10 IST: SiK param failure is QGC-side, on BOTH machines, with the ESP32 alive
- NEW EVIDENCE: the message is QGroundControl's own dialog ("QGroundControl was unable to retrieve the full set of parameters from vehicle 1"), and it happens on the Linux laptop AND the MacBook. Connectors tight, nothing unplugged, ESP32 POWERED during the attempts. Two different ground stations failing identically moves the suspicion off the ground dongle/driver and onto the aircraft side or the link itself.
- HYPOTHESIS A, BANDWIDTH: the ESP32 is a MAVLink node on TELEM1 (SERIAL1_PROTOCOL=2) transmitting at TX_RATE_HZ=10 (config.h:237). OBSTACLE_DISTANCE carries all 72 sector slots and unused sectors are 65535, NOT zero, so MAVLink2 trailing-zero truncation does nothing: 167-byte payload + 12 = ~179 bytes on the wire, ~1.8 kB/s, plus the up-sensor DISTANCE_SENSOR at 10 Hz and a 1 Hz heartbeat, so roughly 2 kB/s. If ArduPilot forwards that broadcast traffic from TELEM1 out to TELEM2, it competes with the param download on a 57600 SiK whose real throughput is well under its serial rate. NOT VERIFIED: ArduPilot's MAVLink routing/forwarding rule has NOT been read from source, because the source tree (/media/sleuther/Stuff/ardupilot-SITL) is outside this repo and the standing rule keeps assistant commands inside it. Permission requested from the user; until then this stays a hypothesis.
- HYPOTHESIS B, QGC CHASING COMPONENT 195: the ESP32 heartbeats as comp 195 (HEARTBEAT_PERIOD_MS 1000). QGC downloads parameters per component, and a component that heartbeats but implements no param protocol can leave QGC retrying and eventually declaring the set incomplete. Also NOT VERIFIED (QGC's per-component param behaviour not read from source). It fits the facts as well as A does: it is new (the ring only came back alive after the reflash), machine-independent, and slow-then-fail rather than dead.
- THE TEST THAT SEPARATES THEM, no code needed: (1) QGC Analyze view -> MAVLink Inspector while connected over the SiK: OBSTACLE_DISTANCE arriving at ~10 Hz means A; comp 195 present but no ring traffic means B. (2) `tools/parameters.py pull` over the SiK from the laptop: mavlink_link targets the AUTOPILOT explicitly (comp 195 is the documented reason it exists), so our tool is immune to B. If our pull completes in about a minute while QGC fails, the RF link is fine and B is the answer. (3) Power the ESP32 down and retry QGC: a fast, complete download proves the ESP32 is involved either way.
- IF A IS CONFIRMED: the ring is display/logging only (AVOID_ENABLE 0), so TX_RATE_HZ 10 -> 2 in config.h costs nothing operationally and cuts the forwarded load by 5x. Requires an ESP32 reflash, so it is a decision for the user, not an automatic fix.

### 2026-08-17 ~19:25 IST: SiK loss is REAL PACKET LOSS, not QGC; ArduPilot 4.7.0 forwarding CONFIRMED FROM SOURCE
- HYPOTHESIS B (QGC chasing comp 195) IS DEAD. `tools/parameters.py pull` over the SiK, which targets the autopilot explicitly and never talks to comp 195, ALSO failed: 730/1035 then 796/1035 parameters, with a DIFFERENT missing set each run. Two runs, two different holes = ordinary packet loss on the link, not a protocol quirk.
- READ THE WARNING LIST CORRECTLY: the "these setup params do NOT exist on this firmware" block at the end of each pull is an ARTEFACT of the incomplete dump. It lists whatever went missing that run (FENCE_TYPE, SERVO9_*, BATT_* ...), it differed between the two runs, and those params demonstrably exist (they were pushed and echoed on 2026-08-15). Nothing is missing from the firmware.
- FORWARDING CONFIRMED (user granted a one-time read of /media/sleuther/Stuff/ardupilot-SITL, Copter 4.7.0): MAVLink_routing::forward() treats a message with no target_system field as broadcast (target_system == -1 -> broadcast_system) and resends it on EVERY channel with a learned route except the source and private channels. OBSTACLE_DISTANCE and DISTANCE_SENSOR have no target fields, so the ESP32's 10 Hz output on TELEM1 IS forwarded out TELEM2 to the SiK whenever a GCS route has been learned there. Traffic estimate: OBSTACLE_DISTANCE fills all 72 slots with 65535 (never 0), so MAVLink2 truncation saves nothing: 167-byte payload + 12 = 179 bytes, ~1.8 kB/s, ~2.2 kB/s with the up sensor and heartbeat.
- THE KNOB, ALSO FROM SOURCE: GCS_MAVLINK::Option::NO_FORWARD = bit 1 of MAVn_OPTIONS (GCS_MAVLink_Parameters.cpp:216 "@Bitmask: 0:Accept unsigned MAVLink2 messages, 1:Don't forward mavlink to/from, 2:Ignore Streamrate, 3:forward mavlink packets that don't pass CRC"). It calls set_channel_private() at INIT only, so it needs a reboot. Private means "sent heartbeats, but no broadcast or forwarded packets" (GCS.h:436) AND excluded from statustext_send_channel_mask (GCS.cpp:275), so a private channel loses STATUSTEXT too. Broadcast messages arriving ON a private channel are still processed locally, so proximity keeps working.
- CHANNEL MAP (setup_uarts + find_serial count MAVLink ports in SERIALn order): MAV1_ = chan0 = SERIAL0 = USB (UNO Q), MAV2_ = chan1 = SERIAL1 = TELEM1 = ESP32, MAV3_ = chan2 = SERIAL2 = TELEM2 = SiK.
- FIX MENU, with the cost of each: (1) do not manage parameters over the radio at all - the file plus a USB push is the supported path and the tool says so; (2) ESP32 TX_RATE_HZ 10 -> 2 in config.h (5x less forwarded traffic, everything keeps working, needs a reflash); (3) MAV3_OPTIONS=2 stops the flood reaching the radio but ALSO costs QGC every STATUSTEXT, including prearm reasons; (4) MAV2_OPTIONS=2 stops the ESP32 being forwarded anywhere, which keeps the dashboard ring (it reads the FC's OWN DISTANCE_SENSOR stream, mavlink_io.py:281/331) but kills the "prx ring N/6" health text that test_everything's ring check parses.
- STILL UNDECIDED, and one physical test settles it: power the ESP32 down and re-run the pull. Full 1035 in about a minute = congestion, fix with (2). Still hundreds missing = RF, and then antennas/placement/radio settings matter and a radio FIRMWARE update would still not be the fix.

### 2026-08-17 ~19:55 IST: tools/esp32_mute.py written (temporary ring mute so the SiK carries QGC alone)
- USER DECISION: full QGC over the SiK is the goal, not a board-side ground station. Wants the ring silenced temporarily now, and a permanent answer after.
- WHY THE RADIO "USED TO WORK": the ring has only recently been a continuous talker. comp 195 first appeared on the bus on 2026-08-14 (TX/RX were swapped at TELEM1 before that, so the ESP32 transmitted nothing), it then degraded to 2-4 live sectors, and the 2026-08-16 firmware added 10 s retries + a 15 s health STATUSTEXT so it now streams all the time. The radio never had to carry a continuous 10 Hz OBSTACLE_DISTANCE before this week. STILL A HYPOTHESIS: proven only when a muted run downloads the full 1035 parameters.
- NEW TOOL `tools/esp32_mute.py` (off | on | status), runs from either machine, auto-picks the port like every other tool:
  * off writes SERIAL1_PROTOCOL -1 and PRX1_TYPE 0; on restores both FROM param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param (never from constants in the script, so restore cannot drift from the project config); status just reports.
  * BOTH PARAMS MOVE TOGETHER on purpose: disabling TELEM1 while PRX1_TYPE stayed 2 would leave a configured proximity sensor with no data, which this project's own record treats as an arming risk.
  * Refuses when ARMED and refuses when the armed state is unknown (it reboots the FC, which is the only way it could hurt anything). Refuses to reboot if either write was not echoed.
  * Reboots via MAV_CMD_PREFLIGHT_REBOOT_SHUTDOWN param1=1; "NO ACK" is expected and is printed as normal, since the board reboots instead of replying. Reconnect re-resolves the port from scratch (USB re-enumerates under a new name after a reboot) and retries 6 times.
  * VERIFIES TWO WAYS: reads both params back after the reboot, then listens 6 s and counts messages from component 195 and OBSTACLE_DISTANCE. Silent = muted. Distinct messages for "off but still talking" (ESP32 is not on TELEM1) and "on but nothing arrives" (ESP32 unpowered).
  * Imports write_param/await_param/load_param_file from parameters.py rather than reimplementing the write-and-echo path, keeping the single-owner rule for parameter writes.
- TESTED WITHOUT HARDWARE (no serial port was opened; the SiK dongle is on the laptop and the standing rule keeps assistant commands off the aircraft): compiles, --help, restore values read as SERIAL1_PROTOCOL 2 / PRX1_TYPE 2 from the file, and 8 stubbed control-flow scenarios all behave: armed refusal, unknown-armed refusal, unwritten-param refusal, lost-across-reboot refusal, both happy paths, and both anomaly messages. NOT yet run against a real flight controller.
- DO NOT FLY MUTED: with PRX1_TYPE 0 there is no proximity sensor at all. Run `on` before any flight.
- PERMANENT CANDIDATES, in preference order: (1) MAV2_OPTIONS = 2 (bit 1 "Don't forward mavlink to/from" on chan1 = SERIAL1 = the ESP32 port, from GCS_MAVLink_Parameters.cpp:216): one parameter, no reflash, the FC still ingests the ring locally (private channels still process broadcast messages) and the dashboard keeps its bins because it reads the FC's OWN DISTANCE_SENSOR stream; COST is the ESP32's "prx ring N/6" STATUSTEXT, which private channels are excluded from forwarding, so test_everything's ring check would need rewiring to the FC's proximity data. (2) ESP32 TX_RATE_HZ 10 -> 2 in config.h: keeps everything working, cuts the forwarded load 5x, needs a reflash. Neither is applied.

### 2026-08-17 ~20:20 IST: working the fundamental-problems list. Two shipped, three need a decision, three are hardware
- SHIPPED 1, AND THE NAME IN THE SCAN WAS WRONG: "set WPNAV_SPEED ~3 m/s" could not have worked. This firmware has NO WPNAV_* parameters; 4.7 renamed the set. The board's own dumps carry WP_SPD, WP_SPD_UP 2.5, WP_SPD_DN 1.5, WP_ACC 2.5, WP_RADIUS_M 2, i.e. metres and m/s, not the old centimetre units. **WP_SPD read 10 on the board**, which settles finding 3's "believed ~10 m/s, UNVERIFIED" from the aircraft's own dump: every coverage, footprint and blur figure in this file assumed 3 m/s and the aircraft has been flying its GUIDED legs at 10. param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param now carries WP_SPD,3 with that history next to it. NOT YET PUSHED, and one thing stays VERIFY: GUIDED position targets are expected to be flown by the waypoint controller (so WP_SPD governs them), but that has not been confirmed on this aircraft - read the achieved ground speed out of the next log before believing it.
- SHIPPED 2: tools/check_log.py gained a COMPASS section, which is the "confirm field under 875 in the log" step. It reports |B| per MAG instance SPLIT BY THROTTLE, and that split is the diagnosis finding 8 never had: over the gate AT REST = stale calibration or something magnetic parked near the compass (recalibration helps), fine at rest but over the gate UNDER THROTTLE = current in nearby wiring (recalibration is wasted, reroute the battery leads). Either failure adds to the exit-1 verdict list. MAG_FIELD_GATE 875 is documented in the source as THIS SITE's number, taken from the aircraft's own "Check mag field (1038, max 875)" message, not as a universal constant.
- TESTED: no .bin log exists in this repo, so the section was exercised against four synthetic message streams (clean, 1038-at-rest, 430-at-rest-1010-flying, no MAG at all). All four print the intended text and exit 0/1/1/0. Not yet run on a real log.
- ALREADY EXISTS, no code needed for "run the model on real in-flight frames": uno_q/spotcheck_onnx.py takes --model and --dir and writes results.jsonl plus annotated images. The pre-take flight just has to produce frames for it.
- SALT FLOW (dose seconds -> grams): blocked on a MEASUREMENT the user has to make, not on code. With grams per second from the bench test, mission.py's dose_s_min/max/default and the area-to-dose mapping can be expressed in grams.
- THREE DECISIONS PUT TO THE USER, nothing written until answered (SCOPE RULE 2): (a) what an aborted descent should do, since detector._fired latches the site at FIRE time (detector.py:113-115) and an abort therefore burns it for the whole flight; (b) how a release over real water should work - beside with an exposed offset flag, or directly overhead at a fixed altitude without the Luna, or wet-dirt only; (c) whether the blur work is a logged sharpness metric, a gate that skips soft frames, or a fixed exposure lock in camera.py.
- NOT SOLVABLE IN CODE, and said plainly rather than left implied: connectors under vibration and heat, the single hub carrying both camera and Pixhawk, no weatherproofing against the monsoon premise, 13 min usable endurance, and the camera's own rolling shutter. Those are build and framing decisions.

### 2026-08-17 ~21:00 IST: water-drop sequence, abort retry, blur metric (user answers to the three decisions)
- USER'S ANSWERS: (a) an abort retries the site ONCE then gives up; (b) the aircraft descends BESIDE the puddle on the Luna, crosses over the centre at that height, drops, crosses back beside so the Luna re-acquires, then leaves; (c) blur handling is my call. ALSO: **wet dirt is trained as a NEGATIVE, the model is meant NOT to detect it**, which kills the "demo on damp soil" fallback: the shoot target is real standing water and the sequence below is the only path to a drop.
- MISSION SEQUENCE REWRITTEN (uno_q/mission.py). New states CROSS and RETURN: DESCEND (beside, on the rangefinder) -> CROSS (translate over the puddle centre) -> DROP -> RETURN (translate back beside) -> CLIMB. The altitude reference over the water is `_drop_rel_alt`, the rel_alt captured at the instant the Luna said drop height was reached BESIDE the water, because that is the last trustworthy AGL; goto() holds it across both translates. Nothing tests the rangefinder in CROSS/DROP/RETURN: a dropout over still water is the expected reading, and treating it as a fault is exactly the abort that ended flights with zero drops. Degenerate cases handled: offsets 0 (or a cross shorter than wp_radius) collapse to the old vertical behaviour, and a missing rel_alt releases beside the water rather than crossing blind.
- ABORT NOW RETRIES (cfg.site_retries = 1). ABORT_CLIMB re-approaches the SAME latched coordinates once before resuming the survey; no detector change was needed because the mission already holds the coordinates, and the detector's _fired latch is what made a retry impossible from the detector side.
- FLAGS: run_mission gained --offset-n (default 3.0) and --offset-e (default 0.0). Direction is a site decision, so it is a flag; finding 4's "run_mission EXPOSES NO FLAG" is closed. cfg.cross_timeout_s 20 s: a failed crossing OUT aborts, a failed crossing BACK just climbs from where it is (the granules are already gone).
- TESTED WITH A KINEMATIC HARNESS (no SITL, which lives outside this repo): fake MavIO with a motion model, a water disc the rangefinder cannot see, and mission.py's clock driven by the harness so wall-clock dwells and timeouts are exercised. Four scenarios pass: beside-3 m crosses and drops 1.40 m from centre at 3.00 m then returns and resumes; offsets 0 shows no CROSS/RETURN; a dropout on the first descent aborts then SUCCEEDS on the retry (2 APPROACHes, 1 drop); a permanently dead rangefinder aborts twice and gives up with 0 drops (which is also what sitl_test's dropout drill asserts).
- BLUR (my call, deliberately conservative): OnnxDetector.infer_rows now measures variance of the Laplacian on the letterboxed image the model actually receives, stores it as last_sharpness, and detect_worker puts it in every payload and logs it with each detection plus every tenth blank frame. IT IS NOT A GATE. A threshold chosen without in-flight numbers would silently blind the aircraft; the pre-take flight now produces the distribution to choose one from. Measured cost 0.6 ms/frame on the laptop against ~500 ms inference; sharp-vs-blurred separation on a synthetic frame was 4 orders of magnitude.
- DASHBOARD: STATE_COLORS gained CROSS and RETURN (amber, like DESCEND/CLIMB) so the new states do not render as unknowns.
- NOTED, NOT CHANGED: rec.latch() still records the DESCENT point, so with a 3 m offset the latch marker on the dashboard sits 3 m from the detection marker. That is honest (it is where the aircraft flies) but it will look like an error to anyone who does not know.
- STILL OPEN from the user's list: salt flow (blocked on a bench measurement, grams per second, before dose seconds can mean grams); the pre-take flight itself (uno_q/spotcheck_onnx.py already runs the model over a folder of frames, no new code); and the packaging/endurance/weatherproofing items, which are build decisions and not code.

### 2026-08-17 ~21:20 IST: FS-i6X transmitter brownout CONFIRMED under load (open 5.18 V, loaded 3.8 V)
- USER MEASURED IT BOTH WAYS: 5.18 V at rest, and under load the meter agrees with the transmitter's own 3.8 V reading. The display was honest; the pack cannot hold voltage. The radio refuses to operate at that level.
- WHAT THE NUMBERS MEAN: 5.18 V across four AA cells is ~1.29 V/cell, so the cells are already largely spent (fresh alkalines read about 1.6 V each, ~6.4 V for the pack). A 1.38 V drop at a transmitter's typical draw (order of 0.1-0.25 A, NOT measured here) implies something in the order of 6-10 ohms in series, where four healthy alkalines plus a clean holder should be well under 1 ohm. Both a depleted pack and a resistive contact produce exactly this, and they are told apart by measurement, not by argument.
- ORDER OF WORK GIVEN TO THE USER: (1) fresh cells first, since the pack is demonstrably depleted; (2) if the sag survives fresh cells, WALK THE VOLTAGE with the radio switched ON, probing from each cell's own terminals, then the holder wires, then the board input, because the fault is wherever the voltage disappears; (3) contacts or holder if the loss is there (clean, re-tension, or bypass the holder), cells if each cell sags on its own; (4) longer term, low-internal-resistance chemistry (NiMH eneloop-class or a 2S pack) INSIDE the input range printed on the radio, which was deliberately not quoted from memory.
- DO NOT lower the low-voltage alarm to get past it: that converts a brownout into an in-flight one.
- LIKELY CLOSES FINDING 9 (RC link intermittency, three ABSENT/UNHEALTHY results on 2026-08-14/15 dismissed with "the TX works"). A transmitter browning out under its own RF load is exactly an intermittent link whose TX state looked fine to the operator. NOT PROVEN: it needs one bench re-test with a good pack, receiver bound, watching test_everything's RC row.
- CORRECTION, SAME DAY: the cells are RECHARGEABLE (NiMH), not alkaline, so the "spent alkaline" reading of the numbers is withdrawn. Re-read for NiMH: 1.29 V/cell at rest is a PART-CHARGED cell (a full one rests around 1.35-1.4 V, nominal 1.2 V), and 0.95 V/cell under load is heavy sag. The dominant NiMH failure in a 4-cell series string is ONE weak cell, which drags the pack while the other three look fine, so the decisive measurement is each cell individually UNDER LOAD, not the pack. Other NiMH-specific suspects given: a charger terminating early (dumb chargers and old timer chargers), self-discharge if the cells are not low-self-discharge types, high cycle count or cheap high-mAh cells (internal resistance rises with both), and FLAT-TOP cells in a holder sprung for button tops, which is a classic high-resistance contact. Replacement rule: swap the whole set, never mix ages or brands in series.
- RESOLVED (user, 2026-08-17): ONE BAD CELL. The per-cell-under-load measurement found it, exactly the series-string failure described above. The transmitter's own 3.8 V reading was correct throughout and nothing is wrong with the radio.
- CONSEQUENCE FOR FINDING 9, still UNPROVEN but now cheap to settle: a transmitter browning out on one weak cell is a fully sufficient explanation for the three RC ABSENT/UNHEALTHY results of 2026-08-14/15, whose TX state nobody had established. The confirmation is one bench check with the good pack in: receiver bound, TX on, watch the RC row in test_everything. Until that runs, finding 9 stays open rather than being closed by a plausible story.

### 2026-08-18 ~13:30 IST: "is 3 m/s too fast for an 80 cm warning?" - the ring cannot protect ANY survey speed, and does not try
- USER CONCERN: at 3 m/s an 80 cm warning gives 0.25 s to react. The instinct is right but the framing is off in both directions:
- (1) NOTHING REACTS AT ALL, at any speed: AVOID_ENABLE=0 (decided 2026-08-14 when the ring degraded; "if it doesn't work, disable"). The ring is display/logging only. No 80 cm threshold exists in the system either; the ring's trusted band is 5-200 cm (config.h RANGE_MIN/MAX_CM) and AVOID_MARGIN=1 m is inert while avoidance is off.
- (2) EVEN IF AVOIDANCE WERE ON, the arithmetic kills 3 m/s independently: braking from 3 m/s at AVOID_ACCEL_MAX=3 m/s2 needs v2/2a = 1.5 m, plus ~0.1 s sensor latency (10 Hz ring) = 0.3 m travelled first, total ~1.8 m against a 2 m sensor ceiling. The ring could only ever serve ~1.5-2 m/s (0.67 m brake + latency from 2 m/s). APPROXIMATE: kinematics from our own params, not a bench measurement.
- (3) WHAT ACTUALLY PROTECTS THE FLIGHT AT 3 m/s: the survey runs at 15 m AGL, above the obstacle field; the hand-drawn fence bounds the plot; the pilot holds kill/RTL. The slow phases are already slow: descent 0.5 m/s, the cross legs are ~3 m gotos that never reach cruise.
- (4) 3 m/s is not an increase: the board has been flying GUIDED legs at WP_SPD=10 all along; 3 is the reduction (chosen for blur + frames-on-target, recorded 2026-08-17).
- IF THE RING EVER STEERS AGAIN (AVOID_ENABLE 7): drop WP_SPD to 2 or below at the same time, or the avoidance is cosmetic. Recorded so the two params never move independently.

### 2026-08-18 ~16:55 IST: CORRECTION (user): the ring IS to steer again - AVOID_ENABLE back to 7 in the file
- USER, primary source: "we already agreed that the ring would be enabled, since we reflashed and fixed the wiring." That agreement was never written into this file, which is why the 2026-08-17 entries and this morning's 13:30 entry kept stating AVOID_ENABLE=0 as current policy. The 08-14 disable was CONDITIONAL on the ring degrading, and its cause is fixed: the sector deaths were the old firmware latching failed channels at boot with no retry (diagnosed 2026-08-15), the 2026-08-16 reflash retries DOWN channels every 10 s, and the TELEM1 wiring has been redone. This entry supersedes those statements; per append-only rule the old entries stand as written.
- FILE: param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param AVOID_ENABLE 0 -> 7 with the full history in the comment; the stale "AVOID_ENABLE stays 0" note in the FENCE block rewritten (bit 1 = fence avoidance, so the aircraft now also slows to a stop at the boundary instead of only breach-and-return; new behaviour, untested in the air). File parses to 73 params. Lands with the SAME pending night-before push (SERIAL5 -1, FENCE_TYPE 7, WP_SPD 3, now AVOID_ENABLE 7); no reboot needed for AVOID_ENABLE itself.
- SPEED COUPLING RESOLVED, not reopened: the 13:30 rule said AVOID_ENABLE 7 and WP_SPD move together. Resolution recorded in the file: cruise at WP_SPD 3 is at 15 m AGL above the obstacle field, so the ring's inability to stop 3 m/s (needs ~1.8 m of its ~2 m ceiling) protects nothing that needs protecting; the ring serves the slow low phases (descent 0.5 m/s, short cross legs). Ring-protected CRUISE would need WP_SPD <= 2; that trade is the user's if ever wanted.
- PRECONDITION, on the checklist not in code: Test everything ring row 5-6/6 with the drone assembled before the first avoidance flight. Dead sectors stream 65535 = no-data, which ArduPilot ignores, so a degraded ring steers less, never wrongly.

### 2026-08-18: tools/ring_channels.py -- name the dead ring channels without the ESP32's USB
- PROBLEM: "prx ring 4/6" says HOW MANY are alive, never WHICH. test_everything's "bearing bins that see an object" cannot fill the gap either, because a healthy channel with nothing in front of it does not appear in that list. The only thing that names channels is the ESP32 debug log over USB, and the ESP32's USB is not reachable (2026-08-18: `ls /dev/serial/by-id/` on the board shows ONLY the Pixhawk; user could not find a way to connect the ESP32 to the UNO Q).
- THE ANSWER IS ALREADY ON THE WIRE, which is why no new cabling is needed: per config.h, a failed channel reports SENSOR_MM_ERROR -> SECTOR_NO_DATA -> **65535** ("unknown"), while a channel that is alive with nothing in range sends RANGE_MAX_CM+1 = 201 cm, a REAL number. So 65535 = DEAD and anything else = ALIVE, per sector, in the OBSTACLE_DISTANCE the Pixhawk is already forwarding. Identity map channel N -> sector N, bearing = N*60 deg clockwise from the nose (SENSOR_ANGLE_OFFSET_DEG 0 on this airframe).
- TOOL: read-only, sends nothing, arms nothing. Samples over --seconds (default 10) rather than reading one message BECAUSE THE FAULT IS INTERMITTENT: it reports DEAD (never reported), FLAKY (<95% of samples) and alive, so a channel that flickers is caught as well as one that is flat dead. Prints the closest object each live channel saw.
- DECODE VERIFIED on synthetic frames 2026-08-18 (dead / flaky / alive / closest-cm all correct). NOT yet run against the aircraft.
- DIRECTION IT POINTS, straight from config.h and not to be re-litigated: this fault is loose contact / sagging 3V3, NOT bus speed. Do not lower I2C_CLOCK_HZ or raise TCA_SETTLE_US. Meter 3V3 at the failing sensor's own VIN pin under load, wiggle its connector, and remember the 2026-08-14 precedent where an UNPOWERED ch6 was also breaking ch1. If power is clean, swap the suspect sensor onto a known-good mux channel: fault follows the sensor = bad chip; fault stays on the channel = mux or wiring.

### 2026-08-18: THE BOARD'S GIT REPO CORRUPTED ITSELF -- cause, fix, and the rule that follows
- SYMPTOM: `git pull` on the board failed with `object file .git/objects/.. is empty`, then `git status` failed with `fatal: bad object HEAD`, and `git fsck` listed MANY empty objects (017ed9db, 0c3f08b, 11119e91, 1651a780, 31178aca, 4e9a9121, 5487e8fb, ...). Zero-byte objects across the store with a broken HEAD is structural damage, NOT a merge problem and NOT repairable by deleting a few objects.
- CAUSE (high confidence, not proven): the aircraft was cold power-cycled repeatedly during the ring debugging, and an abrupt power cut loses buffered filesystem writes. Git had objects in flight. Same mechanism that would corrupt a half-written log or mission JSONL.
- FIX ADOPTED: move the damaged repo aside (do NOT delete it: it is the only copy of any uncommitted local edit, and with a bad HEAD git cannot tell us what those were), clone fresh, then diff against the saved copy at leisure. Nothing the board needs lives inside the repo -- ~/logs, ~/monsoonready_data and ~/venv are all outside it, and models/best.onnx is tracked so it returns with the clone.
- **RULE THAT FOLLOWS: STOP CUTTING POWER TO A RUNNING BOARD.** The Linux side is a full Debian system with a writeback cache, not a microcontroller. Every abrupt power-off risks whatever was being written: the git store this time, a flight log or a mission JSONL next time. Shut it down cleanly before pulling power. CAVEAT, unresolved: the board's sudo password is lost (recorded earlier), so `sudo poweroff` may not be available; whether an unprivileged `systemctl poweroff` works here is UNTESTED. Establish a working clean-shutdown command and put it in the field checklist.
- COST THIS TIME: only time. The remote had every commit, so nothing was lost.

### 2026-08-18 RING DIAGNOSED PROPERLY: it is 1/6 usable, not 4/6 (tools/ring_channels.py, first hardware run)
- **ch1 (60 deg) and ch2 (120 deg) are DEAD**, 0% of 150 messages, and they are ADJACENT. Two neighbouring channels failing together is not two independent chip failures; it points at something they SHARE (a common wiring run, a branch of the 3V3 distribution, adjacent mux pins). ch1 also has previous: config.h records that on 2026-08-14 reconnecting an unpowered ch6 also fixed ch1, so ch1 has already once fallen over because of a power fault elsewhere on the shared net.
- **ch3 (180), ch4 (240), ch5 (300) are ALIVE BUT BLIND.** They report 5 cm, 5 cm, 8 cm at 100% and DID NOT CHANGE when the aircraft was moved to open space, while ch0 changed 39 cm -> clear in the same pair of runs. ch0 changing is the control: it proves sensor, transport and tool all track the real world, so an unchanging reading on the other three means the object is travelling with the aircraft. RANGE_MIN_CM is 5, so those two are pegged at the sensor floor.
- CONSEQUENCE: the "prx ring 4/6 up:ok" health line is MISLEADING as a readiness signal. It counts channels that INITIALISED, not channels that can see anything useful. Only ch0 is proven to observe the world. Do not read 4/6 as "two thirds of a working ring".
- THIS IS ALSO THE ARMING BLOCKER: `PreArm: Proximity 180 deg, 0.07m (want > 0.6m)` is ch3 reporting the same 5 cm. Fixing ch1/ch2 would not clear it.
- NOT YET DISAMBIGUATED, and it decides the fix: are ch3/4/5 seeing THE AIRFRAME (legs/arms/payload -> mounting fault, ring unusable until moved) or THE GROUND (the aircraft was moved laterally but stayed on the floor, so ground distance never changed)? Decisive test = lift the aircraft clear of the floor with nothing within ~2 m in any direction, including below, and re-run. Ground-seeing would clear in flight anyway and ArduPilot has PRX_IGN_GND / PRX_ALT_MIN (default 1.0 m) designed for exactly it (named in esp32_obstacle_avoidance/README.md; VERIFY against the ArduPilot 4.7 docs before setting). Airframe-seeing needs the sensors physically moved and matches the standing mount rule that the 25 deg FOV must not see legs or props.
- SUSPICIOUS OVERLAP worth checking during the fix: config.h records that on 2026-08-06 the three rear sensors (then called channels 2/3/4 = bearings 120/180/240) were REMOVED while chasing the bus fault. Every channel except ch0 is now faulty in one way or the other, which is more consistent with ONE BAD REFIT of the ring than with five separate faults.
- TOOL STATUS: tools/ring_channels.py is now HARDWARE-VERIFIED (150 messages in 20 s, named the dead channels first run, and its alive/clear/closest-cm readout is what exposed the blind-sensor finding that the 4/6 health line hid).

### 2026-08-18 (Raghav, primary source): ch2's sensor is FRIED and disconnected -- which re-points the ch1 diagnosis
- **ch2 IS NOT A FAULT TO CHASE.** Its VL53L0X is dead and the channel is disconnected. Its DEAD line in ring_channels.py is expected, not evidence.
- **BUT IT IS THE PRIME SUSPECT FOR ch1.** config.h's 2026-08-14 entry: an unpowered ch6 was disturbing the shared 3V3 net and "reconnecting it also fixed ch1". Same victim channel, same mechanism, and ch2 is ch1's immediate neighbour. The earlier "ch1+ch2 adjacent so they share a cause" reading was right about the sharing and wrong about the direction: ch2 is likely CAUSING ch1, not co-failing with it.

Still-connected means it can load or clamp the shared rail, which would explain ch1. Genuinely removed means ch1 is an independent fault. Do not proceed on ch1 until this is known.

That array exists for exactly this case and its own comment says so.

The upward sensor is not in OBSTACLE_DISTANCE at all -- it travels as DISTANCE_SENSOR orientation 24 (RNGFND2 to the Pixhawk), while orientation 25 on the same message is the downward TF-Luna and is filtered out. Filter verified on synthetic streams 2026-08-18. Its absence from the first version was a tool gap, not a hardware finding.

### 2026-08-18 CORRECTION (Raghav, primary source): the "ch2 is killing ch1" theory is WRONG, and so is the reasoning behind it
- **WIRING TOPOLOGY, from the user:** ch2 is DISCONNECTED (fried sensor), so it is not on any rail and can load nothing. All REMAINING lidars share ONE common 3V3 and GND. The 2026-08-14 precedent in config.h was about ch6 and ch1 specifically, not about ring neighbours in general.
- **THE ERROR, named so it is not repeated: I treated adjacency in BEARING (60 deg next to 120 deg) as adjacency in WIRING.** Ring position says nothing about electrical topology. Delete the "ch1+ch2 share a cause" reading from the 2026-08-18 entries above; it was built on that confusion.
- **WHAT THE CORRECTED TOPOLOGY PROVES, and it is stronger than the theory it replaces: THE SHARED RAIL IS EXONERATED.** ch0, ch3, ch4, ch5 and ch6 all report on that same common 3V3/GND. A sagging or disturbed shared supply cannot kill only ch1 while five others on it work. So ch1 is a LOCAL fault, confined to exactly three candidates: its own VL53L0X, its own 4-wire bundle, or mux channel SD1/SC1.
- **DECISIVE TEST (uses the free slot ch2 leaves behind): move ch1's sensor onto ch2's now-unused mux channel.** Works there => the sensor is good and the fault is mux channel 1 or its wiring. Stays dead => the sensor is bad and needs replacing, same as ch2's. One move, one re-run of ring_channels.py, and the three candidates collapse to one.
- CONSEQUENCE FOR PARTS: at least one replacement VL53L0X is already needed for ch2. If the swap shows ch1's sensor is also bad, that is two.
- STILL OUTSTANDING, unchanged: ch3/4/5 read 6/5/8 cm across THREE runs in different positions while ch0 tracked the world each time (39 -> clear -> 125 cm). The lift test (aircraft clear of the floor, nothing within ~2 m in any direction INCLUDING BELOW) has not been done and is what separates "seeing the airframe" from "seeing the ground".

### 2026-08-18: ring_channels.py advice now FOLLOWS FROM ITS OWN EVIDENCE instead of reciting history
- The tool used to print the config.h shared-3V3 story after every run. On this aircraft that advice is WRONG (topology corrected above: ch2 is off the rail; the common rail is exonerated by the five channels that work on it), and a tool that hands the operator a disproven theory is worse than one that says nothing.
- NEW LOGIC: the live channels are the control experiment. They sit on the SAME common 3V3/GND and the SAME mux as the dead ones, so if any of them report, the rail and the bus cannot be the explanation, and the tool says so and sends the user straight to the swap test. Only if NOTHING reports does it point at the ESP32 / shared rail / mux. Both branches verified 2026-08-18.
- WHY THIS IS WORTH THE CHANGE: this project has now twice chased a shared-supply theory that its own data already ruled out (2026-08-14 for real, 2026-08-18 by my error). Encoding the inference in the tool is how that stops happening.
- OPEN, ASKED, UNANSWERED: whether the 4th run (149 msgs, ch1+ch2 still dead) was taken BEFORE or AFTER swapping ch1's sensor onto ch2's mux channel. If after, the fault followed the sensor => ch1's VL53L0X is bad and TWO replacements are needed. If before, the swap test is still outstanding. Do not record a verdict on ch1 until this is answered.

### 2026-08-18: the ch1 swap test was RUN but its result is AMBIGUOUS -- bad control, my error
- WHAT HAPPENED: user moved ch1's sensor onto ch2's mux channel and re-ran. ch1 (now empty) DEAD as expected; ch2's channel, now carrying ch1's sensor, ALSO DEAD.
- WHY THAT PROVES NOTHING: **ch2's mux channel is not a known-good control.** It has only ever been tested with a fried sensor on it, so it has never once been shown to pass data. Moving a suspect sensor onto an unverified channel makes "both dead" consistent with (a) ch1's sensor bad, (b) ch2's mux channel bad, or (c) both. MY INSTRUCTION NAMED THE WRONG SLOT: I picked ch2's because it was free and convenient, when the test requires a channel PROVEN to work. The tool's own text says "a mux channel known to work"; the chat advice contradicted it.
- **CORRECT TEST, fully decisive in one move: two-way swap ch1's sensor with ch0's.** ch0 is the only channel proven to track the world (39 -> clear -> 125 -> 11 cm across four runs). Then: ch1's sensor reports on ch0's channel => sensor good, ch1's CHANNEL is bad. ch0's sensor goes dead on ch1's channel => confirms ch1's channel is bad. ch1's sensor dead on ch0's channel WHILE ch0's sensor still works on ch1's channel => ch1's SENSOR is bad. Reading both halves is what makes it decisive rather than suggestive.
- NOTE FOR PART ORDERING: ch2's sensor is confirmed fried (user). Whether a SECOND replacement is needed is exactly what the corrected test answers; do not order on the ambiguous result.
- LESSON, general: a swap test is only as good as its control. Swapping onto an untested channel is not a bisection, it just adds an unknown.

### 2026-08-18 ~18:20 IST: FENCE MYSTERY SOLVED - the polygon bit gates the READ-BACK, and 73/73 params are now on the board

Same read_back code, same link, same fence: the only thing that changed was the parameter.
- FINDING, OBSERVED NOT SOURCE-VERIFIED: with FENCE_TYPE's polygon bit (4) CLEAR, ArduPilot 4.7 serves item count 0 for MAV_MISSION_TYPE_FENCE even though the polygon is in storage, and it still ACCEPTS an upload. So a fence can be uploaded, acked, stored, and read back as empty, purely because a parameter bit is off. That is exactly the "sent N but holds 0" text. It also means the fence pushed 2 DAYS AGO was never lost: it survived every power cycle since, and the read-back was lying, not the storage.
- CONSEQUENCE FOR THE CHECKLIST: parameters BEFORE fence, always. A red fence row on a board with stale params says nothing about the fence.

REBOOT STILL OWED before SERIAL1/2/4/5, RNGFND1/2, PRX1_TYPE and BATT_MONITOR mean anything.
- REBOOT PATH IS A GAP: nothing in this repo reboots the flight controller on its own (esp32_mute does it only as part of a param change), and cold-cutting power is what corrupted the board's git store. Until a clean path exists, the FC reboot must be done with the UNO Q shut down cleanly first, then a pack power cycle.

NOT DIAGNOSED, and the honest position is that nothing in the pushed set should have this effect: SERIAL1_PROTOCOL 2 / SERIAL1_BAUD 115 are what the ESP32 needs, PRX1_TYPE 2 only instantiates the FC's proximity driver, and forwarding of a broadcast message to the USB link does not depend on either. The two changes that coincide are the parameter push and a full power cycle, and a power cycle is exactly what has silenced this ESP32 before.
- DECISIVE NEXT STEP (given to the user): `tools/bench.py nodes --seconds 20`. comp 195 present = the ESP32 is alive and the fault is downstream of it; comp 195 absent = the ESP32 is unpowered, not booted, or its TELEM1 wiring moved. Do not theorise further until that one line is known.
- USER CAUGHT A TOOL BUG: ring_channels.py's silence message said "its TELEM2 link is down". TELEM2 is the SiK radio; the ESP32 is on TELEM1. Wrong port in the one message a silent ring makes you read. Fixed, and the message now carries the bench-nodes bisection above.
- TOOL UPGRADE (user's request): the readout showed only the CLOSEST value, which cannot separate a sensor pinned at a fixed distance from one tracking the world. It now prints the full MIN-MAX RANGE per channel with the SPAN, and says NEVER CHANGED explicitly when min == max (which is what ch3/4/5 would have shown on the cluttered bench). "Clear" sentinel readings are counted separately so a real object cannot hide inside them. Verified on a synthetic stream: 39-115 cm SPAN 76 (tracking), 5-5 NEVER CHANGED (stuck), clear-throughout, DEAD, FLAKY 50%, plus the up sensor.
- USER SUSPECTS THE CHANNEL-TO-BEARING MAP MAY BE WRONG. config.h has SECTOR_FOR_CHANNEL = identity {0..5} and SENSOR_ANGLE_OFFSET_DEG 0, so every bearing this tool prints is a FIRMWARE CLAIM, not a measurement. The tool now says so and gives the one-object test: put a single object ~50 cm from ONE sensor, re-run, see which channel moves. If the wrong channel moves, SECTOR_FOR_CHANNEL is the fix (a firmware edit, so it needs the ESP32 the user does not want to reopen; a remap is also possible on our side if it ever becomes necessary).

Tonight's 73/73 parameter push, every line of it write-and-echoed, is itself proof of the board -> Pixhawk TX direction; nothing further is needed. bench.py's node labels were still calling comp 195 "parked, may be unplugged" and comp 191 a TODO-7 D0/D1 test that no longer exists; both corrected.
- THE EARLIER SILENCE IS UNEXPLAINED, and one candidate is worth testing because it predicts exactly what happened: ArduPilot forwards a broadcast message only to channels where it has LEARNED A ROUTE (MAVLink_routing::forward, read 2026-08-17). A Pixhawk reboot empties that table, and a listen-only tool never teaches it one. So immediately after an FC reboot, ring_channels.py can see nothing until some process on the board TRANSMITS. TEST IF IT RECURS: fresh FC reboot -> ring_channels (silent?) -> fence.py read or test_everything, which transmit -> ring_channels again. If the second run sees the ring, routing is the answer and the tool should send a heartbeat of its own. NOT PROVEN, do not state it as the cause.

### 2026-08-18 ~19:30 IST: dry run is BOARD-side; salt flow reads 0 g; coat ordering
- WHERE run_mission RUNS: the UNO Q, always. It needs the camera, the ONNX model and the Pixhawk USB, all of which are on the board; the laptop has none of them. So the user's "if laptop, add it to the dashboard" condition does not trigger. NOTE: dashboard.py's /api/start ALREADY accepts a `dry_run` flag in the POST body (dashboard.py:428) and the UI never sends it, so there is a working backend with no button. Offered; not built unasked.
- SALT FLOW READS 0 g EVERY TIME. Two classes, and they are told apart by LOOKING, not by more runs: (a) MEASUREMENT RESOLUTION - a kitchen scale resolves 1 g, and at a plausible 0.5-1 g/s a 0.5-2 s dose delivers well under its smallest division, so 0 is what it must print; (b) NOTHING IS COMING OUT - bridged or empty hopper, or the gate not actually opening (finding 5's exact risk: a bridged hopper still logs ok:true and paints "treated"). FIX FOR (a): much longer dwells, `--dwells 5 10 20`, which puts 5-20 g on the scale and still yields g/s plus the tool's straight-line fit whose INTERCEPT exposes the gate's opening lag. flow_test.py asks for grams after EVERY cycle, so accumulating many short doses into one weighing is not something the tool supports; long dwells are the supported path.
- COAT ORDERING GIVEN: electrical checks FIRST (Test everything + the bench dry run), coat after, because coating a connector you then have to reseat means cutting it off. If starting early, coat only what will not be unplugged: ESCs, PDB, buck modules, splices. Leave connectors, the ESP32 carrier and anything the tests might implicate until they pass. Cure time per the product label.
