### 2026-08-20 19:43 DRY RUN RAN ON THE REAL AIRCRAFT (first time ever) - it passed, and it exposed a hard ARMING BLOCKER
- WHAT IT PROVED, and this was the biggest open software risk in the project: run_mission started, computed its footprint (16.0 x 9.0 m at 15 m from the measured 56.2 deg HFOV), spawned the detect worker, got a 1280x720 frame through the worker (preflight ok, seq 1), polled 30 s, ended cleanly and stopped the worker. Camera, model path, worker IPC, MavIO, boardlog and the shutdown path are all real now, not SITL-only.
- 0 detections is CORRECT indoors with no water in frame; it is not a model failure.
- PREARM LIST FROM THE RUN, triaged: "RC not found" = TX off (bench), "Hardware safety switch" = button not pressed, "AHRS: waiting for home" and "Fence requires position" = no GPS indoors. All four clear outdoors with the TX on and the switch pressed.
- **THE TWO THAT DO NOT CLEAR BY GOING OUTSIDE, AND THEY BLOCK ARMING: "PreArm: PRX1: No Data" and "PreArm: Rangefinder 2: No Data".** The ring IS streaming (ring_channels saw 150 OBSTACLE_DISTANCE in 20 s and the ESP32's own health line says 4/6 up:ok in this very log), so "No Data" is not silence on the wire.
- HYPOTHESIS, from OUR OWN FIRMWARE and NOT yet verified against ArduPilot source: config.h sets RANGE_MIN_CM 5 / RANGE_MAX_CM 200, and classifyCm() returns RANGE_MAX_CM + 1 = 201 for "clear". mavlink_proximity.cpp declares min_distance 5 and max_distance 200 in BOTH messages and its own comment says the 201 "lies outside [min,max], so ArduPilot treats it as nothing overhead". If ArduPilot instead treats every out-of-range reading as NO READING, then a ring with nothing within 2 m has no valid sectors at all, which is exactly "PRX1: No Data" - and the same 201 explains "Rangefinder 2: No Data" for the up sensor, which read 201 throughout. INDOORS THAT IS ODD (walls were within 2 m), so this is a hypothesis with a hole in it and must not be treated as the answer.
- **DECISIVE 10-SECOND BENCH TEST, given to the user: hold something ~50 cm in front of ONE live ring sensor and press Check arming. If PRX1 goes quiet, the driver only counts in-range returns and an all-clear field reads as no data.** Whatever the result, it decides tomorrow: either the ring must see something at arming, or PRX1_TYPE has to go to 0 for the flight (which also disables the avoidance that was just re-enabled).
- FALLBACK IF UNRESOLVED AT THE FIELD: PRX1_TYPE 0 arms the aircraft and loses avoidance; AVOID_ENABLE 7 without PRX1 does nothing. That is a param push away and is the safe way to still fly tomorrow.

### 2026-08-20 20:05 IST: per-sensor lidar check (user request), and route generation now FILLS THE FENCE
- **tools/ring_channels.py gained a --sensor mode** (user: "a simple CLI script to check each individual lidar sensor, reliability, range"). The survey mode is unchanged and still answers "which channels are alive"; --sensor answers the different question "is THIS sensor any good", which a survey CANNOT answer because it has no ground truth: a sensor stuck at a plausible 80 cm passes a survey. Usage: `--sensor 3 --truth 50` (one channel against a tape-measured target), `--sensor all --truth 50` (prompts you to move the target from channel to channel), `--sensor up` for the RNGFND2 one.
- WHAT IT REPORTS PER SENSOR: samples reported vs sent (with the LONGEST GAP, because 5% dropped evenly is a different fault from vanishing for a second), valid rate in Hz, in-range vs "clear" counts, min/max/mean/median, jitter as standard deviation, and against --truth the bias in cm and % plus the worst single reading. Verdicts in precedence order: NO DATA / DEAD / FLAKY / MISSING TARGET / clear / NOISY / BIASED / ok.
- GATES ARE OURS, NOT DATASHEET NUMBERS, and say so in the source: 5% dropout, 5 cm jitter, bias over max(5 cm, 10%), 90% detection with a target present. Reasoning recorded in the file: at 2 m/s the aircraft moves 20 cm per 100 ms sample, so 5% dropped still gives a reading every ~20 cm. If a number ever has to be defended, read the VL53L0X datasheet; I did not verify these against it.
- IT ALSO SEPARATES LINK LOSS FROM SENSOR LOSS: it prints the OBSTACLE_DISTANCE message rate against config.h's TX_RATE_HZ 10, because a slow link hits every channel equally and is not a sensor fault. And with --truth it lists which OTHER channels moved, which is the one-object wiring-order check (SECTOR_FOR_CHANNEL) done as a side effect.
- The up sensor is reported without assuming anything about how a FAILED reading is encoded in its DISTANCE_SENSOR: that encoding is unverified, and the tool prints the caveat rather than guessing.
- TESTED OFFLINE against synthetic OBSTACLE_DISTANCE (healthy / dead / flaky / clear-only / noisy / biased): all six verdicts correct, survey output unchanged.
- **make_waypoints.build_coverage(): the generated route now conforms to the geofence** (user: rows must span the fence interior; keep-out 4 m; direction as a field; start point chosen at generation time). build_serpentine (fixed rows x length from wherever the aircraft stands) is kept for the CLI and the no-fence case; it could hang the box outside the fence and needed a GPS fix, which is why a route could not be planned at home.
- HOW THE KEEP-OUT IS ENFORCED, and its honest limit: each scan line is sampled every 0.5 m, a sample survives only if it is inside the polygon AND at least inset_m from every edge, and the row is the longest unbroken run of survivors. Exact for any polygon shape to within the step, with no polygon-offset maths to get wrong. The "longest run" rule means a fence pinched into two lobes keeps only the bigger lobe on that line: coverage is incomplete, never unsafe.
- DEFAULT DIRECTION is along the fence's LONGEST EDGE (fewest turns for a field-shaped polygon); the direction field overrides it. Default start (no click) is the southwest-most row end, so the same fence always generates the same route.
- NO LINK AND NO GPS for the fence path, which is the point: the route can be planned at home tonight. The old from-aircraft path still needs both and is used only when no fence is saved.
- UI (UI_VER 2026-08-20a): with a fence drawn, rows/row-length inputs are HIDDEN (the fence decides them), and keep-out + "Set start point" appear. Set start point steals the NEXT map click for the start marker (a dashed ring, deliberately not a waypoint marker) instead of adding a waypoint.
- BROWSER-VERIFIED against a local replica of the dashboard with a 6-corner fence: Generate -> 22 waypoints, 11 rows along 158.7 deg (the longest edge), 4 m clear, 616 m of path; clicking a start point near the opposite corner and regenerating made waypoint 1 the closest waypoint to that click (3.8 m, the minimum over all 22); direction 90 -> 13 east-west rows; keep-out 40 m -> refused in red with "leaves nothing inside this fence" and the previous route left untouched. Server log lines match, appended to ~/logs/dashboard.log.
- OFFLINE-TESTED the generator itself on a rectangle, a hexagon and a concave U: every waypoint inside the fence and >= the keep-out from every edge (worst 4.00 m for a 4 m ask), all four start corners honoured, explicit heading respected, and both refusal paths (inset too big, fewer than 3 corners).
- STILL OPEN from this thread: reordering an existing route (insert between / drag to renumber) is NOT implemented; and the PRX1/Rangefinder-2 arming blocker above is unchanged.

### 2026-08-20 20:40 IST: FIRST PER-SENSOR BENCH RUN (user, board) - and it caught a bug in the tool I had just shipped
- MY BUG, found by his run: the up sensor's samples were fed to the distance statistics WITHOUT the clear-sentinel filter, because I had said its encoding was unverified and assumed nothing. Its 201s (RANGE_MAX_CM + 1) were averaged in as distances, so a sensor alternating between a real ~54 cm and "clear" was reported as "mean 97.5 cm, jitter 68.1 cm, NOISY". That is an invented fault. FIXED: >200 cm is clear for the up sensor too, and the run's own evidence (values of exactly 201, median 54) is what verifies the encoding. A regression case pins it.
- ALSO ADDED because his numbers were ambiguous without it: clear samples are now counted as RUNS ("31 clear in 24 runs, longest 3 in a row"). Scattered single misses = individual reads returning no-target (weak return / ambient light / reflectance); bursts = the object or the sensor dropping out for a stretch. Different faults, same percentage.
- RESULTS, target at 50 cm +-5, untouched during each 10 s run except for the up sensor (user's own caveat, so the up figures are NOT a clean verdict):
  - ch0 (0 deg): 100% reported, 37-52 cm, sd 5.7, bias -3.3. NOISY by our 5 cm gate. Never missed the target.
  - ch1: skipped, never wired (no space). Unchanged.

### 2026-08-20 23:20 IST: concave fences are now covered IN FULL, with every connecting leg proved inside the keep-out
- WHAT CHANGED in build_coverage: a scan line now yields EVERY piece of interior it crosses, not just the longest, and the route is built by NEAREST-REACHABLE traversal instead of a fixed line-by-line sweep. Before flying from one piece to the next, the straight leg is sampled the same way the rows are: if any point on it is outside the keep-out, that piece is not entered. Unreachable pieces are counted in info['dropped'] and SAID OUT LOUD in the dashboard note; they are never silently omitted and never flown through a notch.
- WHY GREEDY AND NOT A SWEEP: on a convex fence they are the same route (after a row, the nearest unflown end IS the next row's near end). They differ only where the fence is concave, and there the sweep is wrong in both directions - it flies through the notch or it abandons ground it could reach by going round.
- MULTI-START, added after the first version paid a 64 m dead leg: with no start click there is no operator intent to honour, so every row end is tried as a start and the route with the fewest dropped pieces, then the shortest path, wins. leg_ok is memoised, so all 26 starts cost 0.27 s for the whole test suite. A clicked start is still obeyed exactly, one greedy run from that point.
- ON THE USER'S REAL FENCE (4 m keep-out, 5 m spacing, no click): 13 pieces on 12 lines, **0 dropped**, 476 m, 4.0 min at 2 m/s. The old rule gave 12 rows, 467 m, a gap below the notch and two doglegs of 18.7 m and 12.1 m. So full coverage now costs 9 m of extra flying. Route starts at the southern tip and sweeps north; the one wrinkle left is that the stranded west piece at 15.1 m north is collected after the row above it (about 15 m of detour), which is inherent to greedy and not worth a solver.
- DEEP-U TEST (a fence pinched into two arms joined only by a spine): 13 pieces, 0 dropped. The traversal sweeps one arm, crosses the spine at the top and comes back down the other, and the leg check proves it never crosses the notch. My first version dropped 6 pieces here and I predicted in the test that it must; the multi-start fixed it, and the assertion now demands full coverage.
- TEST SUITE (scratchpad t_cov.py) now checks EVERY LEG, not just the waypoints: rectangle, hexagon, deep U, the user's real fence at 4/3/2 m keep-out, plus both refusal paths. Worst clearance measured 4.00-4.01 m for a 4 m ask.
- BROWSER-VERIFIED on the local replica loaded with the board's actual fence.json: 26 waypoints, 13 rows along 270.7 deg, 4 m clear, 476 m, no dropped-piece warning. UI_VER 2026-08-20b.

### 2026-08-20 23:40 IST: MY 201-IS-CLEAR HYPOTHESIS IS DEAD. The arming refusal is the ESP32 STREAM STOPPING, and the source proves it
- USER'S THREE PREARM RUNS, and they settle it: object in front of ch0 -> "Arm: Proximity 0 deg, 0.43m (want > 0.6m)"; object in front of ch5 -> "PreArm: Proximity 300 deg, 0.57m (want > 0.6m)"; two objects -> "PreArm: Rangefinder 2: No Data | PreArm: PRX1: No Data". PRX1 was HEALTHY in the first two (a different check fired, the too-close one) and No Data in the third, which had MORE objects, not fewer. Objects cannot be the variable.
- SOURCE, read from the tree at /media/sleuther/Stuff/ardupilot-SITL (Copter-4.7.0), not from memory:
  - AP_Proximity_MAV::handle_obstacle_distance_msg() sets `_last_update_ms = AP_HAL::millis()` UNCONDITIONALLY, right after decoding min/max, BEFORE any sector is range-checked (AP_Proximity_MAV.cpp:143). So an all-clear ring still refreshes the health timer. My hypothesis required the opposite and is therefore WRONG.
  - AP_Proximity_MAV::update() sets Status::NoData only when BOTH the obstacle message AND the upward one are older than PROXIMITY_MAV_TIMEOUT_MS = 500 ms (AP_Proximity_MAV.cpp:27,34-35). Either message alone keeps it Good.
  - AP_Arming::proximity_checks() just reports whatever prearm_healthy() says (AP_Arming.cpp:1284), and prearm_healthy() prints "PRX%d: No Data" for Status::NoData (AP_Proximity.cpp:351-356).
  - The 201 sentinel IS ignored per sector (range_check at AP_Proximity_MAV.cpp:171-175 drops 0, 65535, and anything outside min/max) - but ignoring a sector is not the same as being unhealthy, and only the latter blocks arming. That distinction is the whole error.
- THEREFORE: "PRX1: No Data" AND "Rangefinder 2: No Data" appearing TOGETHER means the ESP32 stopped transmitting for more than half a second. Both messages come from the same device over TELEM1, so both going stale at once is one fault, not two. This also re-reads the 2026-08-20 dry run, where the same pair appeared: that was not an indoor-walls puzzle, it was the stream stalling.
- CANDIDATE MECHANISMS, none verified: the ESP32 resetting; TELEM1 wiring; or the ESP32's poll loop stalling on the I2C bus, which is plausible because ch2's sensor is FRIED and a failed device on a TCA9548A channel can hold the bus. The 2026-08-18 "RING WENT SILENT after the push+reboot, then came back" is the same signature and supports intermittent rather than permanent.
- TOOL: ring_channels now reports the LONGEST SILENCE across both message types, in both modes, against the 500 ms number, and names it as the arming refusal when it exceeds it. That is the measurement that decides, and it needs no ESP32 access.
- 0.6 m CHECK IS HARMLESS OUTDOORS: "Proximity N deg, 0.43m (want > 0.6m)" is ArduPilot refusing to arm with something inside 0.6 m. In an open field nothing is that close, so it will not fire. It only appeared because the test object was deliberately at 0.5 m.

### 2026-08-21 00:20 IST: THE STALL IS CONFIRMED AND REPRODUCIBLE - 1.10 s of silence, twice

- 1.10 s is more than double PROXIMITY_MAV_TIMEOUT_MS (500 ms), so during that window PRX1 and RNGFND2 are both No Data and the aircraft refuses to arm. That is the full chain from measurement to the prearm text, and the earlier hypothesis about the 201 sentinel plays no part in it.
- ARMING IS INTERMITTENT, NOT IMPOSSIBLE, and this reframes tomorrow: the user's own tests 1 and 2 got a HEALTHY proximity (they failed on the 0.6 m too-close check instead), so the aircraft arms whenever the attempt lands between stalls. Retrying is a legitimate field workaround.
- OPEN CONFOUND, now instrumented: this board reads the ring stream AFTER the autopilot forwards it to USB, while AP_Proximity consumes it upstream on TELEM1. A gap measured here is therefore not by itself proof that the autopilot missed the packets. The tool now uses the autopilot's own 1 Hz HEARTBEAT as the control: it is generated by the autopilot, not the ESP32, and travels the same USB path. Beats INSIDE a silence = the path was alive and the ring packets never reached the autopilot (fault is ESP32 or TELEM1). No beats either = the whole link went quiet (fault is the USB path or the autopilot, and the ESP32 may be innocent).
- ALSO ADDED: the number of silences over the gate, their total duration, and that duration as a percentage of the run, which is roughly the chance an arming attempt lands inside one.
- BOTH BRANCHES TESTED against synthetic streams (heartbeats continuing through the stall, and everything stalling together).

### 2026-08-21 00:35 IST: ROOT CAUSE FOUND IN OUR OWN ESP32 FIRMWARE - the dead-sensor retry blocks the loop for ~1 s every 5 s
- THE RUN THAT PINNED IT (board, 872319b): 444 messages in 60 s = 7.4 Hz, **12 silences over 500 ms, totalling 12.9 s = 22% of the run**, longest 1.08 s. Twelve stalls in sixty seconds is one every five seconds, and every stall is the same length (12.9/12 = 1.07 s average against a 1.08 s maximum). A loose wire does not produce a fixed-length event on a fixed period; a software timeout does.
- **12 AUTOPILOT HEARTBEATS ARRIVED INSIDE THOSE SILENCES**, so the USB path was alive throughout and the ring packets never reached the autopilot. The fault is upstream of the Pixhawk, which is what the heartbeat control was added to decide.
- THE CODE, read from the repo (esp32_obstacle_avoidance/):
  - config.h:186 `SENSOR_RETRY_PERIOD_MS 5000` - maintain() retries ONE failed fitted channel every 5 s (proximity_sensors.cpp:209-220, round-robin via _retry_ch).
  - The retry calls initChannel() -> `VL53L0X::init()` on a chip that is not there. proximity_sensors.cpp:82, our own comment, says probing an absent channel "costs about a second of boot time waiting for a timeout". That is the 1.07 s, and it is BLOCKING: the sketch is single-threaded, so nothing is transmitted while it waits.
  - config.h:148 `RING_SENSOR_FITTED = {true, true, true, true, true, true}` marks ch1 and ch2 as fitted, so both are retried forever. ch1 was NEVER WIRED and ch2's chip is FRIED: neither has produced a single reading in any run.
- SO THE CHAIN IS COMPLETE: two channels that can never succeed are retried every 5 s -> each retry blocks ~1 s -> the MAVLink stream stops -> both proximity messages go stale past PROXIMITY_MAV_TIMEOUT_MS 500 -> "PRX1: No Data" and "Rangefinder 2: No Data" -> arming refused. It also explains the 26% packet loss exactly: 12.9 s of 60 is 22%, and the rest is ordinary jitter.
- THE FIX IS ONE LINE: `RING_SENSOR_FITTED = {true, false, false, true, true, true}`. Needs an ESP32 reflash, which the user has said he does not want to do again unless we really need to.
- THE CONFIG'S OWN ARGUMENT FOR KEEPING THEM FITTED IS NOW WRONG, and the why matters: config.h:139-143 says marking a flaky channel unfitted "would lose the readings they do produce and gain nothing". True for a channel that sometimes works; FALSE for ch1 and ch2, which produce nothing. Both paths put 65535 on the wire (proximity_sensors.cpp:78-88: an unfitted channel keeps _ok false and readAll() returns SENSOR_MM_ERROR, exactly as a failed fitted one does), so ArduPilot sees an identical "unknown" sector either way. The only difference is the retry, and the retry is the fault.
- WITHOUT A REFLASH, TWO WAYS TO FLY: retry ARM, which succeeds about 78% of attempts because the stalls occupy 22% of the time; or PRX1_TYPE 0, which arms cleanly and loses avoidance.

### 2026-08-21 00:50 IST: FIX MADE - ch1 and ch2 marked UNFITTED (user chose the reflash), firmware compiles
- config.h:148 is now `RING_SENSOR_FITTED = {true, false, false, true, true, true}`. The old comment block arguing FOR keeping them fitted is kept and answered in place rather than deleted, because its reasoning is right about flaky channels and only wrong about channels that never work at all.
- CHECKED THE REST OF THE FIRMWARE FOR ANYTHING THAT ASSUMED SIX FITTED, and there is nothing: begin() skips unfitted channels and a false return only prints a warning (.ino:169); maintain() gates every retry on isFitted() (proximity_sensors.cpp:212); readAll() emits SENSOR_MM_ERROR -> 65535 for any channel with _ok false, fitted or not; the whole-bus-clear path counts only fitted channels, so "every fitted channel is down" now means the four real ones, which is more correct than before, not less.
- COMPILED CLEAN on this laptop: `pio run -d esp32_obstacle_avoidance` -> SUCCESS, RAM 7.1%, flash 22.7%. Not flashed by the assistant; the ESP32 is the user's to touch.
- EXPECTED AFTER FLASHING, and this is the acceptance test: the same 60 s run should report ~10 Hz (about 600 messages), 0 silences over 500 ms, and no arming-refusal line. If the stalls survive the flash, the retry was not the cause and the next suspect is TELEM1 itself.
- FLASH ROUTE: `pio run -d esp32_obstacle_avoidance -t upload` from the laptop with the ESP32 on USB. platformio.ini already pins upload_speed 57600 and --no-stub for the marginal cable (2026-08-14 finding), so nothing there needs changing.

### 2026-08-21 (Raghav/Mac): ESP32 FLASHED with the ch1/ch2-unfitted firmware
- FLASHED FROM THE MAC, not the Zorin laptop. PlatformIO was absent here; `.venv/bin/pip install platformio` then `.venv/bin/pio run -d esp32_obstacle_avoidance -t upload`. Auto-detected /dev/cu.usbserial-0001 (no upload_port is pinned in platformio.ini, so macOS auto-detect works and nothing needed changing). ESP32-D0WD-V3 rev3.1, MAC 28:05:a5:27:0a:b0. RAM 7.1%, flash 22.7%, hash verified, 154 s. Byte-identical footprint to Reyansh's laptop compile.
- WIRING CORRECTION (user, primary source): **the ESP32 is on TELEM1, not TELEM2.** Entries above that say TELEM2 are wrong about which port; the block diagram (docs/block_diagram.svg) says TELEM2 and needs correcting.
- USER CHALLENGE, answered: "only ONE lidar isn't connected, why mark TWO unfitted?" ANSWER, from proximity_sensors.cpp:209-230: maintain() makes ONE init attempt per SENSOR_RETRY_PERIOD_MS (5 s), round-robin over whichever FITTED channel currently needs one. The stall cadence is therefore set by the retry PERIOD, not by the number of dead channels, so ONE dead-and-fitted channel sustains the ~1 s stall every 5 s on its own. Excluding only the physically-absent channel would have left the fried-but-connected chip being probed and measured the same 7.4 Hz. A fried chip still plugged in fails VL53L0X::init() exactly like an absent one and costs the same bounded ~1 s timeout.
- COST OF UNFITTING: none. Both channels were already sending SECTOR_NO_DATA (65535) because they were failing, and an unfitted channel sends the identical 65535. ArduPilot reads 65535 as NO INFORMATION about that bearing, never as "clear", so avoidance's opinion at 60 and 120 deg is unchanged (it had none before either).
- OPEN, ASKED: the record says ch1 was NEVER WIRED and ch2's chip is FRIED, but the user says only ONE lidar is disconnected. So one of the two is physically present. WHICH ONE IS DISCONNECTED IS NOT ESTABLISHED, and it matters: a present-but-dead chip is a replace job, while a present-and-good chip with bad wiring is recoverable and would need marking fitted again after repair.

### 2026-08-21 POST-FLASH RUN: stall UNCHANGED, and the reason is CH6, not a failed fix
- NUMBERS (board, 60 s, after the ch1/ch2-unfitted flash): 445 messages, 7.4 Hz, 12 silences over 500 ms totalling 13.0 s = 22%, longest 1.08 s. Identical to the pre-flash run. 14 autopilot heartbeats arrived inside the silences, so the USB path was alive and the packets never reached the autopilot.
- **THE UP SENSOR IS NOW SILENT FOR THE WHOLE RUN** (ch6 SILENT, upward-message silence 60.05 s = the entire window). Before the flash it worked: the earlier 60 s run logged 445 upward DISTANCE_SENSOR alongside 446 obstacle, and test_everything read it at 2.01 m.
- **SO THE FIX DID WORK AND CH6 TOOK OVER AS THE STALLING CHANNEL.** Verified in the source, not inferred: isFitted() returns UP_SENSOR_FITTED for ch == UP_SENSOR_CHANNEL (proximity_sensors.cpp:11-14), UP_SENSOR_FITTED is still 1 (config.h:175), and maintain() loops `i < NUM_SENSORS` = 7 (config.h:78), so channel 6 sits in the retry loop exactly as ch1/ch2 did. A dead-and-fitted ch6 is retried every SENSOR_RETRY_PERIOD_MS and blocks ~1 s on the VL53L0X init timeout. The stall CADENCE is set by the retry period, not by which channel fails, which is why the numbers are byte-for-byte the same.
- WHY CH6 DIED NOW: its power connection has a recorded history of coming loose. config.h:172-175 records 2026-08-14, "ch6 had simply been DISCONNECTED FROM POWER... 20/20 after its power was reconnected", and the first test_everything of 2026-08-18 called it the "known-flaky power plug" before a cold power cycle brought it back. The flash required unplugging the ESP32 from TELEM1, carrying it to the Mac and reconnecting it, which is exactly the handling that would disturb that plug.
- NEXT ACTION IS MECHANICAL, NOT FIRMWARE: reseat ch6's power connector and re-run the 60 s test. If ch6 reports and the stalls go with it, the chain is fully closed. Marking UP_SENSOR_FITTED 0 would also stop the stall but COSTS THE UPWARD SENSOR ENTIRELY and needs another reflash; try the connector first.
- CH6 IS ALSO RNGFND2, so its silence is independently an arming blocker ("Rangefinder 2: No Data") regardless of the stall.

### 2026-08-21 CHANNEL IDENTITY CORRECTED (user, primary source) + arm-colour map

    ch0    0 deg  nose, BETWEEN THE TWO RED ARMS      (verified)
    ch1   60 deg  between top-right RED and right BLACK   -- fried, disconnected
    ch2  120 deg  between right BLACK and rear-right WHITE -- sensor present, never reports

The only change was that reseating, so the handling is the prime suspect for disturbing ESP32 power or the TELEM1 wiring. Discriminator is bench.py nodes: component 195 present = ESP32 alive, fault is downstream; absent = unpowered / not booted / TELEM1 disturbed.

### 2026-08-21: ESP32 ALIVE BUT SENDING NO OBSTACLE DATA -- the bus-clear branch is the suspect
- MEASURED: bench.py nodes, 20 s -> comp 195 PRESENT, 13 heartbeats (autopilot 27). So the ESP32 booted, TELEM1 works, and its packets reach the autopilot and are forwarded to the board. Power and wiring are EXONERATED as the cause of the obstacle silence.
- **THE FIRMWARE HAS NO HEALTH GATE ON THE SEND** (read, not assumed): esp32_obstacle_avoidance.ino:212-226, with USE_FAKE_SENSORS 0 `tx_allowed` is a `const bool = true`, so sendObstacleDistance() runs every TX_PERIOD_MS regardless of how many sensors are dead. Zero obstacle messages therefore means THE LOOP IS NOT REACHING THE SEND, not that it chose to stay quiet.
- SUSPECT, from loop order (.ino:196-202): readAll() then maintain() both run BEFORE the send. maintain()'s bus-clear branch fires when every fitted channel dies at once and then re-inits EVERY fitted channel in one pass (proximity_sensors.cpp:198-206), each absent/dead chip costing a bounded ~1 s init timeout. With ch0/ch3/ch4/ch5/ch6 fitted and all failing that is ~5 s of blocking per trigger, which starves the 10 Hz obstacle send while the 1 Hz heartbeat still gets through. Matches 13 heartbeats + 0 obstacle exactly.
- TRIGGER: reseating ch6's power connector on a LIVE I2C bus, which is a standard way to hang SDA/SCL, and config.h already records a half-powered device on the shared net disturbing more than its own channel.
- CHEAPEST NEXT STEP, and it avoids the UNO Q filesystem risk entirely: power-cycle THE ESP32 ONLY (unplug/replug its feed from the USB-A buck, leave the board running) so its bus recovery runs from a clean boot. Then test_everything, whose ring row surfaces the ESP32's own "prx ring N/6 up:ok" STATUSTEXT -- N tells us what the firmware believes about its sensors, which is the discriminator between "bus hung" and "sending but lost".
- WATCH FOR: ch6 half-seated. Fully in or fully out; a partially connected sensor is the recorded worst case.

### 2026-08-21 CH6 ROOT CAUSE MEASURED: a HIGH-RESISTANCE power joint, not a loose plug
- USER'S METER, at each sensor's VIN: ch0/ch2/ch3/ch4/ch5 all **3.22-3.24 V**. ch6 **3.04 V, and it GREW SLOWLY**.
- **THE SLOW RISE IS THE DIAGNOSIS.** On a shared 3V3 rail every VIN should be at the same potential and reach it instantly. A reading that climbs while the probe is held is the sensor's decoupling capacitor charging through a HIGH SERIES RESISTANCE. A solid connection snaps to rail potential.
- THE 0.2 V DEFICIT SAYS THE SAME THING, and it UNDERSTATES the fault: a multimeter draws almost nothing, so 3.04 V is ch6's BEST case. Under the current the sensor actually draws while ranging, the drop across that resistance is far larger, which is why ch6 inits sometimes and dies other times.
- SO THE FAULT IS A BAD JOINT (cold solder, broken strand, connector barely contacting) IN CH6'S POWER FEED, NOT A LOOSE PLUG. This is why every previous "fix" (reconnect power, cold power cycle, reseat) worked briefly and then failed again: 2026-08-14, 2026-08-18, and the reseat of 2026-08-21 which preceded the total ring silence. STOP RESEATING IT; the joint must be remade.
- IT ALSO EXPLAINS THE WHOLE-RING SILENCE: a partially powered VL53L0X can hold SDA or SCL, and config.h already records a half-powered device on the shared net disturbing more than its own channel. That is the bus hang that starved the send loop.
- OPTIONS, in order of preference: (1) REMAKE CH6'S POWER JOINT (solder it). Fixes everything at once: no dead fitted channel, no retry stall, up sensor back, RNGFND2 arming block gone. (2) Disconnect ch6 FULLY (never half) + reflash UP_SENSOR_FITTED 0 + RNGFND2_TYPE 0: stable but loses the upward sensor. NOTE: disconnecting WITHOUT the reflash leaves ch6 fitted and dead, so maintain() retries it every 5 s and the ~1 s stall returns (the 22%/"arms 78% of attempts" state). (3) PRX1_TYPE 0 and fly with no ring at all.

### 2026-08-21 THE RING IS FIXED. ch6 is not, and ring_channels.py has gone BLIND (tool caveat)
- EVIDENCE (test_everything, after the ch6 power resolder + I2C reconnect): ESP32 health text now reads **"prx ring 4/4 up:ERR"** (was 4/6), i.e. ALL FOUR FITTED ring channels alive, and **"PreArm: PRX1: No Data" IS GONE from the prearm list**. Proximity is streaming with no gap past PROXIMITY_MAV_TIMEOUT_MS. The stall saga is over.
- CH6 IS STILL DEAD: "up:ERR", and "PreArm: Rangefinder 2: No Data" remains the ONLY new arming blocker (the other four -- RC, safety switch, GPS fix, fence-needs-position -- all clear outdoors with the TX on). Power measured good (resistance now matches ch0) and the SD6/SC6 pair was reconnected, so supply and bus are both ruled out: the remaining candidates are a DEAD CHIP (most likely, after repeated brown-out episodes) or mux channel 6 / its leads.
- **TOOL CAVEAT, important: ring_channels.py IS NOW BLIND AND ITS ERROR TEXT IS WRONG.** It reads raw OBSTACLE_DISTANCE forwarded to USB, and the Pixhawk has stopped forwarding that raw copy while still CONSUMING it (proven: test_everything's ring_orients come from DISTANCE_SENSOR, and orientation 0 = the FORWARD proximity sector, which only the autopilot's own AP_Proximity re-emission can produce, since the ESP32 sends DISTANCE_SENSOR only for the UP sensor and that one is in ERR). Why forwarding changed is NOT established; it followed the power cycle for soldering. Until that is understood, "no OBSTACLE_DISTANCE at all" from ring_channels does NOT mean the ESP32 is dead, and its printed advice to suspect power/TELEM1 is misleading. THE TRUSTWORTHY RING READOUT IS NOW test_everything's "prx ring N/M" health line.
- STATE OF PLAY: ring working, avoidance available; ch6 dead; one param (RNGFND2_TYPE 0) stands between here and a clean prearm.

### 2026-08-21 HOPPER DOES NOT FLOW: the cause is COHESION, not orifice size (measured ratio 25:1)
- MEASURED (user): grains ~0.1 mm, orifice ~2.5 mm = **25:1**. Nothing falls even with the gate held open 20 s; only shaking moves it. flow_test.py therefore cannot be run and NO g/s figure exists.
- **THIS RULES OUT GEOMETRIC ARCHING.** The rule of thumb for grains wedging into a stable arch is an orifice ~6-10x the largest particle (a rule of thumb, no verified source to hand); at 25:1 the grains cannot interlock. What is left is COHESION: at ~100 microns, van der Waals attraction and moisture bridging between grains exceed gravity, so the dome is held together by stickiness. Monsoon humidity on hygroscopic fine salt is close to the worst case available. CORRECTION TO THE EARLIER ADVICE IN THIS FILE: enlarging the hole will NOT fix this, and neither will a steeper cone on its own.
- FIX CHOSEN BY USER: change the surrogate. Requirements that follow from the mechanism: >=1 mm, ROUNDED (spheres cannot interlock and shed cohesive contact area), and non-hygroscopic.
- CANDIDATES GIVEN, best first: sabudana/sago pearls (2-3 mm near-perfect spheres, the diagnostic test -- if THIS does not flow the fault is the gate mechanism, not the material); mustard seeds (1-2 mm hard spheres, and the closest household match to VectoBac G's corn-cob granule, so flow numbers measured with it mean the most); COARSE rock/sea salt (1-3 mm, angular so it flows less freely, but keeps the documented "inert salt" story and needs no rewrite of the compliance section); dry rice (~2 mm, elongated so it interlocks more); semolina (0.3-0.7 mm, a middle data point to locate the cohesion threshold, not a flight candidate).
- TWO CONSEQUENCES FOR THE SUBMISSION: (a) anything edible dropped outdoors attracts ants and birds, which is a bad look for a mosquito-control demo, so favour coarse salt for FILMED flights if it flows; (b) any g/s figure belongs to the surrogate it was measured with and does NOT transfer to Bti, so docs must name the material beside the number.
- GOOD MATERIAL FOR docs/ "what we learnt": the dispenser bridged on the bench and the fix was granule choice, not a bigger hole. That is a concrete physical lesson, and the 25:1 measurement is what turned a guess into a diagnosis.

### 2026-08-21 16:10 IST: RNGFND2_TYPE was NEVER pushed as 0; ring_channels' silence advice fixed
- ANSWER TO "was it in my param push": NO. The push of 2026-08-18 wrote 73/73 from pixhawk_full_setup.param, and that file said `RNGFND2_TYPE,10` (MAVLink). 10 is therefore what is on the board right now, RNGFND2_TYPE 0 was only PROPOSED today, and "PreArm: Rangefinder 2: No Data" is live and will block arming.
- FILE NOW SAYS 0, with the reason and the reversal condition beside it. The single live write still has to be made on the aircraft; editing the file alone changes nothing on the board.
- IT DOES NOT COST AVOIDANCE, which is what the user asked to keep: PRX1_TYPE stays 2 and AVOID_ENABLE stays 7. AP_Proximity consumes the up sensor's DISTANCE_SENSOR itself and does not read RNGFND2, so the horizontal ring is untouched. The only thing surrendered is an upward sensor that is already dead.
- REBOOT: ArduPilot 4.7 does NOT tag RNGFND_TYPE @RebootRequired (AP_RangeFinder_Params.cpp, the TYPE block has no such line), but it IS declared AP_PARAM_FLAG_ENABLE and backends are detected at init, so a power cycle is the reliable way to make 0 take effect. Verify by re-running the prearm check rather than assuming.
- RING_CHANNELS SILENCE MESSAGE FIXED (user: "that should have been fixed a long time ago"). It now counts DISTANCE_SENSOR orientations 0-7, which are the autopilot's own 45 deg proximity sectors emitted by AP_Proximity after it has digested the ring. The ESP32 never sends those; it sends orientation 24 for the up sensor and nothing else. So: sectors present + no OBSTACLE_DISTANCE = THE RING IS WORKING and only the raw forwarded copy is missing, with an explicit "do not go looking at ESP32 power or TELEM1" and a pointer to test_everything's "prx ring N/M". Nothing at all = the old ESP32/TELEM1/bench.py-nodes advice, which is still right in that case.
- WHY THE OLD TEXT WAS THE WORST KIND OF WRONG, recorded so it is not repeated: it was confident, specific, and aimed at hardware that was fine, at the exact moment the ring had just been repaired.

### 2026-08-21 (field): VIBRATION PROTECTION AND TX OVERRIDE BOTH ALREADY EXIST - no code written, and one proposed change REJECTED with cause
- USER ASKED FOR (a) code that lets the aircraft fall to ~15 ft then checks the TF-Luna for real height when it "thinks it is falling", and (b) a mechanism for the TX to override the companion.
- (b) IS ALREADY BUILT AND CORRECT. mission.py:239-243: any heartbeat showing a mode other than GUIDED, once GUIDED has been seen at least once, sets STANDDOWN and BREAKS the loop. STANDDOWN is deliberately excluded from the end_mode command (mission.py:256, only DONE and STOPPED command it), so the companion never fights a pilot who has taken the aircraft. The override is INSTANT at the autopilot, because ArduPilot ignores GUIDED setpoints when not in GUIDED; the 1 Hz heartbeat only sets how long until the companion notices and stops talking. The _seen_guided gate exists so stale pre-takeoff telemetry cannot fake a takeover.
- (a) IS ALREADY IN THE FIRMWARE, AND ON BY DEFAULT. ArduCopter/Parameters.cpp:783 `AP_GROUPINFO("FS_VIBE_ENABLE", 35, ParametersG2, fs_vibe_enabled, 1)` - default 1. ekf_check.cpp:293-305 detects exactly the described failure (downward velocity/position innovations positive together with high velocity variance, i.e. the EKF believing it is falling under vibration), and after 1 s continuous sets high_vibes and switches to modified altitude estimation and control. It is not in pixhawk_full_setup.param, so the board carries the default.
- WHY THE COMPANION MUST NOT DO THIS JOB: run_mission commands GUIDED position targets over MAVLink at a few Hz, while the failure lives in the autopilot's own control loop. A Python reaction arrives after the aircraft has already moved tens of metres, and it would add an untested actor to the altitude loop on the day of a flight.
- **REJECTED FOR THIS FLIGHT: pointing the EKF at the TF-Luna (EK3_RNG_USE_HGT).** It is the correct ArduPilot expression of "check the real height with the Luna", but this mission descends over WATER, and a downward lidar over water can return long or short from specular reflection. Making the EKF trust it there converts a sensor error into an altitude error. Not enabled, and the reason is the mission profile, not the parameter.
- REAL VIBRATION WORK IS MECHANICAL AND AFTER THE FACT: prop balance and damping, then read VIBE and accelerometer clipping from the .bin with tools/check_log.py. Nothing to change before this flight.

### 2026-08-21 (field, pre-flight): GO. The ring's red cross is OUR STALE DENOMINATOR, and ch6 came back to life
- FIELD SELF-TEST: logs, disk 17.1 GB, camera 1280x720, Pixhawk heartbeat, GPS 3D 13 sats HDOP 0.77, battery 12.49 V, Luna 0.11 m on ground, fence 6 corners matching, **prearm PASSING**. Only the ring row showed a cross.
- **THE RING CROSS IS A TOOL BUG, NOT HARDWARE.** test_everything.py:291 parses only the NUMERATOR out of the ESP32's "prx ring N/M" text and then prints it against a HARDCODED 6 at line 305. Since ch1 and ch2 were unfitted, the ESP32 now says "prx ring 4/4", meaning every FITTED channel is alive, and our tool renders that as "4/6" and fails it. The denominator must come from the health text, not from a constant. NOT FIXED TODAY: the aircraft was about to fly and the row is cosmetic.
- **CH6 IS ALIVE AGAIN: "up:ok", 2.01 m.** It was up:ERR this morning. So the resolder held and the upward sensor works, which means RNGFND2_TYPE 0 turned out to be UNNECESSARY. It was set on this morning's evidence and stands for today: reverting costs another reboot, and ch6 has a long history of dying and returning. Nothing is lost by leaving it, because AP_Proximity reads the up sensor from DISTANCE_SENSOR orientation 24 directly and does not go through RNGFND2 (the 2.01 m in the ring row is that path working). REVISIT AFTER THE FLIGHT: if ch6 stays healthy, put RNGFND2_TYPE back to 10 in the same edit as the evidence.
- THE 0.37 m PREARM REFUSAL IS CORRECT BEHAVIOUR AND IS ALSO A FREE BEARING CHECK: the user was at the tail fixing props and the aircraft reported "Proximity 180 deg, 0.37m". 180 deg is ch3, the tail. The identity map and the physical wiring agree at a second bearing, having previously been verified only at ch0.
- STANDING FIELD HAZARD, first flight with avoidance live: AVOID_ENABLE 7 with AVOID_MARGIN 1 m means anyone within about a metre PUSHES the aircraft in GPS modes. Everyone stands clear before ARM. And the 60-120 deg arc is blind (ch1 fried, ch2 unfitted), so obstacles on that side are invisible.

### 2026-08-21 (post-flight): LOGS 47-50 ANALYSED. "The GPS error" is THREE separate blockers, and the compass is exonerated
- 47 and 49 are bench logs, motors never spun. 49's file timestamp is 1980, i.e. that session never got GPS time at all. 48 (11:55) and 50 (13:14) are the real ones.
- **GPS_HDOP_GOOD = 140 CONFIRMED ON THE BOARD** (read from log 50's PARM records, not from memory). ArduPilot refuses to arm above HDOP 1.4; the project's own field rule is 1.5. `PreArm: High GPS HDOP` is the message the user remembered.
- **THE NUMBER THAT SETTLES IT: in log 50, 2230 of 4043 3D-fix samples (55%) were above HDOP 1.4. In log 48, 4 of 437 (about 1%).** Same aircraft, same day, 80 minutes apart. So nothing is broken in a way that is always broken; the fix quality collapsed between the two flights.
- **TWO CANDIDATE CAUSES, BOTH LIVE, DISTINGUISHABLE BY ONE TEST.** (a) SKY: 12-13 sats and HDOP 0.79-0.90 in log 48 against 10 sats and 1.05-1.74 in log 50. Three fewer satellites moves HDOP that much on its own. The two logs are about 47 m apart (48: 12.993303, 77.726428; 50: 12.993681, 77.726620), so the second spot may simply have more sky blocked. (b) COLD START: log 48 had a 3D fix 14 s after boot, log 50 took **257 s**. Ephemeris stays valid for hours, so a cold start 80 minutes after a warm one is abnormal and points at a dead backup battery/supercap on the GPS module. TEST: power cycle at one spot and time the first 3D fix. Under about 30 s means the backup is fine and the cause is sky.
- **BLOCKER 2, AND IT FIRED MORE OFTEN THAN HDOP DID: `PreArm: Vehicle breaching Polygon fence`,** continuously from t=715 s to the end of log 50. FENCE_ENABLE 1, FENCE_TYPE 7, FENCE_TOTAL 8, FENCE_MARGIN 2. The fence was verified matching at the pre-flight self-test, which happened at the log-48 spot. The aircraft then moved ~47 m and sat outside its own saved polygon. `Arm: GPS and AHRS differ by 14.0m` at t=725 is the same story from the other side: the position was also wandering.
- **BLOCKER 3: `PreArm: Proximity <bearing> deg, <n>m (want > 0.6m)`, dozens of times across both logs.** This is NEW and is the direct cost of the ring finally working with AVOID_ENABLE 7. Anything inside 0.6 m of the aircraft refuses the arm. 180 deg (tail, ch3) dominates, exactly as the pre-flight bearing check predicted. Not a fault: clear a 0.6 m bubble before every arm attempt.
- **THE COMPASS IS CLEARED, AND THIS IS A REAL IMPROVEMENT SINCE 2026-08-15.** MAG0 375 mG at rest / 364 flying, MAG1 400 / 288, against this site's own 875 gate. Logs 43 and 45 were 1038 and 1058. `GPS Glitch or Compass error` is ArduPilot's SHARED string for two different causes, and the field numbers rule the compass out here. Do not re-open the mag anomaly on the strength of that message alone.
- HEALTHY EVERYWHERE ELSE: VibeZ median 7.8 (gate 15), clipping +0, hover ThH 0.390 unchanged, lowest pack voltage 11.17 V. RNGFND2_TYPE reads 0 as flown, so that write did land.
- ARMED THREE TIMES in log 50 (t=1007, 1042, 1064) plus once at t=849, for 14 s of motor running in total, in the one window where HDOP dipped under the gate. So it did arm; it did not get a flight.

### 2026-08-21 (post-flight, part 2): THE TILT IS INTEGRATOR WINDUP, NOT A BAD MOTOR. And the fence was never "well inside"
- USER REPORT: "we increased throttle but it was tilting towards one side, so we stopped. After that it didn't work." This is the safety-critical item from the day and it outranks the GPS.
- **IT NEVER LEFT THE GROUND.** CTUN.ThO peaked at 0.36 against the learned hover of 0.390, and CTUN.Alt never moved through any of the five armed windows. The tilt happened on the legs.
- **NO MOTOR IS SPINNING BACKWARDS.** Yaw held 276.5 to 276.7 deg with near-zero yaw rate through the entire throttle-up. A reversed motor dumps unbalanced torque into yaw and would be unmissable here. The 3-of-6 reversal history is NOT recurring.
- **THE PROOF IT IS WINDUP, not thrust asymmetry: PIDR.I climbs monotonically 0.003 -> 0.147 over nine seconds while PIDR.P stays under 0.031 and the actual roll rate sits at ~0.** P near zero means there is no instantaneous error; the whole motor spread is the integrator. Second, independent proof: throttle was HELD at 0.24-0.25 from t=1012.7 to t=1018.7 while the motor spread grew from 48 us to 274 us. Constant input, growing output is an integrator. A weak motor or a flipped prop shows its spread INSTANTLY and in proportion to throttle, and at the first instant of throttle-up (t=1012.1) the six outputs were 1378/1381/1403/1355/1402/1355, a 48 us spread, which is what a healthy mixer looks like.
- **WHY IT WOUND UP:** the aircraft rests at roughly -1 to -2 deg of PITCH in all four of today's logs (47: -1.81, 48: -1.60, 49: -1.20, 50: -1.07), across different sessions and spots, so a level-calibration or FC-mount offset is the likely source rather than ground slope. The controller integrates that standing error for as long as you dwell at partial throttle, then applies the whole 0.147 of wound-up roll authority the instant the aircraft gets light on its legs. Dwelling on the ground at 20-30% throttle while reading the OLED is what caused this.
- **FIX IS PROCEDURAL, NOT A REPAIR:** once armed, raise throttle smoothly and continuously to lift-off in about 2 to 3 seconds; do not sit at partial throttle. Then redo the accel/level calibration on a genuinely level surface to remove the standing pitch bias. STILL WORTH DOING ANYWAY, because windup is consistent with but does not prove the absence of a weak arm: props-off motor test, confirm each of the six spins, direction, and no bent shaft or upside-down prop.
- **"AFTER THAT IT DIDN'T WORK" IS NOT NEW DAMAGE.** There is no successful arm after t=1077.6 out to the end of the log at t=1928, and every refusal in those 850 s is HDOP, fence, EKF velocity variance or GPS glitching. Nothing broke during the tilt.
- **THE FENCE, RESOLVED FROM THE FNCE RECORDS IN THE LOG (6 corners, logged).** The polygon is only about 62 m east-west by 55 m north-south. Contrary to the assumption that spot 2 was well inside it, **spot 2 sat about 6 m from the EAST edge** with FENCE_MARGIN 2. During the breach window (t=715-780) only 19% of fix samples read as inside and the position wandered to 10 m OUTSIDE that edge. At the successful arm windows it read 100% inside with 2.4 to 7.5 m of clearance, which is exactly why arming worked there and nowhere else. Log 48's spot got within 0.5 m of an edge too. BOTH SPOTS WERE PARKED TOO CLOSE TO THE FENCE for a receiver that wanders 10 m.
- CONSEQUENCE FOR THE NEXT FLIGHT: park in the MIDDLE of the polygon, not near an edge, or draw a bigger fence. A 62x55 m fence is also small for a survey pattern.
- TREES CONFIRMED BY THE USER at spot 2, one side covered. Consistent with 10 sats there against 13 at spot 1. The 257 s cold start is still not fully explained by canopy alone, so the power-cycle time-to-first-fix test stays worth doing.
- AVOID_ENABLE 7 CONFIRMED AS DELIBERATE AND STAYS. The 0 deg and 180 deg proximity refusals were the two of them standing at the aircraft reading the OLED, i.e. the ring doing its job.

### 2026-08-22 ~09:00 IST: gate travel re-measured after the hole was enlarged; TRIM followed it
- USER: hole enlarged to cure the granule arching; new marks are **closed 500 us, open 1600 us**. Asked whether the code picks that up by itself. IT DOES NOT, and one edit covers it: `PixhawkServoDropper.DEFAULT_CLOSED_US/_OPEN_US` in uno_q/dropper.py is the single source, and tools/flow_test.py, tools/wiring_check.py and uno_q/run_mission.py all read their argparse defaults off that class. Changed 560/1760 -> 500/1600.
- **SECOND-ORDER BUG CAUGHT IN THE SAME EDIT: SERVO9_TRIM was 560, chosen back when 560 WAS the closed position.** With closed now 500, trim sat BETWEEN closed and open, so an output emitting trim at boot (before the first DO_SET_SERVO) would park the gate about 5% open and dribble granules on the pad. param_dumps/pixhawk_full_setup.param now sets SERVO9_TRIM,500. THIS IS A FILE EDIT ONLY: it does nothing until pushed to the board.
- NOT A PROBLEM, and recorded so it is not re-raised: closed 500 equals SERVO9_MIN 500 exactly, but with SERVO9_FUNCTION=0 (k_none) MAV_CMD_DO_SET_SERVO writes the raw pulse straight through with no constrain (AP_ServoRelayEvents.cpp do_set_servo -> SRV_Channel::set_output_pwm), so MIN is GCS slider metadata and not enforcement. Both 500 and 1600 are also inside mavlink_io's PWM_MIN_US/PWM_MAX_US guard of 500-1800.
- 2026-08-22 09:05: I broke the 2026-08-20 wrapper rule again, writing `~/python ~/monsoonready-drone/git-pull`. TWO errors in one: git-pull is a HOME-DIR SH WRAPPER (`~/git-pull`), not a repo file, and it is not python so it never goes through `~/python`. `~/python` is only for .py files under ~/monsoonready-drone. The rule at the top of this section is correct as written; the failure was not reading it before writing the block.

### 2026-08-22 ~10:00 IST: flow_test can now measure a dose the scale cannot see; Bti label rates researched
- USER PROBLEM: granules fall but one dose is under the weighing machine's resolution. Asked for Bti dosage research and proposed entering a GRAIN COUNT times a per-grain mass.
- **BTI FIELD DOSE, from label rates (researched, sources in the flow_test.py header):** VectoBac G is 2.5-20 lb/acre (2.5-10 for 1st-2nd instar, 10-20 for 3rd-4th or polluted water). At 4046.86 m2/acre that is **0.28 to 2.24 g/m2**, mid-label about **1.1 g/m2**. Summit Mosquito Bits' 1 tsp per 25 sq ft works out near 1.1 g/m2 too, BUT that needs a teaspoon-to-grams conversion I assumed at 2.5 g, so it is corroboration and NOT independent evidence. Use the range when anything is at stake.
- **THE DOSE NUMBER KILLS GRAIN COUNTING FOR FULL DOSES, and this is the useful finding:** a 1 mm mustard seed is roughly 0.6 mg, so one gram is about 1700 seeds and a 2 m2 puddle's 2.2 g is ~3700 seeds. Nobody counts that. Counting is only honest for tiny doses or for calibrating the per-grain mass itself from a large counted sample.
- **THE BETTER ANSWER, IMPLEMENTED AS `--cycles N`: fire the gate N times into the SAME tray and weigh once.** A 0.3 g dose becomes 6 g at N=20, which any kitchen scale reads, and averaging N shots also removes single-shot scatter. It needs no new constant, so it introduces no new way to be wrong. `--grain-mg X` implemented too, as asked, and the two compose.
- ALL DOWNSTREAM NUMBERS ARE PER-DOSE: run_cycles returns total open seconds, and the loop divides both grams and seconds by N before recording, so the linear fit, the g/s and the dose table all describe ONE dose, which is what the mission commands.
- run_cycles ABORTS the whole measurement on the first bad cycle instead of continuing, because a partial run weighed as a full one reports a LOW flow rate, which is indistinguishable afterwards from a bridging hopper.
- A zero catch is now rejected rather than recorded: "nothing came out" is not a rate, and letting a 0 into the fit drags the slope toward a lie.
- NEW DOSE TABLE at the end of the run: puddle area -> grams -> seconds, at 1.1 g/m2. **It prints a warning that the SECONDS are real and the GRAMS are not**, because every measurement so far is a surrogate (salt/sooji/seeds) whose density is not Bti's. Re-measure with real Bti before any gram figure goes in front of a judge.
- IT ALSO WARNS IF A 1 m2 DOSE IS UNDER 0.3 s, i.e. comparable to the servo's own travel time, in which case the dose is set by gate speed rather than dwell and either the hole shrinks or dose_s_min becomes a floor.
- BUG CAUGHT IN MY OWN EDIT BEFORE IT SHIPPED: I first gated the dose block on `'slope' in dir()`, which works by accident and breaks the moment the code moves. `slope` is now initialised to None above the fit.

### 2026-08-22 ~10:15 IST: THE HOPPER FLOWS. And the fit found a FIXED SLUG, which my own dose table was ignoring
- **TODO 3 IS FINALLY OPEN AFTER MONTHS: granules flow.** Enlarged hole, `--cycles 5`, two dwells. 0.26 s -> 1.400 g/dose, 0.52 s -> 2.200 g/dose. Fit: **grams = 3.13 * seconds + 0.57**. This is the project's FIRST measured flow rate; before today the mission's dose seconds were proportional to nothing.
- **THE INTERCEPT IS POSITIVE (+0.57 g) AND THAT IS A PHYSICAL FINDING, not noise.** A fixed slug falls the instant the gate cracks, because the material already sitting in the aperture is past the gate before the servo moves. Consequences: (a) 0.57 g is the MINIMUM DOSE and shortening the dwell cannot reduce it, only a smaller aperture can; (b) dose does NOT scale to zero, so any puddle wanting under 0.57 g is over-dosed; (c) dwell for a target mass is (grams - 0.57)/3.13, NOT grams/3.13.
- **BUG IN MY OWN CODE, FOUND BY ITS FIRST REAL RUN AND FIXED:** the dose table computed `grams / rate` and ignored the intercept, so it told the user 0.35 s for a 1 m2 puddle when the correct answer is (1.1-0.57)/3.13 = 0.17 s. It was over-stating every dwell, by 50% at 1 m2 and less as area grows. The table now solves the full line, prints the slug warning, and flags areas the slug alone overshoots.
- SECOND BUG, SAME ORIGIN: the "no meaningful opening lag" branch fired on a POSITIVE intercept and told the user "dose seconds scale honestly", which is the opposite of the truth here. There is now an explicit positive-intercept branch; the old else-branch only claims proportionality when the intercept is genuinely near zero.
- **HONESTY LIMIT ON ALL OF THE ABOVE, and it must not be dropped when quoting: TWO POINTS, ZERO DEGREES OF FREEDOM.** A line through two points fits exactly by construction, so +0.57 is not statistically established and could equally be curvature (rate falling as head drops) sampled at two places. FALSIFIABLE TEST: run --dwells 1 2. If the slug is real, a 1.0 s dose weighs about 3.70 g; if the intercept was an artifact, it weighs about 3.13 g.
- MATERIAL NOT YET RECORDED HERE: the run does not say what was in the hopper. The g/s figure is meaningless without it and PROJECT_STATE must not carry the number alone. ASK BEFORE QUOTING.

### 2026-08-22 ~10:20 IST: THE +0.57 g "SLUG" WAS WRONG. The hopper was STARVING, and the user called it before the test ran
- USER, BEFORE RUNNING IT: "there isn't enough mustard seeds for 5 cycles of 2 seconds each - after the 1st cycle there'll be no mustard right around the hole." He was right and the data says so.
- MATERIAL, NOW RECORDED: **MUSTARD SEEDS**, ~1 mm. Every flow number in this file dated 2026-08-22 belongs to mustard seeds and to nothing else.
- THE FOUR POINTS, in rate terms: 0.26 s -> 5.30 g/s, 0.52 s -> 4.23, 1.02 s -> 4.43, 2.04 s -> 2.70. **A gate open longer cannot deliver LESS per second unless it is running out of material.** The 2 s dose caught 5.5 g against the 1 s dose's 4.5 g: twice the open time, 22% more material.
- **SO THE POSITIVE INTERCEPT IS AN ARTIFACT, IN BOTH RUNS.** A rate that decays during the dwell, fitted with a straight line, manufactures a large positive intercept that reads exactly like a fixed slug at gate-crack. Run 1's +0.57 g and run 2's +3.51 g are the same artifact at two severities. THE 2026-08-22 10:15 ENTRY'S SLUG FINDING IS WITHDRAWN; the physical claim in it is not supported.
- MY FALSIFIABLE TEST DID ITS JOB BY FAILING BOTH WAYS: it predicted 3.70 g if the slug were real and 3.13 g if not. The answer was 4.50 g, outside both, which falsifies the linear model itself rather than choosing between the two hypotheses.
- **BEST DEFENSIBLE RATE FOR MUSTARD SEEDS RIGHT NOW: about 4.2-5.3 g/s**, from the two shortest dwells, which are the least starved. NOT 3.13 and NOT 0.98; both of those are fits to starved data. The mission's real doses are 0.2-2 s, so the short-dwell figure is the relevant one anyway.
- **TOOL HARDENED SO IT CANNOT REPEAT MY MISDIAGNOSIS:** flow_test now checks whether g/s FALLS with dwell BEFORE fitting. A drop below 70% of the shortest-dwell rate prints a SUPPLY-LIMITED banner, names the shortest-dwell rate as the only defensible number, forces the positive-intercept branch to say "supply decay, not a slug", and REFUSES to print a dose table at all. A dose table built on starved data would be confidently wrong, which is worse than no table. The intercept alone cannot distinguish slug from starvation, which is exactly why I got it wrong; the discriminator is the rate trend.
- STILL OPEN: a full-hopper measurement has never been taken. Every number above is from an under-filled hopper. Refill and re-run before any figure goes in the submission.

### 2026-08-22 ~11:00 IST: SECOND FUNDAMENTAL-PROBLEMS SCAN (user-requested, after reading everything since 2026-08-16). Analysis only, nothing changed
Sources: this file end to end, plus mission.py (the new CROSS/RETURN water sequence), detector.py, run_mission.py, mavlink_io.py, make_waypoints.py and the current param file. The four surviving findings of the 08-17 scan are unchanged; two of them have moved (finding 4's release-over-water sequence is BUILT, finding 5's flow rate is MEASURED for mustard seeds but from a starved hopper). These are NEW and none of them is recorded anywhere above.
1. **NOTHING CHECKS A DETECTION AGAINST THE GEOFENCE, AND THE CAMERA SEES WELL OUTSIDE IT.** grep for "fence" across mission.py, detector.py and run_mission.py returns nothing. The footprint at 15 m is 16.0 x 9.0 m, so the swath reaches ~8 m either side of the aircraft, while build_coverage puts the rows only 4 m inside the polygon (make_waypoints.py:76 inset_m=4.0). A puddle seen at the edge of frame on a boundary row is therefore OUTSIDE the fence, and mission.py latches it, adds the 3 m offset (which can push it further out) and commands goto(). Two outcomes, both bad and neither handled: ArduPilot refuses a GUIDED destination outside the fence, in which case the refusal is SILENT because SET_POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT carries no ack and the mission waits in APPROACH forever; or the aircraft flies there and breaches, and FENCE_ACTION 1 commands RTL, which mission.py reads as a pilot override (mode != GUIDED) and answers with STANDDOWN, ending the mission mid-take. UNVERIFIED WHICH: Copter's guided destination fence check has NOT been read from source (the tree is outside the repo; permission asked).
2. **NO ARRIVAL TIMEOUT IN TAKEOFF, SURVEY OR APPROACH.** The only timeouts in the state machine are cross_timeout_s (20 s, CROSS and RETURN), settle_max_s and rng_timeout_s. _st_takeoff waits for rel_alt, _st_survey waits for _at_wp, _st_approach waits for _at_wp, and none of them can ever give up. Anything that prevents arrival hangs the mission until a battery failsafe: a refused arm, fence avoidance holding the aircraft at the margin, wind, or an unreachable latched target. WORSE AT THE START: MavIO.arm() retries 60 times at 5 s (mavlink_io.py, retry_failed=True) so a refused arm blocks for up to 5 minutes, its return value is never checked by mission.run(), and takeoff() is then commanded to a disarmed aircraft. Since EVERY arm attempt on 2026-08-21 was refused (HDOP, fence breach, proximity), the most likely first field failure is the aircraft sitting still with the dashboard showing TAKEOFF and nothing in the UI explaining why.
3. **THE MEASURED GPS WANDER IS LARGER THAN EVERY GEOMETRIC MARGIN THE DROP SEQUENCE RELIES ON.** Log 50 measured the position wandering ~10 m and 55% of 3D samples above the HDOP 1.4 arming gate, at the same site, 80 minutes after a log-48 spot that read 1%. Against that: route keep-out 4 m, FENCE_MARGIN 2 m, wp_radius 1.5 m, cross offset 3 m, target puddle 2-3 m. Every one of those is smaller than the error already measured on this aircraft. Nothing in mission.py looks at sats, HDOP or fix type after takeoff (Telemetry carries all three since 2026-08-16 and the state machine ignores them), so a fix that degrades mid-survey produces a confidently wrong drop instead of an abort. This is the same root cause as blockers 1 and 2 of the 08-21 field day, seen from the mission's side rather than the arming side.
4. **THE CROSS CAN OPEN THE GATE 1.5 m FROM THE PUDDLE CENTRE.** _st_cross ends on _at_wp(*self.puddle, ...), which accepts anything inside wp_radius_m 1.5 m, on a cross that is only 3 m long. The kinematic harness recorded exactly this: "drops 1.40 m from centre". For a 2 m puddle that is the rim. Cheap half-fix = a tighter arrival radius for CROSS/RETURN only; the part that is not cheap is that GPS-relative positioning is being asked to place a fixed aperture on a target the same size as its own error (see 3).
5. **AVOID_ENABLE 7 DURING A GUIDED MISSION IS UNVERIFIED, AND THE TWO BELIEFS CURRENTLY HELD CANNOT BOTH BE TRUE.** If ArduPilot's simple avoidance applies to GUIDED position/velocity targets, then during the take the ring can push the aircraft off a row or off the puddle (AVOID_MARGIN 1 m, AVOID_BACKUP_SPD 0.75 m/s), and anyone who walks within a metre of the drop site moves the aircraft. If it does NOT apply in GUIDED (ArduPilot's documented path for autonomous modes is the separate OA_TYPE planner, which is not set), then the ring protects nothing for the entire autonomous flight and the WP_SPD 10 -> 3 -> 2 decision, whose stated justification was the ring's braking ceiling, bought only lost survey time on a single pack. NOT READ FROM SOURCE, and it must be before either belief is acted on.
6. **A DETECT WORKER THAT DIES MID-FLIGHT IS A WARNING, NOT AN ABORT.** FileDetector returns None on a stale file and warns once; mission.py treats None as "no puddle here". So the farm's own failure mode, the camera vanishing after preflight passed, now produces a full blind survey flown to completion, a clean landing, zero detections and nothing in the mission state saying why. The preflight camera check only covers the moment before takeoff.
EASILY SOLVED, listed separately as asked: row spacing 5 m against a 16 m swath is roughly 3x overlap (13 rows and 476 m where about 5 rows would cover the same fence), which is survey minutes bought for nothing on the one pack; and SURVEY has no HDOP or fix-quality gate, which is a few lines if it is wanted.
QUESTIONS PUT TO THE USER WITH THIS SCAN: the real submission deadline (this file still says "~one week after 2026-08-15, exact date UNVERIFIED", which is today or already past); permission to read /media/sleuther/Stuff/ardupilot-SITL to settle items 1 and 5 from source; and whether the new field has a water source for a real 2-3 m puddle, since wet dirt is a trained NEGATIVE.

### 2026-08-22 ~10:30 IST: tools/level_cal.py - level-horizon cal over USB, because QGC bulk param load fails on the SiK
- USER: QGC params fail to load (the known degraded-SiK bulk-download failure), asked to calibrate "through the arduino" instead.
- KEY INSIGHT: levelling is NOT a param download, it is ONE command. MAV_CMD_PREFLIGHT_CALIBRATION with param5=2 makes ArduPilot compute the current tilt and fold it into AHRS_TRIM_X/Y. It rides the board's reliable Pixhawk USB link, so the SiK failure is irrelevant. VERIFIED against ArduPilot's GCS handler (param5==2 -> ahrs.add_trim) and MAVProxy's ahrstrim, which sends exactly 0,0,0,0,2,0,0. Not memory: web-checked before writing.
- NEW TOOL tools/level_cal.py (calibration is a genuinely new area, not a second home for params/probes/link). It: refuses if armed or if no heartbeat; reads AHRS_TRIM_X/Y BEFORE; requires the operator to type 'level' after confirming the FRAME (not the table) is level on a bubble; sends the command; prints any STATUSTEXT reason; reads the trim AFTER and shows the delta in degrees. The before/after read is the proof, same principle as parameters.py's echo.
- WHY IT FIXES YESTERDAY'S TILT: logs 47-50 rested at -1 to -2 deg pitch every session; the roll integrator wound that up during a slow ground throttle and dumped it at lift-off. Levelling zeroes the MOUNT component. The rest is procedural (brisk throttle), already told to the user.
- ADD_TRIM SEMANTICS, HONESTLY UNCERTain: it is idempotent when re-run on a level frame IF it folds in the displayed (already-trimmed) angle, which is my reading but not proven from source line-by-line. So the tool says run ONCE, shows the delta, and warns if a trim lands near ArduPilot's 10 deg ceiling (= frame was not level, or FC mounted badly). No reboot needed: AHRS_TRIM is live.
- TESTED AS FAR AS POSSIBLE OFF-BOARD: py_compile clean; import chain resolves (parameters.await_param reused, no side effects on import); show() is None-safe and the radians->deg formatting is correct. The MAVLink round trip itself can only be exercised on the aircraft.

### 2026-08-22 ~10:45 IST: level cal DONE on the floor. Trim behaves additively-idempotent as predicted
- RESULT: before roll -1.26 / pitch +1.73 deg, after roll -1.71 / pitch +0.62 deg. Delta roll -0.44, pitch -1.11. Small, sane, well under the 10 deg ceiling, no warning.
- CONFIRMS THE add_trim SEMANTICS I was unsure of: a trim was ALREADY set (-1.26/+1.73), and the cal ADDED the small residual the aircraft still showed on this floor, rather than resetting. So it folds in the DISPLAYED (already-trimmed) angle and is idempotent on a level frame. The -1.11 pitch residual it removed matches the -1 to -2 deg nose-down rest in logs 47-50: that was the uncorrected mount offset, now folded in.
- LIMIT, kept honest: this removes the MOUNT component only. If the field ground slopes, the aircraft still physically rests tilted there and the integrator can still wind up on that slope, so the procedural fix (brisk continuous throttle to lift-off, no dwell at partial throttle) remains the real guarantee. Floor was not phone-checked, so up to ~1 deg of the pitch correction could be floor slope baked in; still a net improvement over the 1.5-2 deg it replaced, and within noise.
- BENCH PREP FOR TODAY'S WINDOW IS DONE: SERVO9_TRIM=500 pushed and confirmed, gate flows (mustard, ~3 g/s least-starved), level cal done. Remaining bench item (full-hopper flow number) is a tonight job, not a field one.

### 2026-08-22 (afternoon): THE SIX FUNDAMENTAL FINDINGS ARE FIXED IN CODE, and finding 5 is SETTLED FROM SOURCE
All changes are in mission.py unless said otherwise, all are configurable, and all are driven end to end by a state-machine harness (8 cases, scratchpad t_mission.py) plus the arm harness (t_noarm.py).

- **FINDING 2a, THE DASHBOARD LIE (the user's own report: "I clicked arm and start mission and it happily told me the mission was underway"). FIXED.** MavIO.arm() used to swallow its result and retry 60 x 5 s = five minutes, and run() called takeoff() afterwards regardless. arm() now RETURNS the ack result and defaults to 12 retries (about a minute); run() checks it and, on failure, enters the new terminal state **NOARM** carrying the autopilot's own words from tel.prearm_messages(), e.g. "PreArm: High GPS HDOP; PreArm: Vehicle breaching Polygon fence". No takeoff is commanded and no end_mode is commanded to a disarmed aircraft.
- **FINDING 2b, ARRIVAL TIMEOUTS. FIXED.** takeoff_timeout_s 60, survey_leg_timeout_s 120, approach_timeout_s 90. TAKEOFF failing to reach altitude -> ABORTED (a new terminal state that DOES command RTL, since the aircraft may be airborne). A survey leg that times out SKIPS THAT WAYPOINT and flies on, because wind or an avoidance hold should cost one row, not the pack. APPROACH that times out abandons the site and resumes the survey; that is the silent-refusal case, since SET_POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT carries no ack and a refused destination looks exactly like one still being flown to.
- **FINDING 1, FENCE-BLIND DETECTIONS. FIXED.** MissionConfig.fence carries the polygon, loaded by run_mission.py from the same fence.json the operator drew and pushed, and logged either way so a flight cannot quietly lose the check. A detection is ignored unless BOTH the water AND its offset descent point are at least fence_margin_m (2 m) inside the polygon. The geometry is imported from make_waypoints (_inside, _seg_dist) rather than rewritten, so the route generator and the mission agree by construction. An empty fence disables the check instead of inventing a boundary.
- **FINDING 3, FIX QUALITY. FIXED at the latch.** hdop_max 1.5 and min_sats 8 gate every detection: a latch taken during the 10 m wander of log 50 is a confidently wrong drop. Missing HDOP/sat fields count as OK so an autopilot that does not stream GPS_RAW_INT cannot silently disable the survey. The refusal is logged once per distinct reason, not per tick.
- **FINDING 4, THE 1.4 m OFF-CENTRE DROP. FIXED.** cross_radius_m 0.5 applies to the CROSS arrival only; _at_wp gained a radius override. The survey arrival radius is untouched at 1.5 m, where it is correct.
- **FINDING 6, A DEAD DETECTOR. FIXED.** DetectionSource.blind_for_s() is new; FileDetector implements it from the staleness it was already computing and only warning about. The mission ends with ABORTED (and RTL) after detector_blind_s 15 s of blindness, but ONLY in TAKEOFF/SURVEY/APPROACH: once DESCEND starts the target is latched and the rangefinder governs, so going blind there must not abort a committed descent.
- **EASILY SOLVED: row spacing default 5 -> 12 m** in make_waypoints' CLI, the dashboard generate endpoint and the UI field. A 16 m swath at 15 m does not need 3x overlap, and on the user's own fence this is about 5 rows instead of 13.

**FINDING 5 IS SETTLED, AND THE ANSWER IS THE OPPOSITE OF THE FEAR. Simple avoidance does NOT apply to GUIDED position targets.** Read from ArduCopter 4.7 source at /media/sleuther/Stuff/ardupilot-SITL:
- The ONLY AP_AVOIDANCE_ENABLED block in mode_guided.cpp is at line 850-853, inside **velaccel_control_run()**, the velocity/acceleration sub-mode.
- **pos_control_run()** (lines 735-777), which is what a SET_POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT position command runs, calls pos_control->input_pos_NED_m() and the two update_controller()s with NO avoidance call anywhere in it.
- CONSEQUENCES FOR THIS AIRCRAFT, since io.goto() sends position targets and io.velocity_ned() sends velocity ones: the ring does NOT protect the aircraft during SURVEY, APPROACH or CROSS, and DOES limit it during the DESCEND and every stop command, which are velocity-controlled. So the ring cannot push the aircraft off a survey row, but it CAN push it laterally while descending beside the puddle, where AVOID_MARGIN 1 m applies and where anyone standing within a metre would move it.
- **THEREFORE THE WP_SPD 3 -> 2 DECISION HAS LOST ITS STATED REASON.** Its recorded justification was the ring's braking ceiling on survey legs, and the ring is not acting there. The blur justification for 10 -> 3 is untouched. Putting WP_SPD back to 3 is a param decision for the user, not something to change unasked; it is worth real survey minutes on one pack.

### 2026-08-22 ~11:45 IST: RE-ANALYSIS after f3fe661, and the two ArduPilot questions ANSWERED FROM SOURCE (user granted the read)
ALL SIX findings of the 11:00 scan are addressed in code, and the fixes are real (read, not taken on trust): fence-gated detections check BOTH the water and the offset descent point against fence.json with a 2 m margin; TAKEOFF 60 s -> ABORTED+RTL, SURVEY leg 120 s -> skip that waypoint, APPROACH 90 s -> abandon the site and resume; MavIO.arm() returns a bool with a bounded 12 x 5 s budget and a refusal ends the mission as NOARM carrying the autopilot's own prearm text; a fix gate ignores detections above HDOP 1.5 or under 8 sats; CROSS/RETURN arrive on a tight cross_radius_m 0.5 m; FileDetector.blind_for_s() drives an ABORTED after 15 s blind during TAKEOFF/SURVEY/APPROACH only. Scoping and terminal-state choices all check out (NOARM does not command end_mode, ABORTED does).
**SOURCE ANSWER 1, GUIDED DESTINATIONS OUTSIDE THE FENCE ARE REFUSED, AND OUR MARGIN IS THE STRICTER ONE.** ModeGuided::set_pos_NED_m / set_destination call copter.fence.check_destination_within_fence(dest_loc) at mode_guided.cpp:372, 471 and 623 and return false. AC_Fence::check_destination_within_fence (AC_Fence.cpp:984-1021) tests alt-max, alt-min, circle radius and `_poly_loader.breached(loc)` with **NO FENCE_MARGIN applied**, so the autopilot refuses only points genuinely outside the polygon while mission.py now refuses anything within 2 m of it. The refusal writes LogErrorSubsystem::NAVIGATION / DEST_OUTSIDE_FENCE into the .bin, so tools/check_log.py's ERR section would name it post-flight even though nothing reaches the companion live (the source comment says the failure is NAKed to the GCS, which is true for the COMMAND_INT path; SET_POSITION_TARGET_GLOBAL_INT carries no ack, which is exactly why APPROACH needed its timeout).
**SOURCE ANSWER 2, AND IT IS AN INVERSION OF WHAT THIS PROJECT ASSUMED: IN GUIDED, AVOIDANCE APPLIES TO THE VELOCITY PATH ONLY, NOT TO THE POSITION PATH.** copter.avoid.adjust_velocity_NED_m is called ONCE in mode_guided.cpp, at line 852 inside the velocity/accel run, plus get_avoidance_adjusted_climbrate_ms at 979; the position and waypoint submodes call neither, and AC_Avoid appears in AC_Loiter.cpp but in neither AC_WPNav.cpp nor AC_PosControl.cpp. So: SURVEY legs, APPROACH, CROSS and RETURN (all goto(), i.e. position targets) get NO obstacle or fence avoidance at all, while DESCEND, CLIMB, ABORT_CLIMB and the DROP hold (all velocity_ned()) DO. TWO CONSEQUENCES, both live: (a) the WP_SPD 10 -> 3 -> 2 decision was justified by the ring's braking ceiling ("cruise must sit inside its reaction ceiling", 2026-08-18 17:15), and that justification DOES NOT HOLD, because the ring cannot influence cruise in GUIDED; speed is now purely a blur-versus-survey-minutes choice, and on one pack the day before the deadline 3 m/s is worth reconsidering (the user's call, file unchanged); (b) avoidance IS live exactly where precision matters, so anything inside AVOID_MARGIN 1 m during the descent or the drop hold pushes the aircraft at up to AVOID_BACKUP_SPD 0.75 m/s off the point it is trying to hold, and the mission cannot see it happening. Keep people well clear of the descent site, not just of the arming spot.
THREE RESIDUALS FROM THE FIXES THEMSELVES, none of them a reason to change anything tonight without the user's word:
1. **cross_radius_m 0.5 m may be tighter than the fix it flies on.** It correctly stops the gate opening 1.4 m off centre, but 0.5 m is inside ordinary short-term GPS scatter, so the likely failure mode moves from "drops at the rim" to "CROSS times out at 20 s and aborts". Middle ground if wanted: 0.8-1.0 m, still well inside a 2 m puddle.
2. **The mission's fence and the autopilot's fence are two different copies.** mission.py reads fence.json from the data dir; the autopilot enforces the polygon in its own storage, uploaded separately. Redraw and Save without a successful Push and the two disagree silently, in the direction that matters (we would allow a target the autopilot then refuses).
3. **The new fix gate is looser than two rules this project already holds**: hdop_max 1.5 against the board's own GPS_HDOP_GOOD 1.4 (read from log 50's PARM records, so the autopilot will not even arm above 1.4), and min_sats 8 against CRASH LESSONS' standing 10+ sats. As written the mission will latch on a fix the autopilot considers unfit to arm on.
**THE TOP-LINE RISK IS NOW SCHEDULE, NOT DESIGN: the submission is TOMORROW 2026-08-23 (user, primary source), the full autonomous loop has still never flown, and six behaviour changes landed on the flight path today.** Water for a real puddle is being carried to the field (user), which closes the last physical unknown in the demo target.

### 2026-08-22 ~13:20 IST: THE RING NOW COVERS THE SURVEY (params, untested), dose placeholder, docs cut again
- **USER: "the ring has to work at all times, fix this."** Fixed in params, NOT flown, NOT simulated. Two writes and it needs BOTH or it does nothing:
  - `GUID_OPTIONS,64` (bit 6) sends guided POSITION targets through wp_nav instead of straight to the position controller. Source: mode_guided.cpp:670 `use_wpnav_for_position_control()` returns `option_is_enabled(Option::WPNavUsedForPosControl)`, and GUID_OPTIONS defaults to 0, so this was OFF and every survey leg went to `pos_control_run()` (line 735), which has NO avoidance call of any kind.
  - `OA_TYPE,1` (BendyRuler). Copter's wp_nav IS an `AC_WPNav_OA` (system.cpp:467), so once position targets route through it, OA path planning applies. BendyRuler reads the OA database, which `AP_Proximity_Backend.cpp:144` feeds from the ring. **OA_TYPE NEEDS A REBOOT** (`AP_OAPathPlanner::init()` switches on `_type` and only allocates the BendyRuler object if it is non-zero, so writing it live leaves a null planner and no avoidance, silently). Added to NEEDS_REBOOT in tools/parameters.py.
  - `OA_MARGIN_MAX,2` (default 5) and `OA_BR_LOOKAHEAD,5` (default 15). Both defaults assume a scanning lidar; this ring sees ~2 m (ESP32 RANGE_MAX_CM 200). LOOKAHEAD 5 is a judgement call, not a measurement, and is the first thing to revisit if SITL shows the aircraft wandering off the rows.
- TWO MECHANISMS NOW, one per phase, and it should be said out loud rather than implied: simple avoidance (AVOID_ENABLE 7) brakes on the descent's VELOCITY targets, BendyRuler bends the path on the survey's POSITION targets. `wp_control_run()` has no simple-avoidance call either, so they never overlap. REVERT IS ONE WRITE: `OA_TYPE,0` restores exactly the old behaviour.
- WP_SPD LEFT AT 2, deliberately. Its 08-18 justification (cruise inside the ring's braking ceiling) does not apply to position targets, but the replacement mechanism bends rather than brakes and has never run, so the speed question cannot be settled from the old argument. `mode_guided.cpp:255` confirms WP_SPD does set guided position cruise speed via `wp_nav->get_default_speed_NE_ms()`. Asked the user; unanswered.
- **DOSE PLACEHOLDER ADDED to docs/01, at user request, computed not measured.** 1.1 g/m2 mid-label Bti over a 4.8 g/s flow (midpoint of the 4.2-5.3 g/s from the two shortest, least-starved dwells): 1 m2 = 0.23 s, 2 m2 = 0.46 s, 4 m2 = 0.92 s, 6 m2 = 1.38 s. THE DOC STATES ITS OWN TWO DEFECTS: every flow run came from an under-filled hopper, and the material was mustard seed while the gate passes a VOLUME per second, so grams scale with bulk density and **no verified bulk density exists for mustard seed or for VectoBac G's corn-cob granule**. Seconds honest, grams arithmetic. Also flagged: at 1 m2 the dwell is near the servo's own travel time, so gate speed sets the dose there, not dwell.
- DOCS CUT AGAIN (user: "still too long"): 8866 -> 7726 words across the ten files, 13%. Biggest cuts docs/06 1734 -> 1278 (answers capped at three sentences, cross-duplication with 01 and 02 removed) and docs/01 1711 -> 1676 despite gaining the whole dose section. Rhythm unchanged at mean 12, em-dashes still 0.
